- `{{@pagination latest-posts}}` (`PaginationNode`): a `<nav class="wc-pagination">` named in the page's language, with the previous page, the first and last pages and those next to the current one (gaps as …), the current page marked `aria-current="page"`, and the next page; nothing unless the Loop paginates on the page with two pages or more. It goes where a `<nav>` can.

### Archive templates

An Archive template (`templates` row of kind `archive`, one per Post Type; `content/archives.ts`) lists a Post Type's Entries through Loops. API: `templates.getArchiveTemplate`, `saveArchiveTemplate`, `getArchiveTemplateMarkup`, `saveArchiveTemplateMarkup` (`structure.edit`), published with `templates.publish`.

- **What it reads**: the Globals (`site.x`) and the Loops of the Site; no Entry's values. It holds neither `<main>` nor `{{@outlet}}`.
- **Its address**: the URL pattern's fixed parts before `{slug}` (`/blog` for `/blog/{slug}`, `/shop/products` for `/shop/products/{slug}`), in every language under its prefix (`/fr/blog`), answered before Entries and Pages. The Page Post Type has none, nor has a pattern starting with `{slug}` (`ARCHIVE_UNAVAILABLE`, `data.reason`: `pages` or `address`). Its page is titled with the Post Type's name (and `· Page N` past the first), lists `hreflang` alternates to the same page in every language, and its Language switcher links go to its first page in each.
- **One path, one page**: a published Archive template owns its address as a URL pattern owns its paths. Publishing one where another Post Type's pattern owns the address or a published Page is, in any language, is refused with `PATH_TAKEN` (`by` the Post Type or the Page); publishing a Page there, creating a pattern that would own it, or changing a Post Type's pattern so its published Archive moves onto a Page or another pattern's path, likewise (`by: { kind: "archive", postTypeId, name }`). Each takes `lockSitePaths` before any row lock (a Template publish always does).

### Globals

The Globals (`content/globals.ts`, one `globals` row per Site, made the first time it is needed) are one set of Fields per Site, read by every Template as `site.x`.

- `globals.get` (`site.view`): the Fields, the Draft and published values, `status`, `problems` and dates.
- `globals.setFields` (`structure.edit`): the full list of Fields, in order. Like a Post Type's, it applies at once, keeps each Field's type, clears the page cache and logs `globals.update`. Keys can't be `name`, `url`, `language` or `languages`, kept for the Site's own values under `site.`.
- `globals.saveDraft` (`entry.edit`, so Authors too) and `globals.publish` (`entry.publish`), which goes through `publish` with `{ kind: "globals" }`.

### Scripts

The Site script and page scripts (`content/scripts.ts`, table `scripts`) are the JavaScript Staff write in Code mode's JS panel: Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only) with a Draft and a published version, as text, published through `publish` with `{ kind: "script", id }` (logged as `script.publish`). The Site script (`kind: "site"`, one per Site) runs on every page; a page script (`kind: "page"`, one per Entry, gone with it) runs on its Entry's page only, after the Site script.

- **Never inline** (ADR 0023): each version is a file named by the first 16 hexadecimal digits of its text's SHA-256 (`draft_hash`, `published_hash`). `PageToRender.scripts` lists them, the Site script first, and the renderer writes each as `<script src defer>` at the end of `<head>`. Published ones are `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`pageScripts`, served by the Site pipeline beside the theme toggle's, `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`); empty ones aren't loaded.
- **Who runs what**: visitors run the published versions, and so does Edit mode (ADR 0022: the Site's own scripts run there, for Members too); Code mode runs the Drafts, from `/_webcraft/edit/js/<hash>.js`, served only to Staff in Code mode (`no-store`). Safe mode runs none of them (see "Site script safety"). In Edit mode each `<script>` says which it is (`data-wc-script="site"` or `"page"`), so the editing bar can name one that fails.
- **API** (all `structure.edit`): `scripts.get({ siteId, entryId })` (`entryId` null for the Site script; null while there is none), `scripts.saveDraft({ siteId, entryId, code, basedOn? })` (makes it the first time; an `entryId` that isn't the Site's is `ENTRY_NOT_FOUND`) and `scripts.publish({ siteId, scriptId })`. A script's `version` is its Draft's hash.
- A published script a publish replaces is kept, like stylesheets, in `retired_script_files` and still served under its name for 30 days, so a page loaded just before the publish finds it; `site-files.purge` forgets it after that.

### Publishing

`services.publishing.publish(scope, item)` (`content/publishing.ts`) is the one way anything is published. `item` is `{ kind: "entry" | "template" | "component" | "loop" | "script" | "form" | "formEmails", id }`, `{ kind: "globals" }`, `{ kind: "consentBanner" }` (the Consent banner's words) or `{ kind: "designSystem" }`. Open the Site with `publishPermission(item)` (`entry.publish` for Content: Entries, the Globals and the Consent banner's words; `formEmails.edit` for a Form's emails; `structure.edit` for Structure: Templates, Components, Loops, scripts, Forms and the Design system); `publish` checks it again, so the worker's Scheduled publishes (#69) hold it too. In one transaction it:

1. locks the item and checks it can be published: an Entry's (or the Globals') values must keep their rules (`EntryInvalidError`, `GlobalsInvalidError`) and every required value must be there, the title and slug included (`EntryIncompleteError`, `GlobalsIncompleteError`); an Entry's slug must be free among published Entries of its Post Type (`EntrySlugTakenError`; for a Page, among the Pages under its parent); a Page's parent must be published (`PageParentUnpublishedError`) and its new path, and those of the Pages under it, free of URL patterns (`PathTakenError`); a Template must still fit what it renders: its Post Type, the Globals, what its Relationships point at and the published Components it places (`ElementTreeInvalidError`); a Component must still fit what it reads and places, and every published Template and Component placing it (`checkPublishedPlacements`);
2. copies the Draft over the published version and sets `publishedAt` (first time) and `lastPublishedAt` (an Entry's published links too);
3. clears the Site's page cache (`clearPageCache`: raises `sites.page_cache_generation`), and indexes a published Entry for Site search (`indexPublishedEntry`, behind a savepoint, so indexing never fails the publish; see "Site search");
4. writes the Audit log (`entry.publish`, `template.publish`, `component.publish`, `script.publish`, `globals.publish`). `entry.publish` holds the Entry's `path` and `pathChanges`: every published path the publish changed (`{ entryId, language, from, to }`: an Entry's new slug, a Page that moved and every Page under it). The result (`Published`) carries the same `pathChanges`: Redirect rules (#94) follow them so old links keep working.

Once a publish commits, its Audit log entry fires the Site's Webhooks (see "Webhooks"); Revisions (#67), Scheduled publish (#69), Review (#70) and the CDN purge hook in here.

`publish` is one item through `publishItems(scope, items, sources, refusal?)`, which publishes several in the order given, all or none: one transaction, the locks every publish needs taken once and always in the same order, before any row lock (`lockSitePaths` for Entries and Templates, then `lockComponents` for Templates and Components, then the Design system's row for anything changing the stylesheet), so no two publishes wait for each other in a circle; one page cache clear and one replaced stylesheet kept; each item its own Audit log entry. A transaction Postgres gives up on (a deadlock, a serialization failure) answers `CHANGED_MEANWHILE`.

### Publish all

Publish all (ADR 0014, `content/publish-all.ts`, procedures `publishing.*`, the admin's top bar) publishes the Drafts of a Site the person ticks, together.

- **The Drafts** (`siteDrafts`, `publishing.drafts`, `site.view`): every item whose Draft differs from what is published (`draft is distinct from published`): Entries, never-published ones too; the Globals and the Consent banner's words (against an empty version while never published); the Design system (section by section, against the default one while never published); Components, Loops, Forms and Templates; scripts (`draft_hash`, an empty never-published one left out). Content first (Entries, newest change first; the Globals; the Consent banner's words), then Structure (the Design system, Components, Loops, Forms, Templates, scripts). Each says `status` (`new` or `changed`), `changes` (an Entry's, the Globals' or the Consent banner's values that differ, by key with the Field's label, and the Design system's sections), who changed it last (`changedBy`, the Draft tables' `updated_by`, which every Draft save writes; null for a Module or an Import's background work) and when (`updated_at`), `canPublish` (`publishPermission(item)`: Members see Structure, never theirs to tick), and for an Entry its Draft address on the Staging address (`path`). `count` (and `publishing.count`, what the top bar shows) is how many the person may publish. Redirect rules and Site settings have no Draft and are never listed.
- **Publishing** (`publishing.publishAll`, two calls): the first, with the ticked `items`, publishes nothing and answers `preview.items` in the order they would go live and a `confirmation`; the second, with the same items and the id, publishes them through `publishItems` in the order they need each other: the Design system, Loops, Forms, Components (each after those its Draft places), Templates, scripts, the Globals, the Consent banner's words, then Entries (Pages first, each after its Draft parent). All or none: an item that can't be published stops them all with `PUBLISH_ALL_REFUSED` (409; `data`: the `item`, its `name` and its own refusal's `code` and `data`), so a redesign never goes live half done. Refused before anything: an item that is no Draft of the Site (`PUBLISH_ALL_INVALID`, `data.items`) and one the person may not publish (`FORBIDDEN`). Once committed, each item's Audit log entry fires its Webhooks (`publish`, one delivery per item) and Module events, as publishing it alone does, and `site.publishAll` records the items and their count.
- **API clients** call the same procedures at `/api/v1/publishing/drafts` (a read) and `/api/v1/publishing/publishAll` (a write, marked `x-webcraft-confirmation`): Publish all takes two calls for everyone, so a script confirms just as the admin does. `publishing.count` is the admin's own.

### Languages

A Site's languages (ADRs 0003, 0010; `content/languages.ts`, `content/site-languages.ts`, table `site_languages`, procedures `languages.*`) are Structure: everyone who sees the Site reads them (`languages.list`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once, clears the page cache and is logged. They aren't part of Publish all.

- **A language** is a BCP 47 tag kept in its canonical form (`canonicalLanguage`: a language, an optional script and region: `fr`, `pt-BR`, `zh-Hant`, `es-419`; `LANGUAGE_INVALID` otherwise), a name (its own name, `Français`, unless given), a direction (`ltr`/`rtl`, from the code unless given) and an optional domain of its own (in lower case and its ASCII form, unique on the Install among every Site's domains; never the admin host or under the sites domain; `LANGUAGE_TAKEN` with `data.field`). A language's domain is also on the Site's domain list (`site_domains`, role `language`), where it is verified like any other domain; once verified, it serves that language without a prefix (see "Domains"). Every Site has a row for its Default language (`sites.default_language`, `en` for a new Site); `siteLanguages(scope)` answers them, the Default language first.
- **Addresses**: the Default language is unprefixed; every other language's paths start with its code in lower case (`/fr/blog/pain-frais`, `/pt-br/…`; its home page at `/fr`). `languageOfPath` reads a path's language, `languagePath` writes one. A prefix belongs to its language: a top-level Page, an Entry under a `{slug}`-first URL pattern, or a URL pattern whose first part is a prefix is refused with `PATH_TAKEN` (`by: { kind: "language", code, name }`), and adding a language whose prefix the Site's paths already start with is refused naming the Page or Post Type there (`checkPrefixFree`). These take `lockSitePaths` first. A path starting with the Default language's own code goes on (301) to the path without it where nothing else answers, so a new Default language's old addresses keep working.
- **Adding** (`languages.add`) makes every published Entry answer in the new language at once, at its Default language slug (`publishSlugsIn`), and found by Site search there once the index is rebuilt (adding a language and changing the Default language ask for a rebuild); **removing** (`languages.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview.pages`) deletes its published slugs, its 404 page and its Site search documents; values written in it stay in the content store, so adding it again brings them back. The Default language can't be removed.
- **Changing the Default language** (`languages.setDefault`, two steps; the first answers `preview: { from, to, changedPaths, withoutOwnTitle }`, the warning the admin shows) moves every shared value (shared Fields, `parent`, `hiddenLanguages`, in Entries' Drafts and published versions and the Globals) to the new Default language's key, so it keeps its value; Translatable fields now fall back to the new one. Every published address that changes is answered and logged as `pathChanges` (`defaultLanguage.change` logs them and their count, `changedPaths`); Redirect rules follow them (see "Redirects and the 404 log").
- **Slugs** are translatable and never change on their own once published: publishing gives each language the slug of its own, else the one it was published with before, else (the first time) the Default language's (`published_slugs` has a row per Entry and language). An Entry's `path` and `published.slug` are its address in the language asked for. Slugs clash per language (`ENTRY_SLUG_TAKEN` with `data.language`).
- **Publishing** checks every language the Entry (or the Globals) is shown in; a problem of another language's own value carries `language`.
- **Language visibility** (Content, in the Draft): `hiddenLanguages` on `entries.create` / `saveDraft` lists the languages an Entry is hidden in, stored beside `parent` as one shared value. Once published, the Entry answers 404 there, has no `hreflang` alternate there, and is left out where Entries are listed in that language: Relationships on pages now; Loops (#62) and sitemaps (#92) call the same rule, `isVisibleIn(version, languages)` or, in a query, `publishedVisibleIn(languages)` (`content/visibility.ts`).
- **Pages**: `<html lang dir>` are the page's language's; every page of a Site with more than one language lists `<link rel="alternate" hreflang>` for each language it is shown in, at that language's address (absolute, from `SiteHost.addresses`: on the Staging address every language under its prefix there; on the Site's domains each language on its own verified domain, else under its prefix on the Primary domain), then `x-default` at the Default language's; the 404 page lists none.
- **The Language switcher** is the Template command `{{@languageSwitcher}}`: a `<nav class="wc-language-switcher">` named in the page's language, a list of links to the page in each language (that language's home page where the Entry is hidden, and on the 404 page), each named in its own language with `lang`/`hreflang` (and `dir` when it differs), the page's own marked `aria-current`. It renders nothing on a Site with one language, and goes only where a `<nav>` can. A link to a language on another of the Site's domains goes straight there (its address); the others go through `/_wc/lang/<path>` (`LANGUAGE_CHOICE_PATH`): the pipeline keeps the language of the path in the `webcraft.language` cookie (`__Host-` on HTTPS, a year) and sends the visitor on (303, `no-store`), without JavaScript; any other path there is a plain 404.
- **The browser-language redirect** (`languages.setBrowserRedirect`, off by default, `sites.browser_language_redirect`): a request to `/` with no language cookie, from a browser (not a robot by its user agent, not without one), not coming from the Site itself, goes on (302, `no-store`) to the home page in the language its `Accept-Language` prefers among those the home page is shown in, which is then remembered in the cookie. Deep links are never redirected, nor anything on a language's own domain (its `/` is already that language's). A language on its own domain is sent to its address there. While it is on, `/` answers with `Vary: Accept-Language, Cookie`.
- **Fixed text** in Templates (and Components, Forms) has a value per language: a text node keeps its text as written and, optionally, `translations` by language tag; pages in those languages show theirs, every other page the text as written (`textIn`, `packages/renderer/src/languages.ts`). Template markup writes it as `{{ "Read more" fr: "Lire la suite" }}`, in text and in attribute values (`\"` for a quote, `\\` for a backslash, `\r` for a carriage return); text with other languages is never joined with the text beside it.
- **Edit mode** shows a page in its path's language: its Drafts in that language, regions saved in it (`save` takes `language`), and the editing bar lists the page in every language (`state.languages`).
- **AI translation** (#81) builds on this: each stored value keeps who wrote it (`by`), which is how Outdated translations will be told from AI ones.

## The Site pipeline and the page cache

`apps/server/src/site/pipeline.ts` answers every hostname other than the admin host (ADR 0004):

0. **Domain verification** comes first: `/_webcraft/verify/<token>` on any hostname answers the Install's own answer for one of its domains' tokens (`services.domains.verificationAnswer`), else the plain 404, before the hostname step, as a domain waiting for DNS belongs to no Site yet (see "Domains").
1. **Hostname → Site** (`services.sitePages.resolveHost`), compared in lower case without a trailing dot: a Staging address, `<site slug>.<WEBCRAFT_SITES_DOMAIN>`, or one of the Site's **verified** domains (see "Domains"). The answer (`SiteHost`) says how the hostname reaches the Site (`address`: `staging`, `primary`, `redirect` or `language`, with the `language` a language's domain serves) and where the Site's pages are (`addresses`). A **Redirect domain** answers every request with a 301 to the same path and query on the Primary domain (`Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so a later change of Primary domain can't loop in a browser), or the plain 404 while the Site has no verified Primary domain. Anything else gets the unbranded 404. The admin host never resolves to a Site, and no Site slug can make a Staging address equal to it (`reservedSlugs`).
1b. **Redirect rules** (`services.sitePages.redirect`), for `GET` and `HEAD` on every path but the Install's own (`/_…`), where the Module keeping them runs (SDK 1.5, see "Modules" and "Redirects and the 404 log"): a 301 or 302 to where the Site's rules send the path, the request's query parameters added to the target's own, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, never kept as a page. They come before everything else the Site answers (its pages, its files, Edit mode), as the spec orders them; a rule WebCraft added for a changed path steps aside where a page answers. Answers are kept per web process with the Site's page cache generation.
2. **Only `GET` and `HEAD`** for now (405 otherwise), except where a reserved path's feature takes more: a Module's Site routes, Form posts (`POST /_webcraft/forms/<id>`, see "Forms") and Consent records (`POST /_wc/consent`).
3. **Paths the Install keeps** (`isReservedPath`): every path whose first part starts with `_` (`/_webcraft/…`, `/_media/…`, `/_content/v1/…`, `/_wc/m/<module>/…`) and every path naming a file, with a dot in a part (`/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt`, `/llms.txt`, Markdown copies such as `/about.md`). No slug or URL pattern part can start with `_` or hold a dot, so no Page or Entry is ever there. Each feature answers its own (add its step here), before Entry routing and never kept as pages: Media files at `/_media/<hash>-<name>.<ext>` (see "Media"); the Site's stylesheet at `/_wc/css/<hash>.css` and scripts at `/_wc/js/<hash>.js`: WebCraft's own (the theme toggle's and the embed script of a body's videos, see "The Design system" and "The Body field"), the Site's published Site script and page scripts (see "Scripts"), the code of its outside scripts' snippets, and any of them replaced in the last 30 days; its Libraries at `/_wc/lib/<hash>-<file name>` (see "Site script safety"); Edit mode and Code mode at `/_webcraft/edit/…` (see "Edit mode"); Form posts at `/_webcraft/forms/<id>` (see "Forms"); Consent records at `POST /_wc/consent` (see "The Consent banner"); the Language switcher's links at `/_wc/lang/…`, which remember the visitor's language and send them on (see "Languages"); Modules' Site routes at `/_wc/m/<route prefix>/…`, which go to the Module host (`services.modules.answerSiteRoute`, with how the hostname reaches the Site, the client address and whether an edit cookie holds) and are answered where the Module runs, with any method it declares (see "Modules"), Site analytics' collection path among them (`POST /_wc/m/analytics/event`, see "Site analytics"). The rest get the plain 404 until their features come.
4. **Edit mode**: a request carrying an edit cookie that still holds gets the page rendered afresh for Edit mode, never from or into the page cache (see "Edit mode"); an edit cookie that no longer holds is cleared and the request goes on as a visitor's.
5. **The browser-language redirect**, when the Site has it on: a first request to `/` may go on to the home page in the browser's language (see "Languages").
6. **Page cache lookup** (after a Form just sent, `?wc-sent=<n>`, is answered for its visitor alone, never from or into the cache; see "Forms") by the address's origin and normalised URL (`canonicalPath`): decoded path segments, no trailing slash, and of the query parameters only `page`, the page of a paginated Loop (`/blog?page=2`: each page its own entry; Filters will keep theirs, sorted). `page` is a whole number from 2 to `MAX_PAGE_NUMBER` (500) written without leading zeros; `?page=1` answers a 301 to the page itself, and anything else (`0`, `02`, `abc`, the parameter twice) the 404 page. Every other parameter is dropped. The Site's search results page is never looked up or kept: it answers each search afresh (`no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, at most `SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT` searches a minute per client address; see "Site search"), and `?q=…&page=1` goes on to `?q=…`. The same path is another page on each of the Site's addresses (its language, its absolute links), so each address keeps its own.
7. **Render on a miss** (`services.sitePages.render`): in the language the hostname and the path name (`pathOnHost`: on the Staging address, and in Edit mode, the language the path's prefix names, the Default language without one; on a language's own domain, that language, without a prefix, a path starting with any language's prefix going on (301, keeping the query) to where that language's path is; on the Primary domain, as on the Staging address, except that the prefix of a language with a verified domain of its own goes on (301) there), the published Archive template whose address the path is (see "Archive templates"); else the published Entry whose Post Type's URL pattern and published slug match, through its published Entry template (an Entry whose Entry template isn't published has no page yet); else the published Page at that path; at `/`, the home page. Every page renders inside the Site template. The page is kept in the cache: it is rendered with `sitePages.render(host, path, undefined, { forms: { cached: true }, page })` (one options value: what its Forms show, see "Forms", and the page number), so its Forms hold the token mark that every answer, HIT or MISS, fills in. The home page's own path answers a 301 to `/`. A page number on a page where no Loop paginates (an Entry's page) answers a 301 to the page itself, and one past the paginated Loop's last page the 404 page.
8. **Answer** with `Content-Type: text/html`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` (browsers and proxies revalidate every time, since a publish must show at once), a strong `ETag` (SHA-256 of the HTML; `If-None-Match` gets 304), `X-Cache: HIT` or `MISS`, the page's Content Security Policy (kept with the page in the cache, see "Site script safety"), and on a Staging address `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` (never on the Site's domains).

Every answer on a Site's hostname, whatever it is (a page, a 404, a file, a redirect, a Redirect domain's, a domain verification, a refusal, a Module's route, Edit mode's), leaves the pipeline through `withSecurityHeaders`, which adds the fixed security headers it doesn't carry already (see "Site script safety").
9. **A path with no page** gets the Site's **404 page** (`services.sitePages.notFound(host, pathname)`), in the path's language: the Page Staff chose for it or for the Site, once published, else the built-in one (`builtInNotFoundPage`: a heading and one sentence in the page's language), inside the Site template, with status 404 and `Cache-Control: no-store`. It is never kept in the page cache and has no ETag, so every miss reaches the Site and a page published at the path shows at once. A visitor's miss goes to the Site's 404 log (`services.sitePages.noteMiss`, see "Redirects and the 404 log") without the answer waiting for it: only `GET` from a browser (not a robot by its user agent, not a prefetch), never in Edit mode, never a file or the Install's own paths (those get the plain 404), never past a paginated Loop's last page.

**The page cache** (`apps/server/src/site/page-cache.ts`) is in the web process's memory, 64 MiB by default, least recently used pages going first. Every page is kept under the Site's page cache generation, read with the hostname; `publish` raises the generation in Postgres in the publish's own transaction, so the whole Site's cache is cleared at once, in every web process, whichever process published (the worker too). A request that read an older generation never stores its page. A page that shows something time alone changes is kept only until then (`SitePage.expiresAt`, read by the Install's clock): a Condition comparing `now` with a later date until that date, a Loop with a date window relative to now (the last 12 months, upcoming) for `RELATIVE_WINDOW_REFRESH_MS` (5 minutes). The Content API (#108) and a CDN purge can key on the same generation and ETags.

### The Staging address

A Site's Staging address (ADR 0014, `<site slug>.<WEBCRAFT_SITES_DOMAIN>`) shows the Site as it would be with every Draft published, to the people reviewing it and nobody else. Core: `packages/core/src/staging/` (`services.staging`: links, sessions, the staging password and the page shown to strangers); the web side: `apps/server/src/site/staging.ts`, before step 1b above.

- **Who may open it**: someone signed in to the admin who sees the Site (`site.view`: its Members reaching it, Staff, the Superadmin), through a hand-off like Edit mode's: "Staging" in the admin's top bar (and View in Publish all, and Site › Domains & Staging) calls `staging.open({ siteId, path })`, which answers `…/_webcraft/staging/enter?token=…`, working once, within 60 seconds, for that person, Site and admin session (`staging_links`); it is the admin's own, never served to API tokens. Following it (rate-limited like sign-in) starts a Staging session (`staging_sessions`, at most 8 hours, gone with the admin session) and sets `__Host-webcraft.staging` (`webcraft.staging` on `.localhost`; `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`), then answers 303 to the page. Every request checks the session again: not run out, its admin session still signed in, the person still seeing the Site. An edit cookie that holds lets the browser in too (Edit mode lives on the Staging address while the Site has no domain), and `/_webcraft/edit/…` keeps its own checks.
- **The staging password** (optional, `staging.get`, `staging.setPassword`: `staging.manage`, Owners and Staff; a Sensitive action, so never through an API token; logged `stagingPassword.change` with `set`): 8 to 200 characters, kept as the same scrypt hash people's passwords get (`sites.staging_password_hash`). Given on the Staging address's page (`POST /_webcraft/staging/password`, from the Staging address's own page only, at most `STAGING_PASSWORD_RATE_LIMIT`, 10 a minute per client address and Site, then 429), it sets `__Host-webcraft.staging-password` for a week (`STAGING_PASSWORD_ACCESS_MS`): an expiry signed with HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from the Master key (`stagingPasswordKey`) over the Site and the password's hash, so setting it again, even to the same password, or removing it shuts out every browser it let in.
- **Everyone else**, for every path (pages, files, Modules' routes, Form posts): a page asking them to sign in (`stagingPage`: in the path's language, the seven pages have words in; a link to the admin's `/staging/<site id>?path=…`, which signs them in and opens the address at that page; and, with a password, its form), with status 403, `private, no-store`, `noindex` and its own policy (`STAGING_PAGE_POLICY`: its own stylesheet at `/_wc/staging/page-<hash>.css`, no script). Anything other than `GET` or `HEAD` gets a plain 403. Cookies that no longer open anything are cleared.
- **What it shows** (`showsDrafts(host)`, read by `site-pages/index.ts`'s `viewFor`): the same renderer as the Site's domains, through a `View` with every Draft: the Entries' and the Globals' Drafts, the Templates' and Components' Drafts, the Design system's Draft (its stylesheet served as `/_wc/css/<hash>.css` on the Staging address only, built by `draftStyles`), the Site and page scripts' Drafts (served as `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` there only), the Loops' Drafts over every Entry's Draft, Forms' Drafts and the Consent banner's Draft words. It reads through `services.data.staging(site)`.
- **Draft addresses** (`content/draft-paths.ts`): a path finds the Entry whose Draft would be published there (`publishedSlug`'s rule, in SQL: its own slug in the language, else the one it was published with there, else the Default language's), a Page by its Draft parents, an Archive template by its Draft, never-published ones too; Loops, Relationships and the page's languages link Entries at their Draft addresses. Edit mode routes the published page first, then such an Entry (`publishedThenDraft`), so a Page never published can be edited in place, and its page switcher lists it.
- **Never indexed, never kept**: every answer says `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`; pages also `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` and `Cache-Control: private, no-store` (no ETag, never in the page cache, so a CDN in front never keeps one either); their canonical URL points at the Primary domain, and while the Site has none they carry none, so the Staging address is never a page's canonical address; the 404 log hears nothing from it. Sitemaps, `robots.txt` and `llms.txt` (#92) read the same rule, `showsDrafts(host)`, to leave the Staging address out.
- **Seams**: Form posts on the Staging address are still checked against the published Form (a Form never published shows there, but its post answers 404); Forms' messages join Content Drafts with #89.

### Element trees and the renderer

Templates are stored as element trees (`packages/renderer/src/tree.ts`, ADR 0002), the one canonical format the builder, Template markup and the renderer share:

```ts
{ version: 1, nodes: [
  { type: "element", id: "Xb3…", tag: "article", attributes: [], classes: ["<Class id>"], children: [
    { type: "element", id: "Q9z…", tag: "h1", attributes: [], classes: [], children: [{ type: "value", path: "title" }] },
    { type: "value", path: "publishedAt", format: { type: "date", style: "long" } },
    { type: "comment", text: " kept for Staff, never sent to visitors " },
    { type: "condition", branches: [{ test: { type: "value", path: "summary" }, children: [/* … */] }], otherwise: null },
    { type: "element", id: "k2P…", tag: "a", classes: [],
      attributes: [{ name: "href", value: [{ type: "text", text: "/blog/" }, { type: "value", path: "slug" }] }],
      children: [{ type: "text", text: "Read more" }] },
  ] },
] }
```

- Elements have a stable `id` (filled in when missing, kept when given), a lower-case `tag` from `TEMPLATE_ELEMENTS`, ordered `attributes` (a list: Postgres `jsonb` doesn't keep key order, and a round trip must lose nothing), `classes` (the ids of the Site's Classes, ADR 0009), `children` and an optional layer `label` (what the visual builder's Layers panel calls it, one line of at most 100 characters, never rendered; `labelProblem`). Text nodes are fixed text, as written and optionally in other languages (`translations`, see "Languages"); value nodes and attribute value parts are **bindings**: a `path` (`title`, `address.city`, `category.url`, `site.phone`; later `props.x`, `item.x`) and an optional `format`, which Edit mode (#72) reads to tag editable regions. Comments are kept and never rendered. Template commands' nodes (a Condition so far) come from the command registry (see "Template markup").
- `parseElementTree(input, options)` checks a tree and answers its canonical form (text next to text joined, empty text dropped, line breaks in comments as `\n`), or throws `ElementTreeInvalidError` with every problem and where (`nodes[0].children[2].attributes[1]`): scripts, elements outside `TEMPLATE_ELEMENTS`, `on…`, `style`, `class`, `srcdoc` and `data-wc-…` attributes, unknown value names (`isKnownValue`), Class ids the Site doesn't have (`classIds`), nesting a browser would change (a block inside `<p>`, a link inside a link, table parts out of place, `<li>` inside `<li>`), duplicate ids, children of void elements, NUL and broken characters, more than 64 levels or 10,000 nodes. The API answers it as `TEMPLATE_INVALID` with `data.problems`. The element and attribute rules are functions (`elementProblem`, `attributeProblem`, `nestingProblem`) the markup parser calls too, so both refuse the same things with the same words.
- New node types join the `TreeNode` union; a change old trees can't be read as raises `ELEMENT_TREE_VERSION`.
- `renderPage` (`packages/renderer/src/page.ts`) writes a whole document: `<html lang dir>` (the direction from the language), the title (`PageToRender.seo`'s, with the page's SEO tags and structured data, see "SEO"; without it `<title> · <Site name>`), a skip link as the first thing in `<body>`, then the Site template (the built-in one when the Site has none published) with the page's content (an Entry template, a Page's layout, the 404 page's content) rendered at its `{{@outlet}}`. The Site template's one `<main>` gets the id the skip link points at and `tabindex="-1"`, so following the link moves focus there; WebCraft adds them through `extraAttributes`. With `editMode` (`EditModePage`), `renderPage` writes the page for Edit mode instead: `noindex`, WebCraft's own files first in `<head>`, and editable regions tagged (`RenderOptions.editRegion`; see "Edit mode"). Landmarks (`<header>`, `<nav>`, `<footer>`) are the Site template's. The words WebCraft writes into pages (the skip link, the built-in 404 page) come from `pageWords(language)`: English, Italian, French, German, Spanish, Portuguese and Dutch, by the language's primary subtag, English for any other. **Accessibility hooks** the Design system (#64) styles from its base layer are named in `PAGE_HOOKS`: the skip link's class `wc-skip-link` (out of sight until focused), the main id `wc-main`, and the custom properties the focus style reads (`--wc-focus-color`, `--wc-focus-width`, `--wc-focus-offset`), set from Design tokens; it can be restyled but not removed (see "The Design system"). A body's hooks are there too: `wc-callout` (with the tones' custom properties `--wc-callout-info`, `--wc-callout-success`, `--wc-callout-warning`, `--wc-callout-danger`) and `wc-embed`. Values are typed (`TemplateValue`: text, numbers, true/false, dates, rich text, a body's Blocks, a table of contents, or a list of them); the Site pipeline hands dates over as dates and rich text marked with `richText(html)` (see "What a binding reads"). A list renders as its values joined with commas, each through the value's formatter. Text and values are escaped for text or double-quoted attributes; `href`, `src` and other URL attributes (each `srcset` candidate, each `ping` URL) keep only `http`, `https`, `mailto`, `tel`, relative paths and `#anchors`, anything else becoming `#`. A date without a formatter renders as `2026-03-02`. Rich text renders as its cleaned HTML in text and as its plain text in attributes and formatters. Comments are left out. A placed Form (`{{@form <key>}}`, a `FormNode`) renders through `RenderOptions.form` (see "Forms"); a Form's CAPTCHA joins the page's outside scripts through `PageToRender.formScripts`, asked once the page has rendered. No inline script, inline style or event handler is ever written: the `<head>` links the Site's stylesheet, and a page showing a theme toggle (or a body's video) links its script file, as a page asking for consent links the consent script (see "The Consent banner").

## Site search

Site search (ADR 0021, `GLOSSARY.md`; `packages/core/src/search/`, behind `services.search` for the worker) is a built-in Filter with only a search box, on a query Loop over the searchable Post Types, on the Site's search results page. Filters (#97) build on the same pieces: the index, `searchQueryIn` (the `q` parameter and its checks), `Narrowing` on a Loop's query, `pageHref`'s state and `searchSnippets`.

- **The index** (table `search_documents`, a Site table): one row per published Entry and language it is shown in, `(entry_id, language)`, holding a `tsvector` (`document`, GIN-indexed) and the text besides the title (`text`, for snippets). Queries always name the Site and the language, so each Site and language has an index of its own. Every Post Type is indexed; `searchable` only decides what Site search lists.
- **Searched fields** (`search/searched-fields.ts`, also read by the admin as `@webcraft/core/search-fields`): each Post Type's `searchedFields`, `[{ field, weight }]`, `field` being `title` or a Field's path (`summary`, `origin.city` in a Group, `steps.text` across a Repeater's rows) of a type search reads as text (`SEARCHED_TYPES`: text, rich text, Body, select (its labels), link (its text), email), `weight` `A` (counts most) to `D`; at most `SEARCHED_FIELDS_MAX` (50). A new Post Type searches its title (A) and its Body field (B); Pages their title. A Field removed from the Post Type leaves its Searched fields. Refusals are `POST_TYPE_INVALID` at `searchedFields[1].field` (`searchedFieldUnknown`, `searchedFieldType`, `searchedFieldTwice`, `searchedFieldsCount`). The admin sets them on the Post Type's **Site search** tab.
- **Languages** (`search/text-search.ts`): documents and queries use Postgres's text search configuration for the language's primary subtag where Postgres has one (`textSearchConfiguration`: `en` English, `fr` French, `pt-BR` Portuguese, `nb`/`nn`/`no` Norwegian, 30 in all), `simple` (words as written, lower-cased, nothing stemmed) for every other language. Values are read in the language with the Default language's as fallback, one Field at a time; a body gives its Blocks' text (`bodyText`), rich text its text. Each weight's text is cut at 100,000 characters, and control characters become spaces.
- **Kept up to date**: publishing an Entry rewrites its documents in the publish's transaction (`indexPublishedEntry`, inside a savepoint: if indexing fails it is undone and the Site's index is marked stale, never failing the publish); deleting an Entry deletes them (foreign key). An Entry hidden in a language has none there, and searches filter with `publishedVisibleIn` too.
- **Rebuilds**: a change to what documents are built from (a Post Type's Searched fields, their Fields' definitions, adding a language, changing the Default language) marks the Site's index stale (`markSearchIndexStale`: `sites.search_index_version = 0`); `search.rebuild-due` (every minute) claims Sites behind `SEARCH_INDEX_VERSION` and queues one `search.rebuild` each, which reindexes every Entry 100 at a time with their rows locked (so a publish never races it) and drops documents of removed languages. Raise `SEARCH_INDEX_VERSION` whenever what documents hold changes (a Field type's text, the configuration list): every Site's index is then rebuilt. Until a rebuild runs, search answers from the index as it was.
- **The query** (`searchQueryIn`): the first `q`, control characters as spaces, spaces collapsed, cut at `SEARCH_QUERY_MAX_LENGTH` (200) characters (a longer one is cut, not refused); empty searches for nothing. It reaches SQL only as a parameter of `websearch_to_tsquery` (words, `"a phrase"`, `or`, `-word`), which never fails on its input; it is shown back escaped.
- **The search Loop** (`site-pages/loops.ts`): on the search results page, the Loop named `search` (`SEARCH_LOOP`, a name Staff Loops can't take) runs `searchLoopQuery`: the searchable Post Types whose Entries have pages (the Page Post Type, and Post Types with a published Entry template), `SEARCH_PAGE_SIZE` (10) a page, paginated, the search results page itself left out, narrowed by the query (`queryRows` joins `search_documents` in the page's language and orders by `ts_rank`, then the newest). Each hit reads as a query Loop's item, plus `snippet`: `ts_headline` around the best match (about 15 to 35 words), all of it escaped, the matched words in WebCraft's own `<mark>` (`searchSnippets`; text Postgres's parser reads as a tag is left out of headlines). Its pages keep the query (`?q=bread&page=2`, `LoopPlace.state`).
- **The search results page**: the Page Staff choose with `pages.setSearchPage` (`structure.edit`, logged `searchPage.change`), at its own path in each language it is shown in (translated slugs, language domains), titled `Results for “bread”` with a query. Its content is the **Search results template** (`templates.getSearchTemplate`, `saveSearchTemplate`, `getSearchTemplateMarkup`, `saveSearchTemplateMarkup`, published with `templates.publish`), or the built-in one until Staff publish theirs (`builtInSearchTemplate`: a heading, the Search element, the hit count, the hits with their snippets and pagination, or a "no results" message saying what to try, in the page's language). It reads the Globals, the Loops of the Site, the Loop `search` (`hit.title`, `hit.url`, `hit.snippet`, the searchable Post Types' Fields), `search.query` and `search.total`; no Entry's values; neither `<main>` nor `{{@outlet}}`. Answers: never cached (`no-store`, no ETag, never in the page cache), `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, a page past the last answers 404. Edit mode shows it with its hits too.
- **The Search element** (`{{@search}}`, `SearchNode`): `<form class="wc-search" method="get" action="<search results page>">` with a visually hidden label, a `type="search"` box named `q` (`maxlength="200"`, the query on the results page) and a button, in the page's language; the first on a page carries `role="search"`, so a page has one search landmark. No script: it works without JavaScript. Styled from the Design system's base layer (`SEARCH_HOOKS`).
- **Rate limits**: a search (a non-empty `q` on the search results page) counts against `SEARCH_RATE_LIMIT`, 30 a minute per client address and Site (`rateLimits.count`, key `search:<site id>:<address>`); past it the answer is a plain 429 with `Retry-After: 60`. Every other page, and the search results page without a query, never counts.
- **Seams**: Filters (#97) attach a search box to any query Loop: the same `q`, `searchQueryIn`, `Narrowing.search` and snippets, with their own controls narrowing beside it; live updates swap the server-rendered hits. Noindex Entries stay out of Site search (`publishedIndexable`, see "SEO"). The Content API (#108) searches through `queryRows` with `Narrowing`. Loop items' `url` carries the language prefix on a language's own domain (it redirects there): a general Loop gap, not search's.

## SEO

Every page carries good metadata out of the box (the SEO decision; `GLOSSARY.md`: SEO fields, Social card, Schema type). Core: `packages/core/src/seo/` (`services.seo` for the Site's settings); the renderer writes the tags (`packages/renderer/src/seo.ts`); the admin: the Entry editor's SEO & sharing card, a Post Type's SEO tab and Site › SEO (`apps/server/src/ui/seo/`).

- **SEO fields** (`seo/entry-seo.ts`): every Entry, Pages included, has an SEO title (the page's whole title), description, canonical URL, social image and noindex. They are part of its Draft and publish with it, stored as Fields are (ADR 0003) as a Group under the key `seo` in each version (`SEO_GROUP`): the first four per language, falling back to the Default language one at a time; noindex one value for every language, under the Default language (a Default language change moves it, `site-languages.ts`). No Field may take the key `seo` (`RESERVED_FIELD_KEYS`). `entries.create` and `saveDraft` take `seo: { title, description, canonical, image, noindex }` (only those given change; null or "" clears one), Authors included (Content, `entry.edit`); the Entry answers them as `draft.seo` and `published.seo`. Refused at their path (`ENTRY_INVALID`, `seo.canonical`): a canonical URL that isn't an http(s) address or a Site path (`isCanonical`), a social image that isn't a JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF or GIF of the Site's library, or is in the Trash (`isShareableImage`: never an SVG, which other sites can't show). They have no rules: what search engines prefer is the SEO check's, never a reason not to publish.
- **A Post Type's SEO settings** (`seo/seo-rules.ts`, which the admin reads as `@webcraft/core/seo-rules`; column `post_types.seo`, `{}` until Staff set them, when `defaultPostTypeSeo` applies): `titlePattern` (tokens `{title}`, `{site}`, `{postType}`, `{separator}`; `{title} {separator} {site}` by default; `applyTitlePattern` drops a token without a value with the separator next to it, never touching the title's own text), `descriptionField` (a text, rich text or Body field's path; null for the start of the Body field), `imageField` (a media Field, or a Body field's first image; the first media Field, else the Body field, by default) and `schema` (`type` from `SCHEMA_TYPES` and `properties`, each schema.org property mapped to a source: `title`, `author`, `publishedAt`, `lastPublishedAt`, `createdAt`, `seo.description`, `seo.image` or a Field's path, a Repeater's Field across its rows for lists). `postTypes.create`/`update` take `seo` (each part given replaces that part); a Schema type given without `properties` takes `guessSchemaProperties`: built-in sources first, then Fields of the right kind (`PropertyKind`) whose key or label names the property (`words`), and for an FAQ page the first Repeater with two text Fields as questions and answers. Refusals are `POST_TYPE_INVALID` at `seo.titlePattern`, `seo.descriptionField`, `seo.imageField`, `seo.schema.type`, `seo.schema.properties.<name>`, with `SETTING_RULES` codes (`seoTitleToken`, `seoTitlePatternLength`, `seoDescriptionField`, `seoImageField`, `schemaTypeUnknown`, `schemaPropertyUnknown`, `schemaSource`, `schemaFaqRepeater`). `keptPostTypeSeo` leaves out what a Field change made impossible. Pages, the home page, the 404 page and search results take the Page Post Type's title pattern; Archive pages their Post Type's, with its name as `{title}`.
- **A Site's SEO settings** (`seo/site-settings.ts`, table `seo_settings`, a Site setting: `seoSettings.get` with `site.view`, `seoSettings.update` with `structure.edit`, applied at once, clearing the page cache, logged `seoSettings.change` with what `changed`): `siteName` (null for the Site's own name), `separator` (1 to 3 characters, `·` by default), `defaultImage` (a Media image) and `organization` (`kind` `organization` or `localBusiness`, `name`, `logo`, `sameAs` https addresses, and the Globals its `telephone`, `email` and `address` come from, by path). Refusals are `SEO_SETTINGS_INVALID` with `data.problems` (`seoSeparator`, `seoImage`, `seoSameAs`, `seoGlobal`, …).
- **Fallbacks** (`seo/page-seo.ts`, worked out with the published values a page renders, in its language): the title is the SEO title, else the pattern filled in (`Recipes · Page 2` past a paginated Loop's first page); the description the SEO description, else the description Field or the start of the Body field as plain text (`textOf`), cut at a word to about 155 characters (`cutText`, `DESCRIPTION_CUT`); the social image the Entry's own, else the image Field's, then the Social card (#93, the seam is marked in `entryPageSeo` and `seoReports`), then the Site's default image (`shareableImages`: ready, not in the Trash, not an SVG; the narrowest WebP variant at least 1,200 pixels wide, else the original, which is then narrower). The server never crops (ADR 0027), so the Focal point plays no part in shared images; the Social card will compose with it.
- **The head** (`PageToRender.seo`, `PageSeo`; `seoTags` and `sharingTags` in the renderer, every value escaped for where it lands): `<title>`, `<meta name="description">`, `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` for a noindex Entry, the Site's 404 page (at its own path too, where it answers 200), a page of search results and a Form sent back with errors; `<link rel="canonical">` (none on a 404 answer): the Entry's canonical URL (an address as written, a Site path at its language's address, `canonicalOf`), else the page's own address where the Site's pages are for visitors (`SiteHost.visitorAddresses`, from `siteAddresses`: the Primary domain and languages' domains, never the Staging address, whose pages say `noindex` and carry no canonical URL of their own) with `?page=N` past the first page; the `hreflang` alternates (#80) agree with it on the Site's domains. Then Open Graph (`og:type` `article` for an Article or Blog post, else `website`; title, description, URL, Site name, `og:locale` and `og:locale:alternate` as `pt_BR`, the image with its absolute address, type, size and alt text in the page's language; `article:published_time` and `article:modified_time`) and the social tags (`twitter:card` `summary_large_image` with an image, else `summary`, its title, description, image and alt). Edit mode pages carry the title, description and canonical URL only.
- **Structured data** (`seo/structured-data.ts`): one JSON-LD graph in `<head>`, a `<script type="application/ld+json">` data block, which a strict policy doesn't block since it is never run (ADR 0023). `jsonLdText` writes `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 as `\u` escapes, so no value can end the block or read as markup, and every value is plain text (rich text and bodies without markup, ISO dates, numbers, absolute addresses). Every page has the `WebSite` (`<main address>/#website`, its name, home page in the page's language, and a `SearchAction` when the Site has a search results page) and the organisation (`/#organization`, an `Organization` or `LocalBusiness` from the Site's settings and Globals). An Entry's page adds its `BreadcrumbList` (the home page by its title, else the Site's name; then the Post Type's Archive page when it has one published, or a Page's parent Pages; then the page) and the page itself: a `WebPage` (or `FAQPage`, whose `mainEntity` lists a Repeater's questions and answers) with the mapped properties, or a `WebPage` and a node of the Schema type (`<address>#<type>`, `mainEntityOfPage`, Articles naming the organisation as publisher) holding them; an author is a `Person`, linked to the Entry's page when the author is a Relationship. An Archive page adds its breadcrumbs and a `CollectionPage`; the 404 page, search results and Form error pages only the `WebSite` and the organisation.
- **Site search** leaves noindex Entries out of its hits (`publishedIndexable`, through `Narrowing.indexableOnly` on its built-in Loop); sitemaps and `llms.txt` (#92) take the same rule.
- **The SEO check** (`seo/checks.ts`, a Check: warnings only): an Entry answers `checks` (after its body's) with `seoTitleLong` (over 60 characters), `seoDescriptionMissing`, `seoDescriptionShort` (under 50), `seoDescriptionLong` (over 160), `seoImageMissing` and `seoImageAlt` (the social image without alt text in the language), each at `seo.title`, `seo.description` or `seo.image` with `params` (`length`, `max`, `min`); the codes and limits are `SEO_CHECKS` and `SEO_CHECK_LIMITS` in `seo-rules.ts`, where the Site's list of open Checks (#95) can collect them. It measures what the Draft gives through the same fallbacks pages use; `seoPreview` (`{ title, description, image: { mediaId, from } }`) is that for the SEO & sharing card.
- **Media**: an Entry's social image is in its versions, so Used in names the Entry (`fields: ["seo"]`); the Site's default image and logo are the `seoSettings` `MediaUseSource` (`settings: ["defaultImage", "organizationLogo"]`), shown before a file goes to the Trash. A file in the Trash stops being shared at once.
- **Seams**: the Social card (#93) joins the image fallbacks between the image Field and the Site's default image (and `SeoPreview.image.from`); the Page details drawer in Edit mode (#74) edits the same `seo` values through `saveDraft`; sitemaps and `llms.txt` (#92) use `publishedIndexable`, `canonicalOf` and `SiteHost.visitorAddresses`; the Content API (#108) can answer `seo`; Filters (#97) make filtered pages noindex with a canonical URL to the unfiltered page by changing their `PageSeo`.

## Domains

A Site's domains (ADR 0015; `packages/core/src/domains/`, behind `services.domains`; table `site_domains`, procedures `domains.*`; the admin's Site › Domains & Staging) are Site settings: everyone who sees the Site sees them (`domains.list`, `site.view`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once and is logged (`domain.add`, `domain.makePrimary`, `domain.remove`), and none is part of Publish all.

- **Names** (`domains/names.ts`): `domainName` keeps a domain in lower case, without the trailing dot, international names in their ASCII form (`Bäckerei.example` is `xn--bckerei-…`; `displayName` shows them as written); it refuses an address with a scheme, a path or a port, an IP address, a wildcard and a single name (`DOMAIN_INVALID`, `reason: "format"`), and `isInstallName` the Install's own names: the admin host, the sites domain and every name under it (`reason: "install"`). A name is unique on the Install, whichever Site or Workspace has it, languages' domains included (`DOMAIN_TAKEN`, `data.name`).
- **Roles**: one **Primary domain** per Site, which serves it; any number of **Redirect domains**, which answer 301 to the same path and query on the Primary domain; and the **languages' domains** (`role: "language"`, `language` set), which serve that language without a prefix. A language's domain is set in Languages (`languages.add`/`update` write both `site_languages.domain` and its `site_domains` row; removing the language removes the row); here it is only listed, verified and checked (`DOMAIN_CHANGE_REFUSED`, `problem: "language"`, for the rest).
- **Adding** (`domains.add`): the first domain of a Site without a Primary domain becomes it, the rest are Redirect domains. With `withPartner`, the apex or `www` partner comes too (`partnerOf`: `www.bakery.ie` for `bakery.ie` and back), as a Redirect domain; one the Site has already is left as it is, one another Site has refuses the whole addition.
- **The DNS records** each domain lists (`records`): A and AAAA records to `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES` (`address`, none without it), or for a subdomain a CNAME record to the admin host (`alias`). The screen shows them for the Install's `edge` first (`domains.list` answers it), with the steps for Coolify and Railway, where the domain is also added in the platform's panel.
- **Verification**: a domain serves nothing until the Install has seen it reach it. `checkReaches` (`domains/checks.ts`) resolves the domain, fetches `http://<domain>/_webcraft/verify/<token>` (on the web process's port for a development name under `.localhost`) and accepts only the answer this Install makes for that token. Token and answer are HMACs under a key derived from the Master key (`domainVerificationKey`): the token from the domain row's id and name (a domain added again gets a new one), the answer from the name and token, so neither is stored and no other Install, nor anything echoing the request, can make the answer. The Site pipeline answers the path before its hostname step (step 0 above), only for a domain's own token on that domain, `no-store`. A domain that doesn't resolve, or answers wrongly while pointing elsewhere than the Install's addresses, has the problem `dns`; one pointing at them that doesn't answer, `unreachable`; one answering something else, `response`.
- **What the checks call** (the SSRF line): every request goes through the guarded fetcher, `GET` only, no redirect followed (a redirect is a wrong answer) except the one to the same address over HTTPS (see "Behind a platform's proxy" below), 1 KB, 10 seconds. Besides public addresses it may call **only the Install's own addresses** (`installAddresses`: `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES` and what the admin host resolves to), since a domain must point at the Install, which may be on a private network or, in development, on this machine. A domain resolving to any other internal address is never called (`dns`), and nothing it answers reaches anyone: only whether it was the Install's own answer. Staff can't choose the path, the method or the port.
- **When**: `domains.verify-due` runs every minute (`coreSchedules`), claims each domain waiting for DNS whose `next_check_at` came (moving it on first, so no check runs twice) and queues one `domains.verify` job each: every 5 minutes for the first hour after the domain was added, hourly until 7 days, then only when Staff press **Check now** (`domains.check`), which checks at once and starts the round again. A verified domain clears the Site's page cache (its addresses changed) and has its certificate looked at, which also starts an on-demand one.
- **The Domain check**: `domains.check-all` runs hourly and queues one `domains.check` job per verified domain: it checks the domain as verification does, then its certificate over TLS (`checkCertificate`, through the `tls` port: `systemTls` in the processes, faked in tests; skipped for `.localhost` names): one that is missing or not trusted is `certificate-missing`, one running out within 14 days `certificate-expiring`. A failing domain is **Unreachable** (`unreachable_since`; it keeps serving, and Caddy keeps renewing its certificate) and active again once a check passes. The check that turns it Unreachable, and only that one, sends the `domain-unreachable` System email to the **Site's Owners**: the Members reaching the Site whose role manages the Workspace's Members (`member.manage`, which only Owners hold in v1), linking to `/sites/<id>/domains`. Check now on a verified domain runs the Domain check at once.
- **Making a domain the Primary domain** (`domains.makePrimary`): only a verified Redirect domain (`DOMAIN_CHANGE_REFUSED`, `problem: "not-verified"`); the Primary domain before it becomes a Redirect domain, so its links keep working. Clears the page cache.
- **Removing** (`domains.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview: { name, role, live }`): the domain stops at once (no page, no certificate); to move it to another Site, remove it, add it there and verify it again. Without its Primary domain a Site is on its Staging address only, and its Redirect domains answer the plain 404 until another domain is made Primary.
- **Caddy's ask** (`GET /caddy/ask?domain=<name>` on the admin host, `apps/server/src/admin/caddy-ask.ts`; point Caddy's `on_demand_tls { ask … }` there): 200 for the admin host, the Staging address of an existing Site and every verified domain (Primary, Redirect and language, Unreachable ones too), 404 for any other name, 400 for what isn't a hostname, 405 for any method but `GET`. It reads at most one row and writes nothing: no rate limit, no log line. The ask comes from inside the deployment: `deploy/compose/` sends it to `http://<admin host>:3000/caddy/ask`, the admin host being a network alias of the web service on a network only Caddy and the web process share, and its Caddyfile answers `/_webcraft/verify/…` over plain HTTP without redirecting to HTTPS, since a domain has no certificate until it is verified.
- **Behind a platform's proxy** (Coolify, Railway), plain HTTP is answered with a redirect to HTTPS. The verification request follows exactly that one redirect, to the same verify address over HTTPS on the same host (through the guarded fetcher again, the certificate checked against the name); any other redirect is still a wrong answer.
- **A CDN in front of a Site** (spec story 43; `packages/core/src/cdns/`, behind `services.cdns`; column `sites.cdn`, table `cdn_ranges`): a Site setting on the Domains & Staging screen, `domains.setCdn` (`structure.edit`, logged `cdn.change` with `from` and `to`), read by `domains.list` as `cdn`. The CDNs WebCraft knows are in `CDNS` (`cdns/registry.ts`, read by the admin as `@webcraft/core/cdns`): their name, the header naming the visitor (Cloudflare `CF-Connecting-IP`, Fastly `Fastly-Client-IP`) and where they publish their address ranges. With one set, the Site pipeline takes the visitor's address from that header, but only when the client address (after the trusted proxies) is in the CDN's ranges and the header holds an address; otherwise the client address stands. The ranges: `services.cdns.ranges(cdn)`, the list the worker last fetched, else `BUNDLED_CDN_RANGES` (made by `bun run refresh:cdn-ranges`), kept in the web process's memory for `CDN_RANGES_CACHE_MS` (5 minutes). `cdn.refresh-ranges` (daily at 03:17 UTC, and queued when a Site chooses a CDN) fetches the list of each CDN a Site uses through the guarded fetcher (its own host only, no redirect, 10 s, 256 KiB) and keeps it only when `checkCdnList` passes: every entry a range, none wider than `/8` (IPv4) or `/16` (IPv6), 1 to 1,000 of them; otherwise the ranges it had stand and a warning is logged. Adding a CDN is one entry in `CDNS` and a run of the refresh script.
- **Addresses** (`domains/addresses.ts`): `siteAddresses` answers where a Site's pages are for visitors (`main`: the verified Primary domain's origin, else the Staging address; `languages`: each language's verified domain), and `placeOf` where a path in a language is. hreflang, the Language switcher, canonical URLs and shared Media addresses use them (see "SEO"); sitemaps (#92) and Social cards should too. Media files are served on whichever of the Site's addresses asks (`/_media/…` is relative).

## Forms

Forms (GLOSSARY.md) are split along the Module boundary (ADR 0024). **Core** owns what only core can do: a Form is Structure in the content store (`packages/core/src/forms/`, behind `services.forms`; table `forms`), `{{@form <key>}}` is a Template command, the renderer renders it, and the Site pipeline answers the post path and runs every check. **The Forms Module** (`modules/forms`, importing only `@webcraft/module-sdk`, in `FIRST_PARTY_MODULES`) owns what the SDK is for: it keeps the Submissions (its table `m_forms.submissions`, inbox and Spam folder) and lets people work through them (see "Submissions" below), and purges them daily (the Spam folder after 30 days, each Form's by its retention). It is switched per Site like any Module: where it is off (or not approved), Forms render nothing and the post path answers 404. What a kept Submission sets off (Notifications, the Auto-reply, "Form submitted" Webhooks) is core's (`forms/kept.ts`), since it needs the Form's published emails, the Site's mail and the Webhooks: the Module host runs it once the Module keeps a Submission, and the Module asks for it when a Submission comes out of spam (`context.submissions.kept`, SDK 1.6). Sections, steps, conditional logic, the forms script and uploads (#90) build on the version and the renderer.

- **A Form** (`forms.*`, all `structure.edit`): a `name` (shown in the admin), a `key` (lower-case words joined by dashes, unique on the Site; made from the name when not given; `{{@form <key>}}` writes it, trees store the id, so `forms.rename` changes nothing placed), and a Draft and a published version, each a `FormVersion` (`packages/renderer/src/forms/version.ts`, which the admin reads as `@webcraft/renderer/forms`): `fields`, `submitLabel`, `success` (`{ kind: "message", message }` or `{ kind: "redirect", pageId }`, a Page shown in the visitor's language) and `spam` (`minFillSeconds`, `perMinute`, `perHour`: 3, 5 and 30 unless Staff change them). `forms.saveDraft` takes any of those parts and replaces them; `forms.publish` goes through `publish` with `{ kind: "form" }` (logged `form.publish`, clears the page cache); `forms.delete` takes two steps and is refused while a Template or Component places it (`FORM_IN_USE`, `data.usedIn`, which `forms.get` answers as `usage`). A refused Draft answers `FORM_INVALID` with each problem at its place (`fields[2].options`, `success.pageId`).
- **Fields**: `text`, `email`, `phone`, `url`, `textarea`, `number`, `date`, `select`, `radio`, `checkboxes`, `consent` (one box) and `hidden` (its value from a parameter of the page's address, the page's address, or fixed text, filled in as the Form is sent; never rendered). Every field has every setting (`label`, `hideLabel`, `help`, `placeholder`, `message`, `required`, then its type's: `minLength`, `maxLength`, `pattern` (RE2, as Fields' patterns), `autocomplete`, `min`, `max`, `integer`, `minDate`, `maxDate`, `options`, `minChoices`, `maxChoices`, `source`), unset ones null; `FIELD_TYPE_SETTINGS` says which a type uses, and a setting it doesn't use is refused. Every field has a label, which `hideLabel` keeps for screen readers. Keys are lower-case letters, digits and underscores, unique in the Form.
- **Text in every language**: labels, help, placeholders, options, messages, the button and the message once sent are `FormText`, `{ text, translations }`, as fixed text in Templates keeps it (#80): pages in a language show its value, every other page the text as written. A new Form's button and message are WebCraft's words (`formWords`, the seven languages pages have) in the Site's Default language, translated into its other languages.
- **Rendering** (`renderForm`, `packages/renderer/src/forms/render.ts`): a `<form method="post" action="/_webcraft/forms/<id>" novalidate>` with every label linked to its control, help and errors linked with `aria-describedby`, radio buttons and checkboxes in a `<fieldset>` and `<legend>`, `required` on required controls (and a visible asterisk explained once), hidden inputs `wc-page` (the page's address: its path, and `?page=N` past the first page of a paginated Loop), `wc-i` (which Form of the page, from 1: its elements' ids are `wc-form-<n>-…`) and `wc-t` (the time trap's token), and a honeypot (`wc-website`, out of sight through the stylesheet, out of the tab order, `aria-hidden`). No inline style or script; the classes are WebCraft's hooks (`FORM_HOOKS`, styled in the Design system's base layer, `wc-visually-hidden` included). Pages render the published version, Code mode the Draft; `pageForms` (`forms/page-forms.ts`) loads them for `renderPage`.
- **Cached pages hold no token**: a page rendered for the page cache holds `FORM_TOKEN_MARK` where each Form's token goes, and the pipeline puts a fresh token there in every answer (`withFormTokens`; the ETag stays the cached page's). Every other answer (Edit mode, the 404 page, a page answered for one visitor) gets its own token. A token is the time the page was handed out, signed with a key derived from the Master key (`formTimeKey`, `createFormTokens`); it names no visitor.
- **Posting** (`POST /_webcraft/forms/<id>`, `apps/server/src/site/forms.ts`, then `services.forms.receive`): any other method is 405; a post must come from the Site's own pages (`Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, else an `Origin` on the same host; 403 otherwise; the Site's policy, `form-action 'self'`, covers the other way); the body is `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` (415 otherwise; uploads come with #90) of at most `FORM_POST_MAX_BYTES` (512 KiB; a larger one is kept as spam, `size`, and answered 413). Then, in order: the time trap (no token this Install made for the Site: `token`; sooner than `minFillSeconds`: `too-fast`), the honeypot (`honeypot`), the Form's rules (the server's check is the one that counts: `checkValues`, `forms/values.ts`), the per-address limits (every kept post counts, per Form and client address, `rateLimits.count`; beyond either limit: `rate-limit`) and the Site's CAPTCHA. A post with errors (and no spam reason) answers 422 with the page it came from (page N of a paginated Loop, as `wc-page` says) rendered again for that visitor (`no-store`, `noindex`): the title starts with "Error:", an error summary (`role="group"`, `autofocus`, so focus lands there) links to each field, each field shows its error (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`), in the page's language (WebCraft's words, or the field's own `message`), and what was typed is kept; nothing is kept or counted. When that page no longer shows the Form, a page of the Form alone (`sitePages.formPage`) answers instead. A post that passes, or is spam, is handed to the Forms Module and answered 303 (post, redirect, get): to the Form's Page in the visitor's language, or to the page it came from with `?wc-sent=<n>#wc-form-<n>` (`/blog?page=2&wc-sent=1#wc-form-1` from page 2), which the pipeline answers for that visitor alone, before the page cache with the Form's message in its place (`role="status"`, `autofocus`). Spam looks sent to whoever sent it. Every Submission records its language (the page's) and page.
- **The Spam folder** keeps spam (with its reason) 30 days: the Forms Module's `purgeSpam` job runs daily in every Site where it runs (SDK 1.2 schedules). Spam sets off nothing (no Notification, Auto-reply, Webhook or `form.submitted`).
- **A Form's emails** (`FormEmails`, `packages/renderer/src/forms/emails.ts`, which the admin reads as `@webcraft/renderer/forms/emails`; checked by `forms/emails.ts`) are Content, unlike the rest of the Form: a Draft of their own (`forms.emails_draft`) and a published version (`emails_published`), changed with `forms.saveEmails` and published with `forms.publishEmails` (`{ kind: "formEmails" }`, logged `formEmails.publish`), both `formEmails.edit` (Staff, Owners, Editors), apart from the Form's fields: publishing one never publishes the other. Nothing goes out until they are first published. They hold:
  - **Notifications** (at most 10): `recipients` (at most 20 addresses, each one valid address, normalised), `routing` (`{ field, routes: [{ value, recipients }] }`: a select or radio field of the Form's Draft and its options' values; a value with a route goes to its addresses, any other to `recipients`), `subject` (one line, at most 200; empty for WebCraft's words) and `intro` (at most 2,000; empty for WebCraft's words). A refused change answers `FORM_INVALID` with each problem at its place (`notifications[0].recipients[1]`).
  - **The Auto-reply**: `enabled`, `field` (an email field of the Form; null for its first one), `subject` and `text` (`FormText`, each Site language's own words, the text as written for the rest; required while it is on).
- **What a kept Submission sets off** (`createSubmissionActions`, `forms/kept.ts`), queued at once as retried jobs once it is stored, never for spam; a failure to queue is logged and never fails the visitor's post:
  - one **Notification** email per recipient (`form-notification` in the Email log), in the Site's Default language: the subject and opening, each value with its field's label (choices by their option labels, a consent Yes or No, an empty value a dash), the Form, page, language and time, and a link to the Submission in the admin (`/sites/<id>/forms/<id>?tab=submissions&submission=<id>`); Reply-To is the first email field the visitor filled in with a valid address;
  - the **Auto-reply** (`form-auto-reply`) to the address in its field, in the visitor's language (the page's), with the Form's words only, never anything the visitor typed (else anyone could send their own words to any address from the Site), and a line saying why they get it (WebCraft's words, `formEmailWords`, in the seven languages Form words come in);
  - the **`formSubmitted` Webhook event** (see "Webhooks"), and `form.submitted` for Modules.
  Both emails are the Site's mail (see "Email"): plain, escaped layout (`renderSiteMail`: no visitor markup, line breaks kept), subjects on one line, every address checked before it reaches a header (`mailAddress`). A Submission taken out of spam sets off the same, its Auto-reply only within `AUTO_REPLY_WINDOW_MS` (a day) of when it came.
- **Submissions** (the Forms Module, `m.forms.*`; Permissions `forms.submissions.view` to read and export, `forms.submissions.manage` to delete, take out of spam and set retention: Staff, Owners and Editors, never Authors): `submissions.list` (of a Form or all, a folder, a search in the values (`search_text`, the `LIKE` wildcards taken as text), read or unread, a language; newest first, `limit`/`offset`; with `total`, the Form's `counts` and `keepDays`), `submissions.get` (one, with its **delivery log**: each email and Webhook call sent for it, from the Email log and the Webhooks' delivery log, through `context.submissions.deliveries`), `submissions.setRead` (read or unread for everyone), `submissions.notSpam` (to the inbox, unread, then `context.submissions.kept`; logged `forms.submissions.notSpam`), `submissions.delete` (two steps; logged `forms.submissions.delete`), `submissions.export` (CSV: UTF-8 with a BOM, every cell quoted, a cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return prefixed with `'`; logged `forms.submissions.export`), `retention.get` and `retention.set` (forever, 30, 90 or 365 days per Form, table `m_forms.retention`; logged `forms.retention.change`). The daily `purgeExpired` job deletes each Form's Submissions older than it keeps them (logged `forms.submissions.purge`, by the Module). Every way a Submission goes passes through `removeSubmissions`, where Form uploads (#90) will go with it. Each Submission keeps its sender (`FormSender`, never an address) to hand back when it comes out of spam.
- **CAPTCHA** (optional, off by default; `forms.captcha.get` and `forms.captcha.set`, `structure.edit`, setting it is the Sensitive action `formCaptcha.change`; table `form_settings`): provider-agnostic, for any challenge provider of the usual shape: its script (`scriptUrl`, an outside script: `PageToRender.formScripts`, asked once the page has rendered and loaded only where a Form shows, never in Edit mode; the page's policy allows the provider's host for its script, frames and requests, `policyHosts`), the class its widget's element takes and the Site's public key (a `<div class="<widgetClass>" data-sitekey>` in each Form), the field its widget fills in (`responseField`), and where answers are checked (`verifyUrl`): WebCraft posts `secret`, `response` and `remoteip` there through the guarded fetcher (that host only, no redirect, 10 s), with the Site secret `forms.captcha.secret` (`data.useSiteSecret`), and accepts `{ "success": true }`. No answer, or a refused one, comes back as an error to try again; a provider that can't be asked keeps the post as spam (`captcha`), so nothing is lost. Switching it off forgets the secret.
- **The client address** comes from `clientAddress` alone: behind trusted proxies the forwarded one, and behind the Site's CDN the visitor the CDN names (see "How a request flows").
- **The admin**: Site › Forms (Structure) lists the Forms with their keys, status and where they are placed, makes new ones, deletes them, and holds the CAPTCHA card. Each Form has its own sidebar item (`apps/server/src/ui/forms/`, the Site place lists the Forms for people holding `structure.edit` or `formEmails.edit`): Submissions (`SubmissionsTab.tsx`, for those holding `forms.submissions.view`, which `sites.get` answers in `modulePermissions`) and Emails (`EmailsTab.tsx`, `formEmails.edit`) for clients and Staff, Build, Settings and Translations for Staff. The tab and the open Submission are in the address (`?tab=submissions&submission=<id>`); opening a Submission marks it read.

## Redirects and the 404 log

Links never break. The Redirects and 404 log Module (`modules/redirects`, importing only `@webcraft/module-sdk`, in `FIRST_PARTY_MODULES`, on by default once approved, switched per Site like any Module) keeps a Site's Redirect rules (its table `m_redirects.rules`) and its 404 log (`m_redirects.misses`); the Site pipeline asks it through the SDK's Redirect rules and 404 log (see "Modules › Extension points" and the pipeline's steps 1b and 9).

- **Who**: Redirect rules are Content (GLOSSARY.md): Editors, Owners and Staff manage them and the 404 log (the Module's Permission `redirects.manage`; never Authors), on Site › Redirects (the Module's admin page, `apps/server/src/ui/redirects/`), with the 404 log as a tab (`?tab=log`). Procedures: `m.redirects.rules.list`, `add`, `update`, `remove`, `m.redirects.notFound.list` (`ignored: true` lists the ignored paths) and `notFound.ignore`. Refusals are `MODULE_ERROR` with `data.code`: `FROM_INVALID`, `FROM_RESERVED`, `WILDCARD_INVALID`, `TO_INVALID`, `TO_SCHEME`, `RULE_TAKEN`, `RULE_LOOP`, `RULE_NOT_FOUND`, `MISS_NOT_FOUND`.
- **No Draft**: a rule applies as soon as it is saved, like Site settings, and isn't part of Publish all (ADR 0014 lists the Drafts it publishes): a rule only sends visitors on from a path, and the rules WebCraft adds must go live with the publish that moved one. Each change is in the Audit log: `redirects.rule.add`, `redirects.rule.update`, `redirects.rule.remove`, `redirects.notFound.ignore` and `redirects.notFound.show`, and `redirects.rules.follow` (one entry per event, with how many rules were added, changed and removed) for the Module's own changes.
- **A rule** sends a Site path to a target with a 301 (moved for good) or a 302 (elsewhere for now). Its path is canonical (decoded parts encoded again, no trailing slash: `/café/` is `/caf%C3%A9`), or ends `/*` for that path and every path under it (`/old/*` covers `/old` and `/old/a/b`; a `*` anywhere else is refused). Paths starting with `/_` are the Install's own; a `?` or `#` is refused. A language's paths are under its prefix (`/fr/ancien`) whatever domain serves it: on the language's own domain the same rule answers `/ancien`, and from the Primary domain a target in that language goes straight to its domain (one hop). One rule per path. The rule for the path itself beats a wildcard, and the nearest wildcard a wider one.
