- **Targets**: a Site path (made canonical; a query or fragment kept as typed, a space in it written `%20`), or an `https://` address without a user name or password; any other scheme (`javascript:`, `http:`, `data:`) and `//host` are refused, and a backslash is encoded, so a target can't name another host by accident. A target ending `/*` carries the rest of the path over (`/old/*` to `/new/*` sends `/old/a/b` to `/new/a/b`), only from a rule ending `/*`. These open redirects are configured only by Editors, Owners and Staff, and WebCraft checks every target again when it answers.
- **Query strings**: the request's query is kept, its parameters added after the target's own; a parameter the target sets wins.
- **Loops and chains**: a rule that would send visitors round in a circle is refused when it is saved (its target is followed through the other rules, up to 50 of them, `MAX_CHAIN`; a longer chain is refused the same way). A chain may be built in any order. At run time one answer goes through at most 5 rules (`MAX_REDIRECT_HOPS`): a longer chain sends visitors on from the fifth rule's target, said in the Module log, and the browser carries on from there; a loop is never followed (the Site's page answers, said once in the Module log).
- **Rules an Import run adds** (SDK 1.7, `redirectRules.add`): from where an item was on the old site to its new path, or from a file's old address to the Media file (`media:<id>`, which the Site pipeline sends to the file's address as it is then, and nowhere while it isn't ready or is in the Trash). They are automatic rules (they step aside where a page answers), never take a path that has a rule, and are logged as `redirects.rules.import`; Undo removes them (`redirectRules.remove`, `redirects.rules.undoImport`), each only while still as added.
- **Rules WebCraft adds**: the Module follows `entry.published` (each publish's `pathChanges`: a new slug, a Page that moved and every Page under it) and `paths.changed` (a Post Type's new URL pattern, a new Default language), always after the event, as a job. The old path gets an automatic 301 to the new one; a rule to the old path, a person's included, now sends visitors to the new one (A→B, then B→C, is A→C), and one that would then send a path to itself goes. No rule is added at a path another page now has (a new path of the same change, such as the old English address the French page answers after a Default language change), nor over a person's rule. Automatic rules **step aside where a page answers** (`whereNoPage`), so a page published at their path later shows at once; the Module then takes the rule away. Each change is stamped with when it happened, and an older event never undoes a newer one (event jobs may run out of order). Changing an automatic rule makes it a person's.
- **The 404 log** keeps each Site path a visitor's browser asked for and found no page at: its language, how many times, when first and last, and the host of the last page that linked there (from `Referer`, the host alone). It never keeps an address, the query string (it may hold personal data), robots (by user agent, `isRobot`), `HEAD` requests, prefetches, Edit mode, files (`/favicon.ico`, `/old.html`: browsers and robots ask for many, which would bury the missing pages; rules still apply to such paths) or the Install's own paths. It is bounded: the 1,000 paths seen most recently per Site (trimmed back once past 1,100), each until 90 days after it was last seen (`purgeLog`, daily). **Ignore** hides a path (still counted) until someone shows it again; **Add redirect** opens the rule dialog at the path, and a rule takes the paths it covers out of the log, as does a page published there.

## Edit mode

Edit mode (ADRs 0016, 0022) shows a Site on its own domain with its Drafts, and lets a signed-in person change Content in place. Core: `packages/core/src/edit-mode/` (`services.editMode`); the web side: `apps/server/src/site/edit-mode.ts`; the editing bar: `apps/server/src/site/edit-bar/`.

### Getting in: the signed link and the edit cookie

The admin's session cookie belongs to the admin's hostname and never reaches a Site's domain, so the admin hands over a link instead.

1. **"Edit on site"** (the admin's top bar on a Site's pages) calls `editMode.open({ siteId, path })` (`entry.edit`); its menu's "Edit in Safe mode" adds `safeMode: true`, and the link then says `&safe=1`, which starts Safe mode as the link is followed (any other link ends it). It answers a link on the Site's address (its verified Primary domain, else its Staging address; the edit cookie then lives on that hostname, and Edit mode keeps every language under its prefix there), `…/_webcraft/edit/enter?token=…`, which works **once**, within **60 seconds**, for this person, this Site and the admin session that asked (`edit_links`, only the token's SHA-256 stored). `path` must be a path on the Site (`isEditPath`: one leading `/`, no query, fragment, backslash or space), so a link can't send anyone elsewhere (`EDIT_PATH_INVALID`). A Site with no address answers `EDIT_MODE_UNAVAILABLE`.
2. **Following it** (`GET /_webcraft/edit/enter`, rate-limited like sign-in) uses the link up, checks that the admin session is still signed in and that the person still reaches the Site with `entry.edit`, starts an **Edit mode session** (`edit_sessions`, for at most **8 hours**, records `editMode.start`) and answers `303` to the page with the edit cookie: `__Host-webcraft.edit` (named `webcraft.…` like every cookie WebCraft sets on a Site's domain, so Modules' Site routes never see it), `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, `Max-Age` up to 8 hours (on a `.localhost` development host: `webcraft.edit`, without `Secure`). The answer has `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `no-store`, so the token neither leaks nor stays in history. A used, expired, unknown or other Site's link is a plain `403`.
3. **Every request after that is checked again** (`services.editMode.session`): the session hasn't run out, its admin session is still signed in (`auth.activeSession(id)`: by id, server-side, without the cookie), and the person still reaches the Site with `entry.edit` (the data layer runs `can()`, with `via: editMode`). A session that fails a check is ended there. Both tables hang off the admin session (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), so **signing out, a revoked session or losing access ends Edit mode at once**; the next page is a visitor's and clears the cookie, the next edit request answers `401 EDIT_MODE_ENDED`.

### Pages in Edit mode

A page request with a session that holds gets `services.sitePages.render(host, path, editing)` (`notFound(host, editing)` for a path with no page): the same page found the same way as for visitors, showing the **Drafts of its Content** (its Entry and the Globals) through the **published Templates** and the published Components they place (Template and Component Drafts show in Code mode, see below, and on the Staging address, see "The Staging address"); a path with no published page finds an Entry whose Draft would be published there, so a Page never published can be edited in place, rendered afresh and never kept in the page cache. It carries:

- **Tags on editable regions** (`RenderOptions.editRegion`): an element whose only content is one binding without a formatter, when the binding is the Entry's `title`, a text or rich text Field (in Groups too: `address.city`; `editableField` in `content/editable.ts`) or such a Global (`site.x`, in the content or the Site template), gets `data-wc-item` (`entry:<id>` or `globals`), `data-wc-field` (the binding's path within it), `data-wc-type` (`text`, `richText`), `data-wc-label` (the Field's label; none for the title, which the bar names itself) and the element's id as `data-wc-id`. Values beside other text, through a formatter, in Repeaters, Relationships, links and selects aren't tagged, nor is a body (its block editor comes to Edit mode with #79). Elements inside a Component are never tagged (their ids are the Component's, not the page's Template's). Values inside a Loop are never tagged: `editRegion` hears the Loop item they sit in (`LoopItemPlace`: the items' name and `key`, `entry:<id>` or `loop:<loop id>:<item id>`), and `editRegions` (`site-pages/index.ts`) answers nothing for them, the seam where editing Loop items in place (#74) and Page Props (#75) add their bindings. Pages for visitors carry none of this.
- **WebCraft's own files first in `<head>`**: `<meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">`, the session's header token (`<meta name="webcraft-edit-token">`), the editing bar's script (a classic script, so it runs before any Site script) and the page stylesheet, all as hashed files at `/_webcraft/edit/assets/<name>-<hash>` (`public, max-age=31536000, immutable`).
- **Headers**: `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`, `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and Edit mode's own strict policy, whatever the Site's mode (`editModePolicy`): scripts, styles and fonts only from the Site's own domain, requests to it and its allowed hosts (which only the Site's own scripts can make there), `frame-src https:` (iframe embeds still show), `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `object-src 'none'`.
- **No outside scripts** (ADR 0022): `editMode` on `PageToRender` is the switch. Besides WebCraft's own, the renderer writes only the Site's own scripts: its Libraries (`data-wc-library`) and the published Site script and page script (`data-wc-script`, see "Scripts"). Each outside script shows instead as a labelled placeholder at the end of the page (`<aside class="wc-outside-scripts">`, styled by Edit mode's page stylesheet). In Safe mode the Site's own scripts are left out too (see "Site script safety").

### Edit requests

The editing bar talks to `POST /_webcraft/edit/api/<action>` on the Site's domain, with JSON. Each request needs, in this order: a `POST` from the Site's own pages (`Origin` the Site's, and `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` when the browser sends it; else `403 EDIT_REQUEST_REFUSED`), an edit cookie whose session still holds (`401 EDIT_MODE_ENDED`), and the session's **header token** in `X-WebCraft-Edit-Token` (an HMAC of the session id under a key derived from the Master key, `editModeSigningKey`; `403 EDIT_REQUEST_REFUSED`). Refusals answer `{ code, message, data }` with the Management API's codes (`toApiError`).

| Action | Body | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `state` | `{ path }` | The page's state (`PageState`): its title and item, the person, the Site, the language, `canPublish` (`entry.publish`), the page's **changes** (its Entry's values, then those of the Globals it shows, that differ from what is published, each with its label, type and who wrote it), `publishAll` (Publish all in the admin and how many other Drafts the person may publish there) and the Site's pages for the page switcher. Adds the bar's `words` in the person's Admin language (the `editBar` part of the admin's message files). |
| `save` | `{ item, field, value }` | Saves one region's value to the Draft through `saveDraft` / `saveGlobalsDraft` (the admin's path: rich text is cleaned, wrong kinds refused, broken rules kept and listed). Answers `{ status, value, problems }`, `problems` being those of that value. Only the title and text and rich text Fields (`EDIT_FIELD_INVALID` otherwise), and only on this Site. |
| `undo` | `{ path, item, key }` | Puts one of the page's changes back as published (`undoDraftChanges`, `undoGlobalsChanges`). |
| `discard` | `{ path }` | Puts every change of the page back, in one transaction. |
| `publish` | `{ path }` | Publishes the page's changed items (its Entry, then the Globals), all or none, with `entry.publish` (Authors get `FORBIDDEN`). Recorded as `entry.publish` / `globals.publish` with `via: editMode`. |
| `safe/start`, `safe/stop` | `{}` | Turns Safe mode on or off for this browser: the Safe mode cookie (`__Host-webcraft.safe`, `webcraft.safe` on `.localhost`), for the rest of the Edit mode session. |
| `end` | `{}` | Ends this Edit mode session and clears the cookie (and Safe mode's). |

Saving a Draft isn't written to the Audit log; publishing is. Edit mode never takes a Sensitive action (`assertSensitiveActionAllowed` refuses anything but the admin). #71 (Co-editing) turns `save` into changes to the Draft's shared document, through the same functions. Code mode's requests (`code/…`) go through the same checks, then `structure.edit` (see "Code mode").

### The editing bar

`editingBar` (`edit-bar/bar.ts`) is one self-contained function: the served script is its source text with its config (`edit-bar/assets.ts`), named by its hash, so it must use nothing from outside itself (no imports, no module-level values). It keeps the browser functions it needs as it starts, before any Site script, and draws itself in an open shadow root (`<webcraft-edit-bar>`, right after the skip link), styled by its own stylesheet there (`BAR_STYLES`); the page stylesheet (`PAGE_STYLES`) only makes room for it and outlines the regions. Everything is a file on the Site's domain: no inline scripts or styles (positions are set through the CSSOM, which the CSP allows).

- **The bar** (variant A of the inline editing prototype): the Site's name, the page switcher (a menu of the Site's pages), the Draft status, Edit and Preview (and Code, for those who may change the Site's Structure: `canCode` in the page state), device widths (desktop, 768 px, 390 px: the page's width, not its media queries), the current language (a menu of the page in each of the Site's languages when it has more than one), the Publish menu and the person's menu (open the admin, end Edit mode). A first-time hint explains Edit mode until dismissed (kept in local storage).
- **Editing in place**: regions get `contenteditable` (`plaintext-only` for text where the browser has it; Enter doesn't break a line). Focus or hover shows the region's label (`Intro · Rich text`); rich text shows a floating toolbar (bold, italic, link, lists, clear formatting, undo, Done) above it, or below when there's no room under the bar, reached from the text with **Alt+F10**, moved through with the arrow keys, left with Escape. Changes save to the Draft 0.8 s after typing stops and when focus leaves; each save is announced in the bar's live region (`role="status"`), and a value's problems show next to the region (and are announced).
- **The Publish menu** lists the page's changes, each with Undo, then Discard (with a confirmation) and Publish page; Authors see "Saved to Draft" instead. It links to Publish all in the admin (the Site's page with `?publishAll=1`, which opens the list at once) with the number of other Drafts the person may publish.
- **Focus**: the bar never covers the focused element (the page has `scroll-padding-top`, and the bar scrolls anything focused under it into view). Menus and the Publish menu open from their buttons, Escape closes them and returns focus; their items exist only while they are open.
- It reads the page's state again every minute and when the tab comes back, so a session that ended shows "Edit mode has ended" and stops editing.
- **Safe mode** (ADR 0022): the person's menu turns it on and off (`safe/start`, `safe/stop`, then the page loads again); while it is on (`<meta name="webcraft-safe-mode" content="on">`) the bar shows a "Safe mode" chip.
- **A failing Site script**: the bar listens for `error` events from the start, before any Site script runs. One from a file tagged `data-wc-script` or `data-wc-library` (a script that throws, or one that doesn't load) shows a notice under the bar (`role="alert"`): which script failed and its message with the line, "Open in Safe mode" (outside Safe mode) and, for those who may code (`canCode`), "Open it in Code mode", which turns Code mode on and leaves the script's kind in session storage (`webcraft-code-script`) so Code mode's JS panel opens at it, its editor focused.

### Code mode

Code mode (ADR 0016, `GLOSSARY.md`) is a third way of using Edit mode, for Staff and the Superadmin only (`structure.edit`). Core: `packages/core/src/code-mode/` (`services.codeMode`); the web side: the `code/…` actions in `apps/server/src/site/edit-mode.ts`; the panels: `apps/server/src/site/code-mode/`.

- **Turning it on**: the editing bar's Code button sends `code/start` (refused with `FORBIDDEN` for anyone without `structure.edit`), which sets the Code mode cookie (`__Host-webcraft.code`, `webcraft.code` on `.localhost`; `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, for the rest of the Edit mode session), then reloads; `code/stop` clears it. The cookie only says the browser wants Code mode: every page and request checks the edit cookie's session and then opens the Site with `structure.edit` through the data layer (`codeMode.access`, `codeModeOf`), so a Member's page ignores it and visitors never get Code mode.
- **Pages in Code mode** (`PageEditing.code`) show the **Drafts of the Site's Structure** around the Drafts of its Content: the Entry template's and Site template's Drafts (the built-in Site template while there is none), the Components' Drafts (`siteComponents(scope, "draft")`), the Design system's Draft (its stylesheet at `/_webcraft/edit/css/<hash>.css`, built by `draftStyles` from the Draft and every Draft tree, served only to Staff in Code mode, `no-store`) and the scripts' Drafts. Content editing (Edit, Preview) keeps showing the published Structure, so Members and Content edits never see a half-made Template. The head loads Code mode's own script after the bar's, and `<meta name="webcraft-code-mode" content="on">`.
- **Tags**: every element carries `data-wc-code` (`RenderOptions.codeSource`), where it comes from: `content` (the page's Entry template) or `site` (the Site template), then the id of each Placement showing it, then its own id: `content/<placement id>#<element id>` (`readCodeRef`). Editable regions aren't tagged in Code mode: a click selects an element instead of editing it. Visitors' and Content editing's pages carry none of this.
- **Opening** (`code/open { path, ref }`, `ref` null for the page's content, else the Site template): follows the reference through the Drafts (`resolveSource`) and answers the Template or Component as Template markup with its **version**, `editable` (a Module's Component isn't), the element (its id and tag), its `path` in the source (`article › h1.title`, a Placement as `<Hero>`), the **breadcrumb** (each Template or Component on the way, with the reference that opens it at the next Placement), `usage.pages` for a Component (the "used on N pages" warning) and the **suggestions**: the Entry's Fields and built-in values and their parts (`address.city`, `link.url`, a Relationship's `title`), the Globals (`site.x`, with their Draft values), a Component's Props (`props.x`), the Components it may place with their Props (never one placing it), the Template commands from `TEMPLATE_COMMANDS` that this kind of Template may hold (each entry's `snippet`, so a new command shows by itself), WebCraft's presets (`BUILT_IN_PRESETS`; Modules' Structure presets, #110, join them), Loops (none until #62), and the Draft's Classes and token utilities. A reference the page doesn't show is `CODE_SOURCE_NOT_FOUND` (404).
- **Previewing** (`code/preview { path, markup?: { ref, text }, css?: { target, text } }`): renders the whole page as Code mode shows it with the unsaved markup read in place of its source's Draft (`readTemplateMarkup`, `readComponentMarkup`: the same rules as saving; inline styles become generated Classes for the preview only) and unsaved CSS read into the Design system's Draft (`withChange`), answering `{ html, stylesheet: { hash, css }, notices }`. Nothing is saved. Markup that doesn't parse answers `TEMPLATE_MARKUP_INVALID` with each problem's line and column (UTF-16 units); CSS the checks refuse, `DESIGN_SYSTEM_INVALID`.
- **Saving** goes through the same saves as the Management API, only when Staff save: `code/save { path, ref, markup, version }` (`templates.save…Markup`, `components.saveMarkup`), `code/saveCss { target, text, version }` (a Class's `styles`, one breakpoint's styles or its own `css`, or the Site CSS, through the Design system's Draft and its checks), `code/saveScript { path, kind, code, version }`. Each names the **version** it was opened at (null when there was nothing yet), and a Draft someone saved meanwhile answers `CHANGED_MEANWHILE` (409) with `data.version`, the version now: the panel offers to reload it, or to save again over that version (overwrite). Nothing is merged. `code/createClass { name }` adds a Class to the Draft for a name the HTML panel doesn't know. `code/css` and `code/scripts` read what the CSS and JS panels show.
- **Versions**: a Draft's version is `draftVersion` (16 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of its JSON, keys in order); a Class's covers the whole Class, the Site CSS's its text, a script's is its Draft's hash. The Management API's markup reads and saves answer `version` and take `basedOn` (`templates.saveEntryTemplateMarkup`, `saveSiteTemplateMarkup`, `components.saveMarkup`), so the CLI can refuse stale pushes the same way.
- **The panels** (`code-mode/code.ts`, `codeMode`, like the editing bar one self-contained function served as a hashed file at `/_webcraft/edit/assets/code-<hash>.js` and drawn in an open shadow root, `<webcraft-code-mode>`, styled by `CODE_STYLES`): loaded only on pages in Code mode, so Content editing stays light; no library, so nothing outside the Site's domain and no inline styles (positions and colour swatches through the CSSOM; the preview's stylesheet through a constructed stylesheet, which the strict policy allows). The HTML, CSS and JS panels sit below the page (the page stylesheet makes room for them; they can be hidden), with the breadcrumb and the usage warning above. Each editor is a text area over the highlighted code (Template markup, CSS, JavaScript) with line numbers; Tab moves on (no trap), Enter keeps the indentation, Ctrl+S saves. Clicking an element of the page (or moving the caret into its markup) selects it. The HTML and CSS panels preview as Staff type (350 ms after they stop). **Format** puts elements on lines of their own only where whitespace never shows (next to a block boundary, between two tags, never next to text, values or commands, never in `<pre>`), so formatted markup saves to the same elements and reads back exactly. **Suggestions** open on `{{`, `{{@`, `<` and a capital, inside `class="`, and in CSS on a property or `--` (Ctrl+Space anywhere): a list box the text area points at with `aria-activedescendant`, showing nine options at a time around the active one, chosen with the arrows and Enter or Tab, closed with Escape; an unknown Class name offers "Create Class". The CSS panel's layout buttons (display, direction, justify, align, wrap) set declarations in the text. The JS panel's **Run** saves the script and runs the Site script then the page script on a freshly rendered page; the console (`role="log"`) shows the page's errors, rejected promises and console messages. A changed script never runs while Staff type.

## Site script safety

Scripts can't abuse Edit mode, and Sites run under a strict Content Security Policy (ADRs 0022, 0023). Core: `packages/core/src/site-scripts/` (`services.siteScripts`; what each page loads goes through `services.sitePages`); procedures `csp.*`, `outsideScripts.*` and `libraries.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/site-scripts.ts`); screens Site › Libraries (Structure) and Site › Outside scripts (Site settings). All of it is Structure: `structure.edit`, Staff only, with no Draft. A change applies at once, clears the Site's page cache and is recorded in the Audit log; none is part of Publish all.

### The policy

- **No inline scripts, no nonces**: pages are cached, so WebCraft never writes a script into a page. Its own scripts, the Site's scripts, Libraries and the code of outside scripts' snippets are files on the Site's domain. Each page's policy is worked out when it renders (`pagePolicyHeaders`), returned with its HTML (`SitePage.headers`) and kept with it in the page cache, so everything that changes a policy clears the cache.
- **Modes** (`sites.csp_mode`, `csp.setMode`): `strict`, the default: `default-src 'self'`, `script-src`, `style-src`, `font-src`, `connect-src` and `frame-src` from `'self'` and the allowed hosts, `img-src 'self' data: https:` and `media-src 'self' https:` (Templates may show outside images), then the fixed directives. `relaxed` adds `'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'` to `script-src` and `'unsafe-inline'` to `style-src`, for tag managers that inject code. `reportOnly` sends the strict policy as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` and enforces only the fixed directives.
- **Fixed headers on every answer** on a Site's hostname (`withSecurityHeaders` in the pipeline, `SITE_SECURITY_HEADERS`): `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, and on answers that bring no policy of their own `FILE_POLICY`: `object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'self'`. Media files get `MEDIA_POLICY`, the same without `object-src`, since a browser opens a PDF in its own viewer; SVG files get `SVG_POLICY`, which runs and loads nothing (see "Media › Serving"). Edit mode keeps its own stricter headers (see "Edit mode"). `form-action 'self'` never takes allowed hosts: forms never post elsewhere (ADR 0023).
- **Frames a page needs**: a page showing a body's video lets `PLAYER_ORIGINS` into `frame-src` (`renderDocument` answers the page with the `frames` it needs); other pages don't.
- **Allowed hosts** (`allowed_hosts`, at most 100): host names in lower case, or `*.` and a domain of at least two labels (`readHost` refuses anything else, so nothing but a host reaches the policy: `HOST_INVALID`), always over `https`. Each says how it got there: `hand` (`csp.addHost`, for what a tag manager loads) or `auto` (an outside script needs it; it goes with the last outside script needing it). Staff can remove either (`csp.removeHost`); an outside script needing a removed host shows it in `blockedHosts`. `csp.get` answers the mode, the hosts with the outside scripts using each (`usedBy`), and `moduleHosts`: the outside hosts of the Site-level Modules running on the Site (`modules.outsideHostsAt`), which its policy allows there and nowhere else. Approving, withdrawing or switching a Module with outside hosts clears the page caches it changes.

### Outside scripts

- **What they are** (`outside_scripts`, at most 50): a name, one https address or a pasted snippet, where they load (`head`: the end of `<head>`, after the Site's own scripts; `bodyEnd`: before `</body>`), their Consent category (`necessary`, `analytics`, `marketing`, `media`), in the Site's order. An address must be https on a host a policy can name, and loads `async`.
- **A snippet** (`readSnippet`, `snippets.ts`) may hold only `<script>` elements, comments and white space; `<noscript>` parts are left out. Anything else is refused with its line (`OUTSIDE_SCRIPT_INVALID`, `data.problems`): other elements (embeds belong in a Component), `on…` attributes, scripts that aren't JavaScript, addresses that aren't https on another host, a script never closed, a snippet with no script. Scripts loading from another host keep `async`, `defer`, `type="module"`, `id`, `crossorigin`, `referrerpolicy`, `integrity`, `charset` and `data-…`. A script holding code becomes a file, `/_wc/js/<hash>.js`, loaded where the snippet had it, without `async` or `defer`, so the order the snippet relies on holds. The hosts a snippet needs are those it loads from and those its code names in an `https://` address (a loader adding another script).
- **Never in Edit mode** (ADR 0022): there each shows as a labelled placeholder in the page's language instead; iframe embeds still show.
- **Consent**: the Consent banner holds back each outside script outside Necessary until visitors accept its category, as an inert `<template data-wc-consent>` in its place (see "The Consent banner"). Its host stays allowed, so it can load once accepted.

### Libraries

- **Adding** (`libraries.add`, at most 30 per Site) by package (`source: "npm"`: a name, a version that is exact, partial such as `3.13` for its newest release, or a tag such as `latest`, and optionally the `.js` file to serve) or by address (`source: "url"`, https). The download happens in the request, through the guarded fetcher, and is refused with `LIBRARY_REFUSED` and `data.problem` (`name`, `version`, `file`, `url`, `notFound`, `fileNotFound`, `integrity`, `size`, `empty`, `unreachable`, `limit`).
- **From the registry** (`npm.ts`), only `registry.npmjs.org`: the version's manifest (`/<name>/<version>`; a partial version reads the abbreviated list of versions first), then the archive from the registry's host only, at most 20 MB, checked against the manifest's `dist.integrity` (SHA-512; else `dist.shasum`), unpacked in memory (`tar.ts`). The file is the one given, else the first the manifest names that the archive holds (`jsdelivr`, `unpkg`, `browser`, `main`, then `index.js`); at most 5 MB. The licence the package declares is kept as data.
- **By address**: the file at the address, through the guarded fetcher to that host only, at most 5 MB.
- **Kept and served**: in the Install's storage, `<workspace id>/<site id>/libraries/<hash>-<file name>`, with a SHA-384 integrity hash; served at `/_wc/lib/<hash>-<file name>`, `immutable`. Pages load every Library in order with `defer` and `integrity`, before the Site script, in Edit mode too, never in Safe mode. A Library needs no allowed host.
- **Updates only when Staff ask**: `libraries.checkUpdates` asks the registry for each package's `latest` version and stores it (`latestVersion`; `newerVersion` when it is newer). `libraries.update` downloads the package's version (the newest unless given) of the same file, or downloads an address again; `libraries.remove` takes it off every page. The file an update or removal replaces is still served for 30 days.

### Safe mode

Edit mode with none of the Site's own scripts (ADR 0022): `PageEditing.safe` leaves out the Libraries, the Site script and page script (published, and the Drafts in Code mode); WebCraft's own scripts (the editing bar, Code mode's panels, the theme toggle, a body's video) and outside scripts' placeholders stay. It is the Safe mode cookie (`webcraft.safe`), set by an "Edit in Safe mode" link (`editMode.open` with `safeMode`) or by the editing bar (`safe/start`, `safe/stop`), for the rest of the Edit mode session; visitors' pages never read it. Pages in Safe mode say so in `<meta name="webcraft-safe-mode" content="on">`. A Site script that fails in Edit mode shows the editing bar's notice, with Safe mode and the script in Code mode a click away (see "The editing bar").

### Replaced files

Script and Library files are named by their hash and kept by browsers for a year, so a page loaded before a change still asks for the old one. The published Site script or page script a publish replaces, and the code of an outside script that a change replaces or removes, are kept in `retired_script_files`; Library files an update or a removal replaces, in `retired_library_files`. Both are still served for 30 days (`RETIRED_FILE_LIFETIME_MS`), then the daily `site-files.purge` (03:37 UTC) forgets them and removes the Library files from storage, unless a Library holds the same file again.

### Not Sensitive actions

Switching a Site to Relaxed, allowing a host and adding an outside script widen what a Site's visitors' browsers load, but none of them fits the list's rule (`SENSITIVE_ACTIONS`: changing a secret, a role, what a person reaches or how they sign in, or what a Module is granted), and none reaches an edit session: outside scripts never run in Edit mode, and Edit mode keeps its own strict policy whatever the Site's. Staff's own Site scripts, which do run in Edit mode, aren't Sensitive either. So they are Structure, Staff only and in the Audit log, and API tokens and AI clients with Structure access can manage them.

## The Consent banner

How a Site asks visitors which Consent categories they accept, and holds back what they haven't (ADR 0020, GLOSSARY.md). Core: `packages/core/src/consent/` (`services.consent`); procedures `consentBanner.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/consent.ts`); the page side: `packages/renderer/src/consent/` (the markup, its words, the consent script); the record endpoint: `apps/server/src/site/consent.ts`; the admin: Site › Consent banner (Site settings).

- **The setting** (`consent_banners.mode` and `layout`, `consentBanner.setSettings`, `structure.edit`): a Site setting (spec story 51), saved at once, logged as `consentBanner.change` with what changed, clearing the page cache, not part of Publish all. Modes: `optIn` (the default: nothing held back loads until accepted), `optOut` (everything loads until declined), `none` (no banner, nothing held back) and `outside` (an outside consent service asks; WebCraft shows no banner of its own and reads its answer). Layouts: `bar` (along the bottom), `box` (a corner) and `dialog` (centred, modal). A Site never changed has no row and reads as Opt-in, a bar.
- **Its words** are Content (ADRs 0003, 0014): a fixed set of Fields (`CONSENT_TEXT_FIELDS`: the heading, message, buttons, each category's name and description, the "Cookie settings" button, the privacy policy link as a link value, and what a held-back embed's placeholder says), translatable, with a Draft and a published version like the Globals. Members write them (`consentBanner.saveDraft`, `entry.edit`, Authors included) and publish them (`consentBanner.publish`, `entry.publish`, through `publish` with `{ kind: "consentBanner" }`, logged `consentBanner.publish`); a refused or rule-breaking Draft is `CONSENT_BANNER_INVALID` with `data.problems`. A word nobody wrote shows WebCraft's own in the page's language (`consentWords`, the seven languages pages have); a language without its own value shows the Default language's, word by word. `consentBanner.get` answers the setting, `version` and `text` (Fields, Draft, published, `status`, `problems`, `defaults` in the language asked for).
- **The banner version** goes up with every change visitors see (a setting change, a publish of the words); pages say it in `<meta name="webcraft-consent-version">` and each Consent record names the one it answered. A new version doesn't ask again: the categories never change.
- **Categories**: Necessary, Analytics, Marketing, Media. Held back on a page asking for consent: outside scripts by their stored `consentCategory`; Modules' Site scripts by their manifest's `siteScript`; a body's videos as Media. Never held back (Necessary): WebCraft's own scripts (the theme toggle, the embed script, the consent script), Libraries, the Site script and page scripts (Staff's own code, which can read `webcraft.consent` itself), and a Form's CAPTCHA script (it protects a post the visitor asked to send, and the Form can't be sent without it). Iframes can't be written in Templates, rich text or Components today, so a body's video is the only embed; later embeds (#103) are held back the same way (`HoldEmbed`).
- **Pages stay cached and the same for every visitor**: `renderDocument` takes `PageToRender.consent` (`pageConsent`: absent with None and in Edit mode). A page that holds something back (or shows `{{@consentSettings}}`) carries `<meta name="webcraft-consent-mode">`, the version, the consent script (`CONSENT_SCRIPT`, `/_wc/js/<hash>.js`, deferred before any other deferred script) and the banner right after the skip link (`hidden`, or a closed `<dialog>`), so the keyboard reaches it right after the skip link. Held-back scripts are inert `<template data-wc-consent="<category>">` elements in their place; a held-back embed is marked `data-wc-consent` with a hidden placeholder (`consentPlaceholderHtml`). A page holding nothing back has none of it, so Sites still ship no JavaScript by default. Without JavaScript nothing shows and nothing held back runs; a video stays its link.
- **The consent script** (`consent/script.ts`, documented at its top) reads the visitor's choice from the `webcraft.consent` cookie, shows the banner to a visitor who hasn't answered, turns accepted templates into running scripts (keeping their order), shows placeholders for embeds not accepted ("Load content" loads that one; its box accepts the category), records answers, reloads the page when a category is withdrawn after something of it ran, and opens the banner again from "Cookie settings". With an outside service it reads the TCF v2.2 data (`__tcfapi`), the consent-mode signal (`dataLayer`) or `webcraft.consent.set()`; the mapping is in the file. On a Site with WebCraft's banner, `set()` does nothing, so a script that always runs (a Library, a Site script) can't answer for the visitor.
- **For other scripts** (the seams for Site analytics, #102, and Popups, #100): `window.webcraft.consent` (`mode`, `version`, `answered`, `has(category)`, `choices()`, `set()`, `open()`, `whenAnswered()`), the `webcraft:consent` event on `document` (`detail: { choices, answered }`) at start and on every change, and `data-wc-consent-state` (`waiting`, `answered`) on `<html>`. Popups wait for `whenAnswered()`; a page without the consent script asks nothing, so there everything is accepted and nothing waits. Server side, the cookie names the record (below) the visitor's choice is in, and Modules' Site routes read the visitor's choice through `visitor.consent()` (the cookie by the Site's mode; see "Modules › Extension points"). A Module's Site script declared `beforeConsent` (Site analytics') isn't held back, but a page carrying it still asks for its category.
- **Consent records** (`consent_records`): `POST /_wc/consent` on the Site's own domain (`consentRecordPost`), JSON `{ choices: { analytics, marketing, media }, version, language }` from the Site's own pages (`Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, else a same-host `Origin`; 403 otherwise), at most 2 KB (413), JSON only (415), a version the Site has had and one of its languages (400 otherwise), only with Opt-in or Opt-out (404 otherwise), `CONSENT_RECORD_LIMIT` (10 a minute) per client address and Site (429 with `Retry-After`), `no-store`. The record is a random id, the time, the choices, the banner version and the language, never the address. The answer is `201 { id }` and sets `webcraft.consent` (`__Host-` on HTTPS): `<record id>.<version>.<bits>` (Analytics, Marketing, Media as `1`/`0`), `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` on HTTPS, six months, not `HttpOnly` (the consent script reads it). Like every `webcraft.*` cookie, Modules' Site routes never see it. `consentBanner.record` (`site.view`) finds one by id (`CONSENT_RECORD_NOT_FOUND` otherwise); `consent.purge-records` (daily, 04:41 UTC) removes records after 12 months.
- **Edit mode** (ADR 0022) never asks: no banner, no consent script, no placeholders (videos are their links), and outside and Modules' scripts don't run there anyway.
- **Styles**: the base layer styles the hooks (`CONSENT_HOOKS`: `wc-consent` with `data-layout`, `wc-consent-button`, `wc-consent-settings`, `wc-consent-placeholder`) in the page's own colours, "Accept all" and "Reject all" alike, with `--wc-consent-space`, `--wc-consent-radius`, `--wc-consent-shadow` and `--wc-consent-accent` set from the Design tokens by id; the banner slides in only without reduced motion. Staff restyle any of it in the Site's custom CSS.

## Site analytics

WebCraft's own cookieless analytics (ADRs 0019, 0020), a first-party Module: `modules/site-analytics`, off on each Site until Staff switch it on (Sites ship no JavaScript by default). Its tests are in `tests/analytics/`.

- **The collection script** (`script.ts`, under 2 KB): the Module's Site script, served as `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` from the Site's own domain, loaded deferred on every page where the Module runs, never in Edit mode. It runs before consent (`beforeConsent`), so pages carrying it ask for Analytics. It sends, with `navigator.sendBeacon`, as text, to `POST /_wc/m/analytics/event` (`connect-src 'self'`): one page view on load (the path, another site's host as the referrer or that the visitor came from the Site itself, the page's `utm_source`, `utm_medium` and `utm_campaign`, the window's width), and only once `webcraft.consent.has("analytics")`: a mark when the visitor accepts on the page, clicks on outbound, download, phone and email links, and custom events (`webcraft.analytics.track(name)`). Nothing from a browser sending Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control. It sets no cookie and keeps nothing.
- **The collection path** (`collect.ts`): only from the Site's own pages (`Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, else a same-host `Origin`; 403), at most 4 KB (413), 120 a minute per client address (429), `no-store`. Every event it takes answers 204, counted or left out. Left out: the Staging address, anyone of the Install (`visitor.signedIn()`), programs by their user agent (and requests without one), the Site's excluded address ranges, `DNT: 1` and `Sec-GPC: 1`.
- **Consent** (ADR 0020): before Analytics is accepted (by the visitor's answer on the Site's banner, which the server reads; with an outside consent service, by the page's word), only page views are kept, with no visitor id; link clicks, custom events and the mark wait for consent. After it, each event carries the visitor's daily id (`visitor.dailyId`) and a visit: a visit ends after 30 minutes without an event, and at midnight UTC, when ids change. The address is used to work out the country and the id, and to count against the limit, and is never kept or logged (`tests/analytics/dimensions.test.ts` checks no table and no log line holds it).
- **What is counted** (`measures.ts`): page views; visitors (distinct daily ids); visits; events (link clicks, custom events and Form Submissions, which the Module keeps from the `form.submitted` event, by Form, with nothing the visitor typed, on the collection path's terms as its `sender` says: only after Analytics consent, never from a Staging address, someone signed in, or with Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control; with an outside consent service, never, since WebCraft can't read that answer; the Site's excluded address ranges can't apply, as the event never holds the address). Since SDK 1.8 the event carries the sender's daily id (`visitor`, only when analytics may count them), and the Submission joins the visit that id had going on the Site (an event in the 30 minutes before), with the visit's source, country and device, so Funnels and filters count it; without such a visit it stands alone, counted by Form only. Every event of a visit carries the visit's source (`visit_source`: its arrival's; before consent a page view's own). By page, source (arrivals only: a campaign's `utm_source`, else the referring site's host without `www.`, else direct, empty; a move within the Site has none), country (empty when unknown), device (`desktop`, `mobile`, `tablet`), browser and operating system (families); and, for Goals (#103), by outbound link, download, phone link, email link, custom event and Form.
- **Storage** (ADR 0019): raw events (`events`) in monthly partitions, kept 13 months unless the Superadmin sets otherwise (Install settings › Modules); hourly and daily rollups (`rollups`, one row per period and value of each dimension, and the totals), built by the Module's hourly `rollUp` job for every hour and day that ended, kept forever. Days are UTC days (Sites have no time zone yet).
- **The query interface** (`query.ts`): the one way anything reads a Site's analytics (Dashboards' Widgets do; Reports and Share links will), so a column store could go behind it later. The Module's procedures (`analytics.view`, Staff and Owners): `stats.totals`, `stats.series` (by hour, at most 31 days, or by day), `stats.breakdown` (by a dimension, the biggest first), `stats.goals` and `stats.funnel` (below) and `stats.coverage` (the oldest raw event still kept, and how far rollups are built). A range from midnight to midnight counts by day, any other by whole hours; built periods come from the rollups, the rest from the raw events; a range's visitors are its periods' added up.
- **Filters** (`filters.ts`, every `stats` procedure's optional `filters`, and Widgets'): a page (`exact`, or `prefix`: it and the pages under it), a source (the visit's: a visit from a source counts as a whole; empty for direct), a country (empty for unknown), a device, and `goal`: the visitors who reached a Goal (by name) in the range, through `context.data.values` (SDK 1.8). Rollups hold each dimension on its own, so a filtered count reads the raw events only, as far back as they are kept (`countedFrom` on a Widget says where its counts start when the range reaches further).
- **Goals** (`goals.ts`; table `goals`; Structure: `goals.list` for `analytics.view`, `goals.create`, `update`, `delete` for `structure.edit`): a name (unique on the Site whatever its case, at most 100 Goals) and what it counts: a Form's Submissions (`form`), a page view of a path (`page`, `exact` or `prefix`), a click on an outbound, download, phone or email link (`link`, optionally whose target matches a pattern, `*` for any characters, upper and lower case alike) or a custom event by name (`event`). Only what visitors did after accepting Analytics counts: page views before consent count towards totals, never Goals. `stats.goals` answers each Goal's conversions and the visitors who reached it, with the range's visitors (who accepted Analytics), the whole a conversion rate is a share of. Deleting takes two steps (`context.confirmations`) and is refused while a Funnel uses the Goal (`GOAL_IN_FUNNEL`). Logged `site-analytics.goal.create`, `.update`, `.delete`.
- **Funnels** (`funnels.ts`; table `funnels`; the same Permissions; `funnel.create`, `.update`, `.delete` logged): two to five steps, each a Goal (by id) or a page visit, counted per visitor within one day (the daily id): a visitor reaches a step when one of their events that day reaches it after the step before (`stats.funnel`; the events of the visitors who reached the first step, walked in order in memory with `goalMatches`, the mirror of `goalWhere`). A Form Submission step counts Submissions linked to the sender's visit (above).
- **Widgets** (`widgets.ts`, SDK 1.8): the catalog Dashboards are built from: `totals`, `trend`, `top-pages`, `sources`, `countries`, `devices`, `browsers`, `operating-systems`, `links`, `custom-events`, `goals`, `goal` and `funnel`, each reading through the query interface; Goal and Funnel settings name them by name. `widgetGoals` offers the Site's Goals to Widgets' Goal filter. See "Dashboards".
- **Admin pages**: Site › Goals and Site › Funnels (`structure.edit`; screens in `apps/server/src/ui/analytics/`).
- **Settings**: `settings.get` and `settings.update` (`structure.edit`, Staff): the Site's excluded address ranges (addresses or CIDR blocks, at most 50), logged `site-analytics.settings.update`.
- **Countries** come from the countries port: `WEBCRAFT_COUNTRY_FILE`, a list of address ranges an agency downloads (no list ships with WebCraft); without one every country is unknown.

## Dashboards

Grids of Widgets showing a Site's analytics, or a Workspace's Sites added together (spec story 131, `GLOSSARY.md`). Dashboards are WebCraft's (core: `packages/core/src/dashboards/`, `services.dashboards`; tables `dashboards`, a Site table, `workspace_dashboards` and `dashboard_templates`, Workspace tables; procedures `dashboards.*` and `dashboardTemplates.*` in `packages/api/src/procedures/dashboards.ts`); the Widgets on them come from the catalog Modules add (SDK 1.8, so far Site analytics') and WebCraft's own note. Core keeps them because only core reaches across a Workspace's Sites (Workspace Dashboards) and across Workspaces (templates), while each Widget counts through its Module on one Site at a time, so ADR 0024's boundary holds: no Module reads another place, and no Module code runs in the admin. Their tests are in `tests/dashboards/`.

- **A place**: every procedure names a Site (`siteId`) or, for a Workspace Dashboard, a Workspace (`workspaceId`), exactly one (`BAD_REQUEST` otherwise). A Workspace Dashboard's Widgets add up the Workspace's Sites the person sees (`sites.counted`/`of` in each answer): counts add, a rate's two counts add, tables add by row key then keep the biggest (each Site answers up to 1,000 rows first), Funnels add step by step, and visitors are added Site by Site and day by day, as Site analytics counts them (the screen says so).
- **Who**: `dashboard.manage` builds (Staff), `dashboard.view` sees all (Staff and Owners); a Dashboard Staff show to everyone (`sharedWithEveryone`) is seen by everyone who sees the place, and exported by them too; any other answers `DASHBOARD_NOT_FOUND` to them. API tokens read and export like their person (a read-only token never changes one).
- **A Dashboard**: a name, a range (`last30Days` by default; `today`, `yesterday`, `last7Days`, `last30Days`, `last90Days`, `thisMonth`, `lastMonth`, `last12Months`, or `custom` days, both counted, at most 3,660; UTC days, by the Install's clock; two days or less count by hour), and its Widgets, kept as JSON with it (`StoredWidget`), at most 40, read and written whole under a row lock, so two people moving Widgets never lose a move.
- **Widgets**: `kind` (`note`, or `<module id>:<kind>`), `title` (null: the kind's name), a place and size on a 12-column grid (2 to 12 cells wide and tall), `range` (null: the Dashboard's), `filters` (those the kind takes), `settings` (the kind's choices; fixed options are checked when saved, options worked out per Site, such as Goals and Funnels by name, when counted) and `rows` (5, 10, 20 or 50, for tables). `widgets.add` puts a new one in the first free place at its shape's size; `widgets.move` moves and resizes one and moves down only what it would cover (`placeWidget`); a refused kind, filter, setting or range is `DASHBOARD_INVALID` with `data.problem`.
- **Data** (`widgets.data`): worked out when asked, one Widget at a time, for the Widget's own range, else the `range` given (exploring, which changes nothing), else the Dashboard's: the Module's `query` runs as the Module acting by itself on the Site (`ModuleHost.runWidget`), after the person's access was checked. Counts come as counts, a rate as two (`{ count, of }`), labels in the person's Admin language. A Widget that can't count answers its `problem` instead: the Module's own refusal (`GOAL_NOT_FOUND`, `FUNNEL_NOT_FOUND`, `WIDGET_SETTING_MISSING`), `MODULE_NOT_RUNNING`, `WIDGET_UNKNOWN`, or `WIDGET_FAILED` (written to the Module log).
- **CSV exports** (`widgets.export`, `export`, reads): UTF-8 with a byte order mark, every cell quoted, CRLF, a cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return made text with a `'` (`csvCell`); each Widget headed by its title and its days (and the Sites counted, and where counts start, when they say so), rates as percentages, sources, countries and devices in words; a Dashboard's file holds each Widget in turn. Logged `dashboard.export`.
- **Dashboard templates** (`saveAsTemplate`, `dashboardTemplates.list`, `.delete`): a Dashboard's Widgets and range kept in its Workspace (at most 50 a Workspace), offered by New Dashboard on any Site or Workspace of a Workspace where the person builds Dashboards. Widgets name Goals and Funnels by name, so a template finds the Goals of the same names where it is used; `create` answers `unmatched`, the names the place lacks, whose Widgets say so until they are added.
- **The admin**: Site › Dashboards and Workspace › Dashboards (`ui/dashboards/`; the sidebar offers them where the person sees a Dashboard, `place.dashboards`), a Dashboard at `…/dashboards/<id>` with the days explored in `?range=`. Every chart has the same numbers as a table, and lines differ by dash and marker as well as colour. Staff move a Widget by dragging its handle (or resize by its corner), or from the keyboard on the handle: Alt with the arrows moves it, Alt and Shift resize it, Space picks it up so the arrows move it (Shift resizes), Space puts it down and Escape puts it back; every change is said in the screen's live region and saved in order.
- **Seam for Reports and Share links (#104)**: `services.dashboards.widgetAnswers(access, place, dashboardId, { range, language })` works out every Widget of a Dashboard as plain data, the read-only rendering both start from; a Share link will be one more way to be allowed to see a Dashboard.

## Media

A Site's Media library (`packages/core/src/media/`, behind `services.media` and `services.storage`; ADRs 0026, 0027). Its tables (`media_folders`, `media`, `media_files`) are Site tables; `media_quotas` is a Workspace table; `install_storage` and `install_settings.media_max_file_bytes` hold the Install settings.

### Storage

- **One interface, two kinds** (`media/storage.ts`): `put`, `read`, `stream(key, range?)`, `delete`. **Local disk** keeps files under `WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR` (written beside the final name, then renamed; each read opens and closes the file, so a visitor who goes away never keeps one open). An **S3-compatible bucket** (`media/s3-storage.ts`: AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO, …) is spoken to through `services.http`, every request signed with AWS Signature Version 4 (`aws4fetch`) by `services.clock`'s time, with a SHA-256 of the body. It goes to the endpoint the Superadmin set, not through the guarded fetcher: a self-hosted store is usually on an internal address.
- **Keys** have one folder per Workspace and Site: `<workspace id>/<site id>/media/<name>` for files visitors get, `<workspace id>/<site id>/uploads/<name>` for raw uploads the worker hasn't cleaned (never served), `<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>.csv` for CSV files uploaded for an Import and `<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>/part-000001`, `…-000002`, … for the parts of a Module source's file (private, never served, see "Imports"); Form uploads will get `…/form-uploads/…`. A bucket's `prefix` goes in front, so one bucket can hold several Installs. A file served as uploaded is named by its content hash, so identical bytes uploaded twice under the same name share one stored file (two `media_files` rows, one key); a stored file is removed only once no row names its key (`removeUnusedFiles`, `media/trash.ts`).
- **The Install setting** (Install settings › Storage, `install.storage.get` / `update`): local disk (the default) or a bucket. Changing it is a Sensitive action (`storage.change`): the bucket's secret access key is the Install secret `storage.s3.secretAccessKey`, write-only like every secret; leaving it out keeps the stored one. A bucket is checked before it is saved: WebCraft writes, reads back and removes `webcraft-check/<random>`, refusing the change with `STORAGE_UNREACHABLE` when it can't. Recorded as `storage.change`, never with the secret.
- **Each stored file records which storage holds it** (`media_files.storage`), and is always read from there: switching storage changes only where new files go, and never breaks a URL. The S3 settings are kept when the Install switches back to disk, so files in the bucket stay readable. Moving files between them is `webcraft admin media move` (later).

### Limits and quotas

- **The largest file** is an Install setting (Install settings › Storage, `install.media.get` / `update`, `install.manage`): 50 MB unless the Superadmin sets another, from 1 MB to 1 GB (`MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES`), logged as `mediaMaxFileSize.change`. Uploads, replacements and imports over it are refused (`MEDIA_TOO_LARGE`, 413, `data.maxBytes`). Before the procedure reads a multipart body into memory, `/api/rpc` refuses one whose `Content-Length` passes the limit (plus 64 KiB for the form's other parts) with the same answer, and stops reading one that doesn't say its length once it passes it (`bodyWithinLimit`, `packages/api/src/rpc.ts`).
- **A Workspace's quota** (`media_quotas`; `workspaces.storage` with `workspace.view`, `workspaces.setQuota` with `quota.manage`, at least 1 MB, null removes it; logged as `storageQuota.change`) is optional. Usage is counted from stored bytes (`workspaceMediaBytes`): every stored file of the Workspace's Sites (originals, variants, uploads waiting for the worker, files in the Trash, replaced files still served), a key shared by several items counted once. An upload, replacement or import that would take usage past the quota is refused with `MEDIA_QUOTA_FULL` (413, `data: { usedBytes, quotaBytes }`), checked before the file is stored and again, with the quota row locked, in the transaction that records it, so two uploads at once can't both squeeze past. Uploads and quota changes also take turns on a per-Workspace advisory lock (`lockQuota`: uploads hold it shared, a quota change alone), since a Workspace with no quota yet has no row to lock. Once usage passes 80% (`QUOTA_WARNING_SHARE`), the Workspace's Owners and the Staff assigned to it get the `media-quota-warning` alert email once (`noticeQuotaUse`); it is sent again only after usage went back under 80%. `media.overview` and the Workspace page show `{ usedBytes, quotaBytes, warning, full }`; the Media library and the Workspace page show the notice.

### Uploads and the worker

- **Uploading** (`media.upload`, a File in the oRPC input). What a file is comes from its first bytes (`media/sniff.ts`), never its name or the type the browser claims: JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF, HEIC/HEIF, GIF and SVG images, PDF, MP4/MOV/WebM video, MP3/M4A/OGG/WAV/FLAC audio; anything else is `MEDIA_TYPE_REFUSED` (`reason: "unknown"`).
- **Import from URL** (`media.importUrl`, `media.upload`): fetches the file at an address through the guarded fetcher (see "Outgoing calls"), allowed any host on the public internet (`allowedHosts: "any"`), pinned to the address checked, at most 3 redirects each checked again, 30 seconds (`MEDIA_IMPORT_TIMEOUT_MS`) and the Install's largest file, then takes it exactly as an upload, named by the server's `Content-Disposition` or the last part of the address it finally came from. Refusals are `MEDIA_IMPORT_REFUSED` with `data.problem` (`scheme`, `host`, `address`, `redirects`, `timeout`, `size`, `unreachable`, or `status` with `data.status` for an error answer). Logged as `media.import` with the host (never the path: an address can carry a token).
- **Images are cleaned before anyone sees them**: they may carry GPS data. The upload is stored apart (`uploads/`), the item is `processing`, and the `media.process` job runs in the worker: it strips every piece of metadata (EXIF with GPS and camera data, XMP, IPTC), turns the pixels upright by the EXIF orientation, converts to sRGB, converts HEIC to JPEG (other formats keep theirs), stores the result as the **original** visitors get, deletes the raw upload, marks the item `ready`, clears the Site's page cache and queues `media.variants`. A file that can't be read as an image is `failed` (`failure: { reason: "unreadable" }`) and never served.
- **SVG files** go through the same job, cleaned instead of resized (`images.cleanSvg`, `apps/worker/src/svg.ts`): read as XML with jsdom, left without what only drawing programs read (comments, processing instructions, `<metadata>`, editors' own namespaces), then passed through DOMPurify (its SVG and SVG-filter profiles, plus `<use>` within the file). A file is refused rather than changed (`failed`, `failure: { reason: "svgUnsafe", found }`) when it holds a script, event handlers, `foreignObject`, anything outside the SVG namespace, outside references (an `href` that isn't `#id` or an inline PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP, a `url()` that isn't `url(#id)` in an attribute, a `style` or a style sheet, `@import`, `image-set()`, an `xml-stylesheet` instruction), entities (any document type with declarations) or anything else DOMPurify would remove; one that isn't well-formed SVG is `svgInvalid`. SVGs are never resized (no variants); their size comes from their `width`/`height` in pixels, else their `viewBox`.
- **Variants** (`media.variants`): every width of `VARIANT_WIDTHS` (320, 640, 960, 1280, 1920, 2560) up to the original's (equal counts), each in AVIF and WebP, stored together in one transaction, then the page cache is cleared. Until then pages show the original alone. Adding a width later means running the job over the library again.
- GIFs (kept animated), documents, video and audio are stored as uploaded and `ready` at once.
- **Image processing is a port** (`InstallPorts.images`, `media/images.ts`): `prepare` (the cleaned original), `variant` (one width) and `cleanSvg`. Only the worker hands in the real one (`apps/worker/src/images.ts`: sharp on libvips, libheif in WebAssembly for HEIC, which sharp's own libvips doesn't decode, and DOMPurify on jsdom for SVG); the web process and server commands get `imagesOnlyInWorker`, so the Bun web process never loads them (ADR 0027). The test harness uses the real one, since it drains the worker's jobs.

### Serving

`/_media/<name>` on the Site's own domain (its Staging address for now) answers the Site's originals and variants by name, never a raw upload, never a file in the Trash, and never another Site's file. A name is the first 16 hexadecimal characters of the file's SHA-256 and a readable name: `3f9a1c2b7d4e5f60-sourdough-loaf.jpg`, `…-sourdough-loaf-640.avif`. So a name always holds the same bytes: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, a strong `ETag` (the hash; `If-None-Match` gets 304), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the stored `Content-Type`, `Accept-Ranges: bytes` with single byte ranges (206, 416), and `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on a Staging address. Media files get `MEDIA_POLICY`, SVGs `SVG_POLICY` (`default-src 'none'; script-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; font-src data:; sandbox; …`), so an SVG opened by itself runs and loads nothing whatever the cleaning missed (`mediaPolicy(contentType)`). The page cache never keeps them. The admin shows the same files at `/api/media/<site id>/<name>` on the admin host, only to signed-in people who reach the Site (`private` caching, the same policies), files in the Trash too, so previews work whether or not the Site has an address.

### The library

- `media.list` (newest first; a folder, `none` for files in no folder; a search over file name, title and alt text in any language; a kind; `trash` for the files in the Trash, most recently deleted first; `limit`, `offset`), `media.get`, `media.update`, `media.overview` (the largest file, the Workspace's storage, how many files are in the Trash, whether alt text can be suggested) and `media.folders.list`, `create`, `rename`, `delete`. Folder names are unique on the Site whatever their case; deleting a folder keeps its files, in no folder.
- **Title, alt text and caption** are stored per language like Entry values (`{ values: { alt: { en: { value, by } } } }`, ADR 0003), in any of the Site's languages (`language` on `media.get`, `list` and `update`), falling back to the Default language one text at a time. They change at once, with no Draft, and clear the page cache: published pages show new alt text immediately.
- **The Focal point** (`focalPoint: { x, y }` on `media.update`, whole percentages of the image's width and height from its top left; null clears it; images only, else `MEDIA_INVALID` at `focalPoint`) is set once per image and shared by every language. It changes at once and clears the page cache (see "Media on pages").
- **Used in** (`media.usage`, `media/usage.ts`): every place a file is used on its Site, each with the versions using it (`draft`, `published`): Entries (a media Field, a Body image Block, a Component's Prop on them, in any language, with the Fields by key), the Globals, Templates and Components (`media:<id>` written in them), Loops (their Loop items), and the Design system (its CSS names the file's address). Each place is found by the item's id (or, for CSS, its served names) in the stored versions, so a new kind of Field or Block needs nothing here; a feature that keeps references in a table of its own adds a `MediaUseSource` (the Site's SEO settings, `seoSettings`: its default image and logo). Forms hold no Media.
- **Deleting** (`media.delete`) takes two steps (see "Two-step confirmation"): the first answers `{ trashed: false, usedIn, confirmation }`, the admin's warning; the second puts the file in the **Trash** (`media.trashed_at`, logged `media.trash`). From then on it isn't served, pages stop showing it (the page cache is cleared), it can't be chosen for a media Field (`ENTRY_INVALID`; Entries already pointing at it show the `media` rule in their `problems`), and other changes answer `MEDIA_IN_TRASH`. `media.restore` puts it back as it was (`media.restore`); `media.deleteForever` (two steps, files in the Trash only, else `MEDIA_NOT_IN_TRASH`) and `media.emptyTrash` (two steps, `media.manage`; the first answers how many) delete files for good (`media.deleteForever`, `mediaTrash.empty`). The daily `media.purge` (03:47 UTC) deletes files 30 days after they went to the Trash (`TRASH_LIFETIME_MS`); a stored file goes only once no other item shares its bytes.
- **Replace file** (`media.replace`) gives an item a new file of the same kind (an image for an image; `MEDIA_REPLACE_REFUSED`, `reason: "kind"`), keeping its id, texts, Focal point and every use; logged as `media.replace`. A document is swapped at once; an image waits for the worker (`replacing: true`) while the current file is served, then is swapped and the page cache cleared. The files it replaced (`media_files.retired_at`) are still served under their names for 30 days, for pages loaded before, then `media.purge` deletes them. A replacement the worker refuses leaves the item as it was, with `failure: { reason, replacement: true }`.
- **Suggest alt text** (`media.suggestAltText`): an AI provider describes an image in a language, with the Workspace's AI key (the Workspace secret `ai.key`, `WORKSPACE_AI_KEY`), and nothing is saved. Without a key `media.overview` answers `altTextSuggestions: false` (the admin hides the button) and the procedure answers `AI_KEY_MISSING` (409); a document is `ALT_TEXT_UNAVAILABLE` (`reason: "kind"`), an image with no file providers read (an SVG) `reason: "format"`; a provider that fails `AI_PROVIDER_FAILED` (424, `data.problem`). The image sent is the smallest WebP width of at least 640 pixels (else the original as a JPEG, PNG, WebP or GIF). Providers are a port (`InstallPorts.ai`, `packages/core/src/ai.ts`, ADR 0011: called directly): AI keys and providers (#81) fill it in; until then every process gets `aiProvidersToCome`, which refuses every call as unavailable.
- **Who changes what**: everyone who reaches the Site sees the library, uploads to it and imports into it; Authors change, replace, delete and restore only their own uploads (`FORBIDDEN` otherwise), Editors, Owners and Staff every file, the folders and the Trash. Each item says `canEdit` for the person asking.

### Media on pages

An `<img>` whose `src` reads `media:<id>`, written as fixed text in a Template or read from a media Field (Templates read a media Field as `media:<id>`), renders as a Media image (`packages/renderer/src/media.ts`):

```html
<picture>
  <source type="image/avif" srcset="/_media/…-320.avif 320w, /_media/…-640.avif 640w" sizes="(min-width: 800px) 800px, 100vw">
  <source type="image/webp" srcset="…" sizes="…">
  <img src="/_media/…-croissants.jpg" alt="…" width="800" height="400" loading="lazy">
</picture>
```

- `alt` is the Template's own when it gives one (`alt=""` makes it Decorative), else the Media library's in the page's language, else empty. `width` and `height` are the original's, or the Template's (one of them scales the other). `sizes` is the Template's, or made from the width shown. The Template's other attributes and Classes stay on the `<img>`. An SVG has no variants: its `<img>` comes alone.
- **The Focal point** places the image through a fixed set of WebCraft's own classes, after the Template's: `wc-focus wc-fx-<n> wc-fy-<n>`, to the nearest 5% (`FOCAL_POINT_STEP`, `focalPointClasses`). Every Site stylesheet holds their rules in its base layer (`focalPointRules`: `.wc-focus{object-position:var(--wc-fx,50%) var(--wc-fy,50%)}` and one custom property per step), so pages keep their strict policy (no inline styles, ADR 0023) and a Class Staff give the image, in a later layer, can still position it. It matters where a design crops the image (`object-fit: cover`).
- The first Media image of the page's content (inside the Site template's `<main>`, at its outlet) loads with `loading="eager" fetchpriority="high"`; every other one, the Site template's own included, `loading="lazy"`. A Template's own `loading` or `fetchpriority` wins, and a first image the Template makes lazy gets no high priority.
- Before variants exist, the `<img>` comes alone; before the original is cleaned, for a file in the Trash, or for an id that isn't one of the Site's ready images, nothing renders. In any other URL attribute (`href`, a video's `src`), `media:<id>` becomes the file's address.
- Template markup writes it as it is stored, `<img src="media:<id>">`: the parser checks the id's form only (a file can be deleted later, so it isn't looked up), and `{{ photo }}` in an `src` reads a media Field the same way.

## Imports

Imports (GLOSSARY.md › Importing; `packages/core/src/imports/`, behind `services.imports`; tables `imports`, `import_files`, `import_runs`, `import_items`, `import_run_changes`, `import_run_problems`; procedures `imports.*`; the admin's Site › Imports and Import CSV on a Post Type's Entries) bring outside content into one Site as Drafts, through one pipeline whatever the source: a **source** reads items as a stream, a **mapping** turns them into Entries, an **Import run** writes them as Drafts with their **Source id**, and its **Import report** records what happened. CSV files are WebCraft's own source; Modules add theirs through the SDK (SDK 1.7, "Import sources" below): the WordPress import (`modules/wordpress-import`) reads a WordPress export file.

- **The seam** (`imports/items.ts`, `imports/pipeline.ts`): a source turns what it reads into `EntryItem`s (a position, a Source id, the value it matches Entries by, values as saving a Draft takes them (SEO fields and a Page's parent included), what must be found first, problems) each for an `ItemDestination` (a Post Type, a language, what items match by; a CSV file gives every row the same one, a Module source's items each their type's). The pipeline then decides where each goes (`decide`), finds what its values point at (`resolveFinds`: Entries by slug or title, or by the Source id this Import gave them; Media files by id; files to fetch by address, with the texts the source gives them) and writes it (`writeItem`), whatever the source. A **Source id is looked up within the Post Type its item goes to** (`import_items.post_type_id`, `itemKey`): an item whose type a later run sends to another Post Type comes in anew there, and the Entry it made before stays where it is. A source that brings Media items fetches them with `fetchFile` (Import from URL, recorded as the run's, so Undo puts them in the Trash, and kept by address so a later run reuses them, once per address per Import; a fetched file gets the source's title, alt text and caption where it has none). Redirect rules from a run belong to the Redirects Module, which the run reaches through the SDK (`redirectRules.add`), recorded among the run's changes (`import_run_changes.kind` `redirect`, with the rule's id, path and target) so Undo removes them; Import leftovers are problems with the rule `leftover` (their text, the markup they were and their Block), which the report lists apart.
- **Imports** (`imports.create`, `update`, `list`, `get`): a name, a source (`csv`, or a Module's import source such as `wordpress-import.export`) and its settings, checked against the Site (`IMPORT_INVALID`, each problem at its place with its rule; settings of another source's kind are `settingsKind`). An Import counts its runs, so run numbers keep rising after reports are purged, and the Entries it keeps track of (`entries`: those it brought in that still exist). A CSV Import that keeps track of any keeps its Post Type (`IMPORT_POST_TYPE_FIXED`, 409; undo its runs, or start a new Import), and the admin shows the Post Type fixed; Source ids are looked up within each item's Post Type either way, so a Module source's Import, whose types go into several Post Types, maps them anew freely.
- **CSV files** (`imports.uploadCsv`, `imports/csv.ts`): read whole when uploaded, then kept privately (`<workspace id>/<site id>/imports/<id>.csv`: never in the Media library, never served, like a Form upload) for the person who uploaded it, until a run takes it (the run deletes it when it ends) or a day passes (`imports.purge`). UTF-8 only, with or without a byte order mark (UTF-16, UTF-32 and bytes that aren't UTF-8 are refused, never guessed); a comma, semicolon or tab, whichever the header holds most of; quoted fields with separators, `""` and line breaks; `\n`, `\r\n` or `\r` line ends; empty lines skipped. At most 10 MB (refused by `/api/rpc` before it is read, `OWN_UPLOAD_LIMITS` in `packages/api/src/rpc.ts`), 10,000 rows, 200 columns and 100,000 characters a field (`CSV_LIMITS`); the header names every column once. Refusals are `IMPORT_FILE_REFUSED` with `data.reason` (`size`, `encoding`, `empty`, `header`, `quote`, `cell`, `rows`, `columns`) and what fills in its wording. Rows are numbered as a spreadsheet numbers them: the header is row 1.
- **CSV settings** (`imports/csv-source.ts`, `csvSettingsSchema`): one Post Type, a language (null: the Default language), what rows match existing Entries by (`title`, `slug`, or a top-level text, email or number Field) and where each column goes (`title`, `slug`, `fields.<key>`, `fields.<group>.<key>` inside Groups; never a Repeater) or `null` to skip it. Columns are matched by name, whatever their case, so Run again with a new file keeps the mapping; file columns the settings don't map are skipped and listed (`skippedColumns`).
- **Reading a cell** goes through its Field type (`fromText` in `FIELD_TYPE_SPECS`): text, email and rich text as written (rich text is HTML, cleaned like any rich text); numbers with a dot or a comma as the decimal mark and no thousands separators (`5.50`, `5,50`); true/false as `true`/`false`, `yes`/`no`, `1`/`0`, `y`/`n`, `on`/`off` and the Italian words; dates in their stored forms, a moment without an offset (`2026-03-02 09:30`) read as UTC; a choice by its value or label in any case; a link as its address; a media Field as an `https` address (fetched into the Media library through Import from URL and the guarded fetcher, once per address per Import) or the id of a file already there; a Relationship by slug, else title, in any language and case, among its Post Types' Entries (as they are when the row is written); a Body as plain paragraphs (a blank line starts one, a single line break stays one, nothing is read as markup). Several choices or Entries are separated by semicolons, or by commas when there is no semicolon. **Empty cells change nothing**: a new Entry keeps the Field's default, an updated one its value.
- **Problems**: a cell its Field can't take is left out and listed at its row and column with its rule (`number`, `boolean`, `date`, `choice`, `mediaAddress`, `mediaNotFound`, `entryNotFound`, `entryAmbiguous`, `notFetched` with the fetch's `problem`, `tooLong`, `slug`, `link`); the rest of the row is imported. A **problem row** isn't imported at all: no value to match by (`matchMissing`), the same value as an earlier row (`matchedTwice`), matching several Entries (`matchesSeveral`), more cells than columns (`columnCount`), a slug another Entry has (`slugTaken`), or, in another language, no Entry to translate (`translationWithoutEntry`). Values that break a Field's rules are kept, as in any Draft, and show in the Entry's `problems`.
- **Matching and Source ids**: a row's Source id is its matching value (`fields.sku:pk-700`; text compared without case). A row goes to the Entry this Import brought in with that Source id (`import_items`, kept as long as the Import and the Entry), else to the one Entry of the Post Type whose Draft has that value (in the run's language, falling back to the Default language), else it creates one. An Entry this Import brought in that someone **edited** since it last wrote it is skipped and reported (`skipped`, `reason: "edited"`), never overwritten; Entries made by hand that a row matches are updated, since the person chose to match them. "Edited" is any change to the Draft the run left (its hash and when it was saved): any Draft save by a person, even one changing nothing, or a later run. Publishing isn't an edit: Run again updates a published Entry's Draft (it shows `changed`; visitors keep the published version).
- **Language**: values are written in the Import's language. Translations come in by running a file in that language matched by the same column: Translatable fields get its values, shared Fields (one value for every language) are written too, so map only what is translated, and a row matching no Entry isn't imported.
- **Preview** (`imports.preview`): the same pipeline without writing or fetching anything: how many rows create, update or skip Drafts, how many have problems, the files it would fetch, the first 200 problems. With `importId`, rows match what that Import brought in (Run again).
- **Runs** (`imports.start`, `imports.stop`, `imports.report`): starting a run (logged `import.run`) queues `imports.run`, which runs in the worker as the person who started it (`actors.person`, their access checked again; a run whose person lost `import.run` fails with `failure.reason: "access"`). It goes through the rows from where it got to, 500 per job, each row's write in its own transaction, counting `done` of `total` (rows, not lines), so a big file is gone through by several jobs and a restarted worker takes up where it stopped. One run of an Import at a time (`IMPORT_RUNNING`). **Stop** (logged `import.stop`) ends a waiting run at once; a running one finishes the row it is on, keeps what's done and ends `stopped`. A run ends `done`, `stopped` or `failed` (`access`, `settings`, `file`, `error`), and its file is deleted.
- **The Import report** (`imports.report`): status, progress, counts (`created`, `updated`, `unchanged`, `skipped`, `problemRows`), skipped columns, problems by row and column with their rules, the first 200 Entries created, updated and skipped, files fetched, Import leftovers, and whether Undo is available (`undo.available`, `undo.reason`). It is kept 90 days after the run ends (`expiresAt`), then `imports.purge` deletes the run, its changes and its problems; the Import and its Source ids stay.
- **Undo** (`imports.undo`, two steps through `askConfirmation`; logged `import.undo`): deletes the Drafts the run created (removing links other Entries make to them, as deleting an Entry does), puts back the Drafts it updated exactly as they were (`replaceDraft`), and moves the files it fetched to the Trash unless something outside the run uses them (Used in).
