Refused with `IMPORT_UNDO_REFUSED` (`data.reason`, `data.entries`): `status` (the run hasn't ended, or was undone), `published` (something it brought in was published since), `edited` (edited since, as above; a later run counts, so undo the later run first) or `linked` (another Entry must point at one it created, through a required Relationship; `removalBlocks` in `content/entries.ts` works it out without changing anything). Every refusal comes at the first step already, and in the report's `undo.reason`. After Undo, the Import runs again as if the run had never been.
- **A Module source's file** (`imports/uploads.ts`) comes in parts, never in one body: `imports.startUpload` (its source, name and size, at most what the source takes: 1 GiB, `IMPORT_FILE_MAX_BYTES`) answers the file's id and `partBytes` (8 MiB, or a smaller size asked for, 1 KiB at least, 10,000 parts at most); `imports.uploadPart` takes each part at its `offset` (a multiple of `partBytes`) with the SHA-256 of its bytes, refusing a part that doesn't have that hash (`IMPORT_UPLOAD_REFUSED`, `reason: "hash"`: send it again), one at another offset (`offset`, with `receivedBytes`: where to go on) or of the wrong size (`part`), and taking a part sent again as it was; `imports.finishUpload` checks every byte came and that the SHA-256 of the parts' hashes (each part's 32 bytes, in order) is the one sent (`hash`). So an upload that stopped goes on where it got to (`imports.file` answers `receivedBytes`). `/api/rpc` takes a part of at most `partBytes` (`OWN_UPLOAD_LIMITS`); its other body limits are unchanged. Each part is its own private object (see "Storage"), read back as one stream; the file is deleted when the run that took it ends, or a day after it was last used (`imports.purge`).
- **The survey** (`imports.survey`, `imports/survey.ts`): once a file is whole, the worker reads it through its Module, as a stream, and keeps what it holds on the file (`import_files.details`): each type (its key, name, whether it holds Entries or files, how many items), each type's keys (their kind of value, `title`, `slug`, `text`, `html`, `date`, `file` or `items`, how many items have them, and a value from one), and each type's first 20 items as samples. A file the Module refuses is `refused` with its code (the WordPress import's: `doctype`, `notExport`, `xml`, `itemTooLarge`, `valueTooLarge`; WebCraft's own `encoding` for bytes that aren't UTF-8). `imports.file` answers the survey with `suggested`, the mapping the Map step starts from (`suggestedSettings`, by generic patterns only: names that match, each value's kind, `seo`/`meta` with `title` or `desc` in a key's name for the SEO fields, keys starting with `_` skipped).
- **A Module source's settings** (`imports/source-settings.ts`, `sourceSettingsSchema`): a language, and for each type where it goes (`{ postTypeId }`, `{ newPostType: { name } }`, `"media"` for files, or `null` to skip it) and where each key goes (`title`, `slug`, `seo.title`, `seo.description`, `seo.canonical`, `seo.noindex`, `seo.image`, `fields.<key>`, `{ newField: { key, label, type, to? } }`, or `null`). A key's kind of value must suit where it goes (`takes`, `@webcraft/core/import-rules`, which the admin reads too); Pages take no Body field (a new rich text Field takes their content). Types and keys the settings send nowhere are skipped and listed in the report.
- **A Module source's run** (`imports/source-runs.ts`) first makes the Structure its settings ask for, once: the Post Types it creates (`/<key>/{slug}`, with their new Fields; a new Relationship's Post Type first) and the Fields it adds to Post Types the Site has, each recorded among its changes, then written back into the Import's settings (`{ postTypeId }`, `fields.<key>`) so Run again finds them. Then it goes through the items from where it got to (2,000 a job, each job reading the file again from the start): each type's items become Drafts as above; a body's HTML becomes core Blocks (`blocksFromHtml`, `packages/renderer/src/body/from-html.ts`, with parse5: scripts, styles, event handlers and every attribute left behind; a shortcode, page-builder layout (containers holding containers) or an embed with no Block becomes an Import leftover, kept as a plain paragraph where it stood); each image becomes an image Block pointing at its file once fetched; rich text is cleaned as rich text always is; a source's slug is kept (`-2`, `-3`… when taken, said in the report). Where an item's path changed, and for each file whose address was on the old site, it adds a Redirect rule (from the old path to the Draft's path once published, or to the Media file, `media:<id>`), through the Redirects Module when it runs on the Site; the rules step aside where a page answers (they never hide a live page) and never take a path that has a rule.
- **The Preview of a Module source** (`imports.previewSource`, the `imports.preview` job) is worked out in the worker over the whole file, without writing or fetching anything, and kept on the file for its settings (a new mapping asks again): `working`, then the Entries each type would create, update or skip, the new Post Types, the files it would fetch, the Redirect rules it would make (none, with `redirectsOn: false`, where the Redirects Module is off), its leftovers and problems, and the first 20 Entries of each type with their Blocks, leftovers and rule. **The live sample** (`imports.sample`) reads one of a type's samples with the settings as they are, at once.
- **The report of a Module source's run** adds `leftovers` (with the Entry and Post Type each is in), `notFetched`, `redirects` (`on`, `count`, `rules`), `structure` (the Post Types and Fields it made), `skippedTypes`, `skippedKeys` and `skippedItems`; **Undo** also removes the Redirect rules it added (each while still as added) and deletes the Post Types it made once they hold no Entry and nothing else names them (a Template, a Relationship of a Post Type that stays), putting the Import's settings back as they were; Fields it added to a Post Type the Site had stay.
- **The admin** (`apps/server/src/ui/imports/`): Site › Imports (Content, `import.run`) lists the Imports with their last runs and the ways to start one (a Module's sources for those who may use them); New Import and Run again go through Source, Map and Preview (`ImportFlow`, which offers the sources: `CsvImportFlow` for a CSV file, `SourceImportFlow` (`SourceFlow.tsx`) for a Module's: the upload in parts with its progress, the survey, Map variant B (the types, each key's card, the live sample Entry) and the Preview's real Entries), then the run's page shows progress with Stop (polled every 2 seconds) and becomes its Import report with Undo and Run again (`RunReportPage`). Import CSV on a Post Type's Entries opens the same mapping and preview in a side sheet (`CsvImportSheet`).

## Template markup

Template markup (`packages/renderer/src/markup/`, ADR 0017) is the one written form of element trees, for Code mode, the builder's code view, files, MCP, the CLI and imports. It is never stored: `parseTemplateMarkup(markup, options)` reads it into a canonical tree and `printTemplateMarkup(tree, options)` writes a tree as markup. Printing then parsing gives the same tree, for every setting (`tests/markup/round-trip.test.ts` checks it by hand and over generated trees).

```html
<article class="card" aria-label="Post" data-wc-id="Xb3kP9a2Lm4Q">
  <h1 data-wc-id="Q9zT2b7Rk1Wd">{{ title | truncate: 80 }}</h1>
  <!-- Comments stay in the markup and the tree; visitors never get them. -->
  {{@if summary and status != "draft"}}<p data-wc-id="r5Hs8Kq0Zx3N">{{ summary }}</p>
  {{@else if author}}<p data-wc-id="m2Lp7Vd4Ty9C">By {{ author }}</p>
  {{@else}}<p data-wc-id="w8Bn1Fj6Gu5E">Write \{{ summary }} to show the summary.</p>{{/if}}
  <time datetime="{{ publishedAt | date: datetime }}" data-wc-id="c3Xr9Ma1Pq7S">{{ publishedAt | date: long }}</time>
</article>
```

- **Element ids** are written as `data-wc-id`, always last. An element written without one gets a new id; when two elements carry the same id (a copied element), the first keeps it and the copy gets a new one, with a notice. An element's layer label is written as `data-wc-label`, just before it (no values in it). Other `data-wc-` attributes are WebCraft's.
- **Classes** are written by name in `class` (printed first) and stored by id; a name the Site doesn't have is refused. An inline `style` is read strictly (declarations only, safe `url()`s) and becomes a **generated Class** (`style-` and a hash of the canonical declarations, so the same style finds the same Class), with a notice; pages never carry inline styles. Names come from the Draft of the Design system: its Classes and its token utilities (`classCatalogue`, `packages/core/src/design-system/classes.ts`). The inline style is checked like any CSS of the Design system (no addresses on other hosts, no `!important`) and the generated Class joins the Design system's Draft; it goes live with the Template that made it (see "The Design system").
- **Values**: `{{ path }}` or `{{ path | formatter }}`, in text and inside attribute values. Formatters are a fixed set (`packages/renderer/src/formatters.ts`): `date` (`short`, `medium` (the default), `long`, `full`, `time`, `iso`, `datetime`; in the page's language, in UTC until Sites have a time zone), `number` (optionally `number: 2` decimals) and `truncate: <length>` (cut at a word, then `…`). Which paths are known depends on the kind of Template (`isKnownValue`): an Entry template reads the built-in Fields, its Post Type's Fields and their parts, and the Globals (`site.x`; see "What a binding reads"); inside a Component its Props (`props.x`) and the Globals; anywhere, the Loops of the Site's `<name>.count` and the rest of `LOOP_STATS`; inside a Loop, its items (`post.x`) and their position (`loop.x`). See "Loops in Template markup".
- **Fixed text in other languages**: `{{ "Read more" fr: "Lire la suite" pt-BR: "Leia mais" }}`, in text and inside attribute values: the text as written in double quotes, then each language's (`\"` for a quote, `\\` for a backslash, `\r` for a carriage return). It is a text node with `translations` (see "Languages"); the printer writes the languages in alphabetical order, and plain text without other languages as plain text. A language tag WebCraft doesn't keep, or one given twice, is refused where it is.
- **Escaping**: text prints `&`, `<` and `>` as references; `\{{` is a literal `{{` (the printer writes `{{` in text that way), and a `{` or `\` at the end of text prints as `&#123;` / `&#92;` so it can't run into a following `{{`. Whitespace is kept exactly: the printer adds none, so a file pulled, edited and saved shows only your changes.
- **Strict HTML**: every element needs its end tag (`<br>` and other void elements have none, `<br/>` is fine), nothing a browser would repair is accepted, and doctypes, `<script>`, `<style>`, `<meta>`, `on…` attributes and values in tag names, attribute names, `style`, `class`, `srcdoc` and `on…` are refused. A `media:` address must be `media:<id>` (see "Media on pages"). PascalCase tags place Components (see "Components"); the reserved commands (`form`, `filter`, `popup`) are refused until their tickets.
- **Rules by kind of Template**: besides `isKnownValue` and `refuseElement`, a kind of Template gives `refuseCommand` (a command it can't hold: Entry templates refuse `{{@outlet}}`) and `checkTree` (rules about the whole tree once it is read, such as the Site template's one `<main>` and one outlet). Both parsers take them and report each problem where its node is.
- **Problems and notices**: `TemplateMarkupInvalidError` lists up to 20 problems, each `{ line, column, message }`; the API answers it as `TEMPLATE_MARKUP_INVALID` with `data.problems`. A save that works answers `notices` in the same shape.
- **API**: `templates.getEntryTemplateMarkup({ siteId, postTypeId })` answers `{ template, markup: { draft, published }, version }` (all null without a template); `templates.saveEntryTemplateMarkup({ siteId, postTypeId, markup, basedOn? })` saves the Draft (creating the template) and answers `{ template, markup, version, notices }`, the markup as stored now. With `basedOn` (the version it was made from, null for none yet), a save over a Draft someone changed meanwhile is refused with `CHANGED_MEANWHILE` and the version now in `data.version`. Both need `structure.edit`; `getSiteTemplateMarkup` and `saveSiteTemplateMarkup` do the same for the Site template. In core, `services.templates.entryTemplateMarkup` and `saveEntryTemplateMarkup` (`content/templates.ts`).

### Template commands and Conditions

`{{@name …}} … {{/name}}` blocks are **Template commands**, each one entry in `TEMPLATE_COMMANDS` (`packages/renderer/src/commands.ts`): its name, its node type, whether it is a block, the branch keywords allowed inside it (`else if`, `else`; a Loop's `empty`), the `snippet` Code mode's `{{@` menu inserts (`$0` where the caret goes), and how it reads from markup, prints, is checked as a stored node, lists its children and renders. The parser and printer handle every command through the registry, so **adding a command** is: a node type in the `TreeNode` union, an entry in `TEMPLATE_COMMANDS`, and removing its name from `RESERVED_COMMANDS` (`filter`, `popup`, refused until their tickets). A command that names something of the Site (`{{@form <key>}}`) reads it through the tools the parser hands it (`FromMarkupTools.form`, `CheckTools.form`, `ToMarkupTools.formKey`), filled from the parse and print options (`forms`, `formKey`). A command whose content reads more than what is around it (a Loop's items) gives `bodyScope`, which both parsers use for its first section. A kind of Template that can't hold a command refuses it with `refuseCommand`.

`{{@outlet}}` is the content outlet: where the Site template shows each page's content. It takes nothing, holds nothing, and renders the page's content, already rendered (`RenderOptions.outlet`); only the Site template holds it, once (see "The Site template").

`{{@themeToggle}}` is the theme toggle: a button that switches the page between the Design system's light and dark values (see "The Design system"). It takes nothing, can't sit inside a link, a button or another control (`placementProblem`, which both parsers call), and renders only while the Site's published Design system has dark mode.

`{{@languageSwitcher}}` is the Language switcher: links to the page in each of the Site's languages (see "Languages"). It takes nothing, renders a `<nav>` and so can't sit inside a link, a control, a paragraph, a heading or inline text, nor directly in a list, and renders nothing on a Site with one language.

`{{@search}}` is the Search element (see "Site search"): a search box sending visitors to the Site's search results page in the page's language with a plain GET. It takes nothing, is a `<form>` of its own (so it goes where a Form can, Loops' items included), and renders nothing while the Site has no search results page there.

`{{@form <key>}}` places one of the Site's Forms (see "Forms"): written by the Form's key, stored by its id (`{ type: "form", form: <id> }`), so changing the key changes only how markup writes it. A `<form>` of its own, it can't sit inside a link, a control, a paragraph, inline text, another form, nor directly in a list; nor in a Loop's items, as a page shows a placed Form once, never once per item (`{{@empty}}`, which renders once, may hold one; one a Component brings into a Loop's items renders nothing there); a key the Site doesn't have, or a Form never published in a tree being published, is refused where it is. A Module's Component can't place one (each Site has its own Forms).

`{{@consentSettings}}` is "Cookie settings": a button that opens the Consent banner again (see "The Consent banner"). It takes nothing, can't sit inside a link, a button or another control, nor directly in a list, and renders (hidden until the consent script shows it) only where the Site asks with a banner of WebCraft's, never in Edit mode; a page showing it carries the banner.

`{{@if}}` / `{{@else if}}` / `{{@else}}` is a Condition. Its tests are stored as data (`Expression` in `packages/renderer/src/expressions.ts`) and run by WebCraft's own interpreter, never `eval`:

- **empty**: a value on its own passes when it isn't empty (blank text, false, empty rich text, a body without content, an empty list are empty; 0 is a value); `not summary` tests that it is empty;
- **equals**: `==` and `!=` (a number as a number, true/false as yes or no, otherwise as text; empty equals `""`);
- **compare**: `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`: dates as dates (a date, or text written `2026-03-02…`), numbers as numbers, other text alphabetically; an empty side, a list, or a date against a number never passes;
- **contains**: a list holding a value (`tags contains "vegan"`), or text holding text whatever its case;
- **language**: `site.language`, the page's language (`site.language == "fr"`);
- **date window**: `now`, the time the page is rendered, compared with a date (`now >= "2026-12-01" and now < "2027-01-01"`); `now` alone or with `contains` is refused, and a page reading it is kept in the page cache only until the next date it compares with;
- **Loop position**: `loop.first`, `loop.last`, `loop.odd`, `loop.even`, `loop.index` (from 1) and `loop.count`, the innermost Loop's;
- **and/or/not** and brackets, with literals `"text"`, numbers, `true` and `false`.

`now` and `contains` are words of their own, so no Field can be named after them. A test nests at most 32 deep; markup is read by the same bounded parser.

`{{@loop}}`, `{{@empty}}` and `{{@pagination}}` are described in "Loops in Template markup".

### Rich text

`cleanRichText(html)` (`packages/renderer/src/rich-text.ts`) parses with parse5 (the HTML standard's algorithm, in a `<div>`'s context, as a browser would) and writes back only the allowlist: `p`, `br`, `strong`, `b`, `em`, `i`, `u`, `s`, `sub`, `sup`, `code`, `mark`, `small`, `ul`, `ol`, `li`, `blockquote`, and `a` with `href` (safe schemes only) and `title`. Scripts, styles, embeds, SVG, MathML, forms and comments go with their content; other elements keep their text. Nothing is matched with regular expressions, and the output is the cleaner's own, so cleaning clean rich text changes nothing. The rich text Field type (`content/field-types.ts`) cleans values on save (rich text with no text left is empty; at most 20,000 characters of cleaned HTML), and the renderer cleans them again on render.

### What a binding reads

`content/render-values.ts` works out what a binding's path names on a page (`entryParts`, `resolvePath`), and which paths a template may hold (`knownValues`, checked on save and on publish):

- a built-in Field or a Field of the Post Type: `summary`, `price`;
- a part of a Field: a Group's Field (`address.city`), a Repeater's Field across its rows (`team.name`: each row's name, a list), a link's `url` or `text`, a select's `value` or `label`, a body's `excerpt`, `image`, `reading_time` and `toc` (see "The Body field");
- what a Relationship points at, one level deep: the published Entries' `title`, `slug`, `url` (from their Post Type's URL pattern; a Page's path for a Page), dates (as dates, for the `date` formatter) and Fields (`category.title`, `category.url`, `authors.bio`); unpublished Entries are left out;
- `site.x`: the published Globals (`site.phone`, `site.address.city`).

On its own a select shows its label, a link its text (or its address), a Relationship its Entries' titles, rich text its cleaned HTML, a body its Blocks as HTML, true/false `true` or `false` (Conditions test them as they are); Groups and Repeater rows show nothing, and a media Field reads as `media:<id>` (see "Media on pages"). A list (a multi-select, a Repeater's Field, a multi-Relationship) shows its values joined with commas; a Loop over it (`{{@loop tags as tag}}`) shows them one by one. A path through a list keeps one value per item (null where an item has no such part), so a Loop finds each item's value at its place.

- `site.language` is the page's language and `site.name` the Site's name (the Globals can't take those keys);
- `<name>.count`, `.empty`, `.total`, `.page` and `.pages` read a Loop of the Site (see "Loops in Template markup").

## The visual builder

Staff build Templates and Components in a full-screen visual builder (#66; ADRs 0002, 0009, 0017). Core: `packages/core/src/builder/` (`services.builder`); procedures `builder.*` (`packages/api/src/procedures/builder.ts`, all `structure.edit`); the screens: `ui/builder/` and the route `routes/builder.$siteId.$.tsx`, outside the admin shell. Staff open it from Site › Templates (`templates.list`: every Template the Site can have, made or not; an Archive template whose URL pattern starts with `{slug}` has none) and Site › Components (which also creates them).

- **What it opens** (`BuilderTarget`): `siteTemplate`, `searchTemplate`, a Post Type's `entryTemplate` or `archiveTemplate`, or one of the Site's own `component`s (a Module's changes with its Module). Its address is `/builder/<site>/site-template`, `…/entry-template/<post type>`, `…/component/<id>` and so on. `builder.open` answers the Draft's tree (empty while the Site hasn't made the Template: the first save makes it), its `version`, a Component's Props, Dark mode and the Default language, and the **catalogue**: the elements and Template commands this kind of tree may hold (from the same `refuseElement` and `refuseCommand` its saves use), the Draft's Classes (with their styles as CSS), token utilities and token variables, the Components it may place (never one placing it), the Loops, the Forms and the values bindings may read.
- **One Draft, two views** (ADR 0017): the builder edits the tree in the browser, and saves by printing it (`builder.markup`) and handing the markup to the same saves Code mode, the CLI and AI clients use (`templates.save…Markup`, `components.saveMarkup` with the Props), based on the version it opened (`CHANGED_MEANWHILE` opens the conflict prompt: reload theirs, or save over it). The Template markup view shows the same print; switching back reads it (`builder.read`), so markup typed with its own line breaks comes back exactly as typed (whitespace-only text is kept in the tree, hidden from the Layers panel, and moves step over it). `ui/builder/save.ts` is the one place the builder writes its Draft: Co-editing (#71) replaces it with changes to the Draft's shared document.
- **One-off styles** stay pending until the save: the builder keeps them by element id, `builder.markup` prints them as inline `style` attributes, and the markup save turns each into a generated Class in its transaction, exactly as a style typed in markup does (`builder.read` keeps a style that would make a new Class pending, so switching views makes nothing). An element's one-off style is its pending declarations, or its generated Classes' styles; changing it replaces them. Named Classes are made (`designSystem.createClass`) and restyled (`designSystem.updateClass`) in the Design system's Draft at once, as Code mode's CSS panel does: pages show those once the Design system is published.
- **The canvas** is an iframe of `/api/builder/canvas` on the admin host: an empty page whose own policy allows no script (`default-src 'none'`, images and media as Site pages may show them, `frame-ancestors 'self'`), framed `sandbox="allow-same-origin"` without scripts and out of the tab order. The builder fills it itself: `builder.preview` renders the tree as the Site renders it (the same renderer, escaping and URL rules) with real values where the Site has them (an Entry template shows the Entry saved last, from its Draft; the Globals' Draft; a Component's Props' defaults; Loops over published Entries), a binding with no value shown as written (`{{ summary }}`), Media from the admin's `/api/media/<site>/…`, every element tagged `data-wc-code="b#<id>"` (inside a Placement, `b/<placement id>#<id>`), and what only a page shows (`{{@outlet}}`, a Form, the Search element, the theme toggle, the Language switcher, Cookie settings) as placeholders the builder words; with the Design system's Draft stylesheet (`compileStylesheet` over the Draft trees and this one, pending one-off styles as the generated Classes they will become), applied as a constructed stylesheet. The light/dark switch sets `data-wc-theme` on the canvas's `<html>`. Outlines (selection, hover, fixed text) and drop targets are drawn by the admin over the iframe, never into the Site's HTML. A tree its saves would refuse is refused by the preview too (`TEMPLATE_INVALID`, each problem with where, `styles.<element id>` for a one-off style), and the canvas lists the problems.
- **The Layers panel** is an ARIA tree (one tab stop): the arrow keys move between layers and open and close them; Alt with the arrows moves the selected layer up, down, into the layer before it and out of what holds it; Space picks it up so the arrows move it, Space puts it down and Escape puts it back (as the component-arranging prototype's layout drawer); Ctrl/⌘ with X, C, V and D cut, copy, paste and duplicate (copies get new ids); Delete removes; F2 renames the layer label in place. Every change is announced (`role="status"`) with where the layer is now (`Moved li: 3 of 3 in ul.`), undone with Ctrl/⌘ Z and redone with Shift. A toolbar offers the same on the selected layer, rows drag, and the selected element drags on the canvas; both drop the same way (`dropPositionAt`: inside the middle of something that can hold children, else before or after). Moves are checked in the browser with the rules stored trees keep (`@webcraft/renderer/builder`: elements, nesting a browser would change, text placement, Template commands' places), so a refused move says why in the tree's own words.
- **Attribute and Prop fields** show and read the text between an attribute's quotes exactly as Template markup writes it (`printAttributeText` and `parseAttributeText`, `@webcraft/renderer/builder`: the renderer's own printer and parser), so `{{ path }}` is a value, `\{{` a literal `{{` and `&amp;` an `&`, and what a field shows reads back as the same parts.
- **The insert menu** (Ctrl/⌘ I) offers semantic elements first (sections, headings, paragraphs, lists, links, a link styled as a button, since Templates hold no `<button>` and run no scripts, quotes, figures, tables, disclosure), Media images (chosen in the Media library, written `media:<id>`), values, every Template command of the registry the tree may hold, the Components it may place (asking for required Props), text, comments and any other element by its tag; inside the selected layer, after or before it.
- **Fixed text**: a text node that shows text is flagged as "Fixed text" in the Layers panel (a badge) and on the canvas (a dashed amber outline around the element holding it, shown or hidden from the top bar, with a count). Its settings offer to make it editable: "Make it a Prop" in a Component (a new text Prop whose default is the text, saved with the Component, or one it has), "Make it a Field" in an Entry template (a new text Field of the Post Type whose default is the text, added at once, or one it has) and "Make it a Global" in the other Templates (a new Global whose default is the text, added with `globals.setFields` in one call, which starts the Globals' Draft with it, so a refusal, such as text longer than a text Field holds, adds nothing; or one the Site has); the text becomes the binding that reads it. The dialog names the text by its start.
- **Seams**: Co-editing (#71) replaces `saveDraft` in `ui/builder/save.ts`; Checks (#95) can read the same trees; Conditions' and Loops' settings are edited as text (`readExpression`), so a richer Condition builder can replace those fields.

## The Design system

Each Site's Design system (ADR 0009) is its Design tokens, Classes, base styles, custom CSS and dark mode setting. It is Structure (`structure.edit`, Staff only), has a Draft and a published version, and is published as one item. The pure parts (the document, tokens, colour ramps, CSS checking, the compiler, DTCG files, the theme toggle's script) are in `packages/renderer/src/design-system/`; storage, usage, publishing and the Site's stylesheet in `packages/core/src/design-system/`.

- **Storage**: one `design_systems` row per Site holds the Draft and the published version as documents (`DesignSystem`), made the first time the Site changes it. Until then the Draft is the default one (`defaultDesignSystem()`), and until the first publish visitors see the default one. Every change locks the row (`lockDesignSystem`). Sections a stored document lacks read as the default ones (`readDesignSystem`), which is how the Classes #58 made were moved in by a custom migration.
- **Tokens** (`tokens.ts`, `colour.ts`): colours, the space and text scales, fonts, radius, shadows, breakpoints, content widths and the focus style. Each has an id that never changes and a name, and becomes custom properties in the stylesheet's `tokens` layer: `--<colour>-<step>`, `--space-<step>`, `--text-<step>`, `--font-<name>`, `--radius-<name>`, `--shadow-<name>`, `--width-<name>` (breakpoints are used by Classes instead). A colour is one base colour; its shades (10, the lightest, to 95; the base is 60) are worked out in OKLCH and its transparencies (`a10` to `a90`) from the base, and any shade but 60 can be given its own value. Dark values run along the same steps the other way (10 stays the page's background end) from a dark base colour, the light one unless set, with dark overrides of their own: Light/Dark is one set of ramps. Scale steps are `base × ratio^exponent` at the smallest and the largest viewport (360 and 1440 px by default), written as `clamp()` in between; any step can be given its own sizes. The smallest named breakpoint is where phones end (768 px by default).
- **Classes**: `{ id, name, generated, styles, breakpoints, css }`. `styles` are declarations for every width, `breakpoints` declarations from a breakpoint's width up (by the breakpoint's id), `css` the Class's own custom CSS, declarations and rules nested with `&`. Names are unique on the Site, never the name of a token utility, and never start with `wc-` (`isReservedName`: WebCraft's own classes, `wc-skip-link`, `wc-theme-toggle`). Elements store Classes by id, so a rename changes every page once published.
- **Token utilities**: one-declaration classes the tokens make (`bg-primary-10`, `p-m`, `text-xl`, `radius-m`, `shadow-s`, `max-width-content`, …, `UTILITIES`). Elements name them like Classes and store them by an id made from the token's id (`u:bg:colour-primary:10`), so renaming a token renames its utilities everywhere.
- **CSS as stored** (`css.ts`): every piece of CSS (Class styles, breakpoint styles, a Class's own CSS, base styles, the Site's custom CSS, later a Component's) is read with css-tree before it is saved, and each problem has its line and column: syntax, unknown properties, values a property doesn't take (values with `var()` are left to the browser), at-rules out of place, `!important` (layers make it unnecessary), `@import`, `@layer`, `@charset`, `@namespace`, properties that only ever ran script, and any address outside the Site (`url()`, `image-set()`, `src()`, in custom property values too, escaped names read as CSS reads them): only paths such as `/_media/…` and `#ids`. It is stored as written, with each `--token` and each `.class` in a selector kept by id (`CssText`); printing writes today's names, so a rename changes every use. When a save sends CSS text unchanged from what it read, the stored references are kept, even if a name changed in the same save.
- **In use**: deleting a Class, or removing a token, that an element tree (any Template's or Component's Draft or published version) or the Design system's own CSS, focus style or breakpoint styles uses is refused with `CLASS_IN_USE` / `TOKEN_IN_USE` and `data.usedBy`, each use saying where (`usage.ts`: `treeSources` reads Templates and Components; Popups add theirs).
- **Template markup** reads Class and utility names from the Draft (`classCatalogue`). Saving and publishing a Template check its Class ids against the Draft. Visitors see a Class's name and styles once the Design system is published, except generated Classes, which go live with the Template that made them (`publishGeneratedClasses`): a one-off style shows with its Template; a Class Staff made or changed waits for the Design system.
- **The stylesheet** (`compileStylesheet`, `core/design-system/stylesheet.ts`): one per Site, built from what it has published: the published Design system (the default one before) and the Classes and utilities its published trees use, nothing else. Its cascade layers are declared first, `reset, tokens, base, classes, utilities, custom`, so later layers win whatever their selectors: `reset` (box sizing, media, `[hidden]` always hidden, reduced motion), `tokens` (`:root` custom properties, and dark values when dark mode is on), `base` (the focus style's custom properties from the focus token, base styles of plain elements, the skip link, the theme toggle, the Focal point classes of Media images, a body's callouts and videos: the info tone follows the primary colour token by id, the others have their own colours until custom CSS sets the `--wc-callout-*` properties; Forms; the Consent banner, see "The Consent banner"), `classes` (Class styles, then each breakpoint's from the smallest up), `utilities`, `custom` (Classes' own CSS, Components' (#63, `StylesheetUse.componentCss`), then the Site's). Everything is written out by the parser from the stored CSS, never pasted, so nothing can end a layer early; each piece is checked again as it is built, and one that fails the checks is left out (`buildCss`), so CSS stored before the checks existed (Classes moved from the earlier `classes` table) never reaches a stylesheet unchecked. It is served at `/_wc/css/<16 hex digits of its SHA-256>.css` from the Site's domain with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, an ETag and `nosniff`; its name changes whenever what it holds changes. The same published data always builds the same file, so every web process agrees on it; each process keeps a Site's built styles for the Site's page cache generation. Publishing a Template or the Design system keeps the stylesheet it replaced in `stylesheets` for 30 days (`RETIRED_STYLESHEET_LIFETIME_MS`), so a page loaded just before still finds its file; such publishes take the Design system's row lock before reading the current stylesheet, so two at once take turns and neither loses the one the other made. Paths are `/_wc/…`, never naming WebCraft, since Site output stays unbranded.
- **Base styles** (`BASE_TARGETS`): `body`, headings, paragraphs, links, lists, tables and the rest, from tokens; links in `<nav>` are at least 24 px so lists of links are big enough to tap (WCAG 2.5.8). Staff can change them; the defaults pass axe in light and dark.
- **Dark mode** (`darkMode`): off by default. With it on, pages say `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark">` and the dark values apply under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`, unless the visitor chose light with the theme toggle (`data-wc-theme` on `<html>`), and whenever they chose dark.
- **The theme toggle** (`{{@themeToggle}}`): a `<button class="wc-theme-toggle" aria-pressed hidden>` in the page's language. Pages never carry inline scripts (ADR 0023), so its script is a file, `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`THEME_TOGGLE_SCRIPT`), linked in `<head>` only from pages that show a toggle; it applies the stored choice (`localStorage` `wc-theme`) before the page shows, unhides the toggles and keeps `aria-pressed` true to the theme on screen. Without dark mode the toggle renders nothing and no script is linked.
- **DTCG files** (`dtcg.ts`): `designSystem.exportTokens` writes the tokens as DTCG 2025.10 JSON (every shade and transparency, each step's size at the smallest viewport, `$type` per group, the focus style as aliases such as `{color.primary.70}`), WebCraft's own details (ids, base colours, overrides, dark values, ratios, fluid ranges) under `$extensions.webcraft`; importing an export gives back the same tokens. `designSystem.importTokens` also reads files from other tools (plain colour tokens, hex strings or DTCG colour objects, `"16px"` or `{ value, unit }`): each top-level group it holds replaces that kind of token, kinds it leaves out stay, and a token keeps its id by `$extensions.webcraft.id`, else by name. A file it can't read is `TOKENS_FILE_INVALID` with `data.problems`.
- **API** (all `structure.edit`): `designSystem.get` (Draft and published, CSS as text with today's names, `status`), `saveDraft` (sections `tokens`, `classes`, `baseStyles`, `customCss`, `darkMode`; each given replaces that section, `classes` being every Class in order; tokens without an id are new), `createClass`, `updateClass` (rename or restyle), `deleteClass`, `exportTokens`, `importTokens` and `publish` (through `publishing.publish` with `{ kind: "designSystem" }`, clearing the page cache, logged as `designSystem.publish`). A Draft that can't be saved is `DESIGN_SYSTEM_INVALID` with `data.problems`, each `{ at, message, line?, column? }` (`at` such as `customCss`, `classes[2].styles`, `tokens.colours[0].light.base`).
- **Seams for later tickets**: Components' trees are in `publishedTrees` (`stylesheet.ts`) and `treeSources` (`usage.ts`), and a Component's generated Classes go live with it; Component custom CSS (stored with the Component's version, published with it) goes into `StylesheetUse.componentCss`; Code mode (#76) shows the Draft with `draftStyles` (`compileStylesheet(draft, { classIds })` over the Draft trees) and saves Class CSS and the Site CSS through `changeDraft`; the Design system screen is #65.

## Migrations

Migrations are drizzle-kit files in `packages/db/src/migrations`, applied by their own `migrate` step (ADR 0031), never by web or worker at start-up.

- Change the schema in `packages/db/src/schema/` (one file per area, re-exported from `index.ts`), then `bun run db:generate`. Commit the generated folder.
- Hand-written SQL (functions, triggers, data fixes) goes in its own custom migration: `bun run db:generate --custom --name=<what-it-does>`. Keeping it separate means a regenerated schema migration never loses it.
- Every change keeps the previous version working on the new schema: add first, remove in a later release. There are no down-migrations.
- **The migrate step** (`webcraft admin migrate`, `migrateStep` in `packages/core/src/migrate-step.ts`, on top of `runMigrations` in `packages/db/src/migrations.ts`):
  1. When anything is pending (WebCraft's migrations, a Module's, the job queue's schema) on a database that already holds an Install, it **takes a Backup first** (reason `before-migrations`, where Install settings › Backups says), reading the database through its catalog, since this build's tables may not exist yet. `--no-backup` skips it, and the output says so. A new database has nothing to back up.
  2. **Each migration runs in its own transaction**, WebCraft's first (oldest first), then each Module's in the order the build lists its Modules (`FIRST_PARTY_MODULES`), each Module's oldest first. Module migrations come after all of WebCraft's because they may point at WebCraft's tables, never the other way round. A migration that fails is rolled back and stops the step; the ones before it stay applied, which the previous version runs on (add first). WebCraft's are recorded in Drizzle's own table (`drizzle.__drizzle_migrations`), as drizzle-kit records them, so its tools still read it.
  3. It installs or upgrades the job queue's schema, then **makes every job queue** of `coreJobs` and brings the existing ones' retry and expiry settings up to date (`jobQueues(coreJobs)`), so a job sent before the worker ever started waits in its queue.
  4. It records this build's version (`WEBCRAFT_VERSION`) in `install_versions`: the newest is the version the data was written by, which a Backup's manifest carries.
  Only one migrate step changes a database at a time (an advisory lock). Web and worker check the result at start-up and refuse to run on a database that is behind; `/readyz` reports the same check.
- **Going back a version**: a database ahead of the build is fine: WebCraft's applied migrations the build doesn't have are left alone, and a Module's applied migrations that sort after every one the build has are accepted (with the tables they made). A migration the build has lost from the middle, or one that changed after it was applied, is still refused. The job queue is the exception: pg-boss refuses a schema newer than its own, so `checkSchema` answers `job-queue-newer` (and `/readyz` 503) and start-up says to restore the Backup the migrate step took before that update.
- **Module migrations** follow the same steps (see "Modules › Tables and migrations"): each in its own transaction, with its rules checked inside it (nothing outside the Module's schema), and the Module's tables checked against what it declares with its last pending migration (and on every run), recorded in `module_migrations` with a checksum. The schema check counts a pending Module migration as behind. `runMigrations`, `checkSchema` and `assertSchemaCurrent` take the build's Modules as `{ modules: moduleSchemas(FIRST_PARTY_MODULES) }`; `services.modules.schemas` holds the Install's.
- **A restore** rebuilds a Backup's schema with `applyMigrationLevel`: exactly the migrations the Backup's database had, each in its own transaction (see "Backups").
- When two branches both add migrations, keep the integration branch's and regenerate yours on top with `bun run db:generate`. Git often merges two such migrations without a conflict, but both were generated from the same parent, so the newer snapshot misses the other's tables and the next `db:generate` would write them again. `tests/db/migration-chain.test.ts` fails when that happens: delete your migration's folder and generate it again (a custom one: recreate it with `--custom` and paste its SQL back).

## Backups

A Backup (GLOSSARY.md, ADR 0032) is a whole Install in one archive; `packages/core/src/backups/`, behind `services.backups`; tables `backups` (the Backup log), `backup_settings` and `install_versions`.

- **The archive** is a plain tar file (`tar.ts`: POSIX ustar, pax headers for long paths and large sizes, written and read as streams, never held in memory), `webcraft-backup-<yyyymmdd>-<hhmmss>-<reason>-<4 hex>.tar`: `manifest.json` first (`manifest.ts`, `backupManifestSchema`: format and version, the archive's name, when, why, the WebCraft version the data was written by (`install_versions`) and the one that took it, the SDK version, the Modules and their versions, the migration level, the Master key's fingerprint, the tables in load order with their columns and row counts, sequences, partitions, and what the files are), then `database/<n>.copy.gz`, then `files/<storage key>`. `tar -tf` and `tar -xf` read it.
- **The database dump** (`dump.ts`) is written by WebCraft itself through `pg` and `pg-copy-streams` (Postgres's COPY text format, gzip-compressed per table), in one `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot, so web, worker and the CLI need no `pg_dump` and any Postgres server works. It holds data only: the schema is the migrations. It covers `public` and every Module schema (`m_*`), partitioned tables through their parent (the partitions' bounds are in the manifest), and every sequence's value, in foreign-key order. Left out (`LEFT_OUT_TABLES`): the job queue (`pgboss`), the migration records (rebuilt by the restore's migrations) and `visitor_salts` (a day's salt must not outlive its day).
- **Files** (`files.ts`): every file under local storage (`WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR`) except Media variants on disk (`media_files.role = 'variant'`, read in the dump's snapshot), half-written `.partial` files and `webcraft-check/`. A file deleted while the Backup runs is left out. Files in an S3-compatible bucket aren't copied; the manifest counts them and names the bucket.
- **Taking one** (`takeBackup`, `take.ts`): one at a time on the Install (an advisory lock; `BackupBusyError`), recorded in the Backup log as `running`, then `done` with its size or `failed` with why (`BackupFailedError`, logged as an error). `services.backups.take({ reason, to? })`; the server commands build their context with `openBackupForCommand` (it works on a database that is behind, and refuses a Master key that isn't the Install's).
- **Targets** (`targets.ts`): the Backups folder (`WEBCRAFT_BACKUP_DIR`; written beside its name, then renamed), a file or folder `--to` names, or an S3-compatible bucket (`<prefix>/backups/<name>`, a multipart upload of 32 MB parts through `s3Client` from `media/s3-storage.ts`, aborted on failure; the bucket's secret is the Install secret `backups.s3.secretAccessKey`). Like Media storage, the bucket is spoken to through `services.http` directly, not the guarded fetcher: a self-hosted store is usually on an internal address.
- **Install settings › Backups** (`install.backups.get`, `install.backups.update`; `install.manage`; changing it is the Sensitive action `backups.change`, logged as `backups.change`, never with the secret): scheduled on or off (on unless changed), the target, the bucket (checked like Media storage's before it is saved: `STORAGE_UNREACHABLE`). `get` also answers the Backup log (newest 30) and `status` (`{ last, lastDone, failing }`), the seam the Install's health and its alerts (#86) read; until then the admin shows `shell.context`'s `banners.backupFailing` on every page for the Superadmin.
- **Scheduled Backups**: `backups.scheduled` (01:11 UTC) takes one while the setting is on, then `prune()` removes the scheduled Backups the keep rules (`keep.ts`, `backupsToRemove`) no longer keep: the newest of each of the last 7 days and of each of the last 4 weeks (Monday to Sunday, UTC; `BACKUP_KEEP`). Backups taken by command or before migrations are never removed. A failed one isn't retried before the next day.
- **Restoring** (`restoreBackup`, `restore.ts`; `webcraft admin restore`): refuses (`RestoreRefusedError` with `refusal.reason`) a file that isn't a Backup (`not-a-backup`; that includes a manifest naming anything but tables, columns and sequences of WebCraft's and the Modules' schemas as plain names, or a partition bound other than the monthly ranges WebCraft makes, `PARTITION_BOUND`: the manifest is read before the archive can be trusted, and the partition statement is built again from the bound's two timestamps), a newer WebCraft version (`newer-version`, by semver) or migrations this build doesn't have (`newer-migrations`), another Master key's fingerprint (`master-key`), a database web or worker is connected to (`in-use`, by `application_name`), and a database that holds any table (`not-empty`) unless `replace: { confirm }` names the database (`confirmation`); then it empties it. Then `applyMigrationLevel` rebuilds the schema at the Backup's level, the partitions are made, the rows load in one transaction and the sequences are set, the files go back into local storage, the migrate step runs (an older Backup is brought up to this version; the job queue is made), and `afterRestore` deletes the variants' rows of local files and queues `media.variants` for every image without variants, `media.process` for uploads waiting, `imports.run` for runs under way, raises every Site's page cache generation and marks every search index for a rebuild, and settles the Backup log's `running` line. Waiting jobs (emails being retried, Webhook deliveries) aren't in a Backup.
- **Tests** (`tests/backups/`): `install.backup({ to? })`, `install.restore(archive, { replace? })` and `install.migrate({ backup: true })` run what the commands run; `tests/harness/backups.ts` reads an archive's manifest and writes a copy with a rewritten one (a Backup "from a newer version"); `tests/backups/commands.test.ts` runs the real commands.

## The update check

`packages/core/src/updates.ts`, behind `services.updates`; table `update_checks` (one row).

- The worker's `updates.check` (05:29 UTC, at most once a day: `check({ onlyIfDue: true })`) and `webcraft admin check-update` (at once) read the release feed (`config.updateFeed`, `WEBCRAFT_UPDATE_FEED`) through the guarded fetcher, to the feed's host only, 10 seconds, 2 MB. The request carries `User-Agent: WebCraft` and `Accept: application/json`, nothing else: no Install id, no version, no query of its own.
- The feed can be GitHub's release JSON (`/releases/latest`, or the list `/releases`, skipping drafts and pre-releases) or a static file `{ "version", "url", "notes", "publishedAt" }`. The newest version (semver, `v` dropped), its `https` release URL, its notes as plain text (4,000 characters at most) and when it was published are kept; a failed read keeps the release found before and records why (`problem`: the guarded fetcher's, `status` or `format`).
- `services.updates.latest()` compares it with `WEBCRAFT_VERSION` (`updateAvailable`; a version that isn't semver never is behind). The admin shows `shell.context`'s `banners.update` on the Install's pages for the Superadmin, and `install.updates.get` on Install settings › General.
- **WebCraft's version** is the `version` of the repository's root `package.json` (`WEBCRAFT_VERSION`, `packages/core/src/version.ts`): a release sets it, then is tagged `v<version>`.

## Jobs

Jobs are defined with `defineJob({ name, payload, queue, run })` (in `packages/core/src/jobs/`, or next to their module, such as `email/outbox.ts`) and listed in `coreJobs` (`packages/core/src/jobs/index.ts`); the worker only works listed jobs. `sample-ping.ts` is the sample to copy.

- `name` is the queue name, `area.action` (`email.send`). Never rename one that has shipped.
- `payload` is a Zod schema; it is checked when the job is sent and again when it runs.
- `queue` holds retry and expiry settings (`retryLimit`, `retryDelay`, `retryBackoff`, ...).
- `run(payload, services)` gets the same `InstallServices` as the web process: use `services.clock`, `services.http` and `services.log`, never `Date.now()`, `fetch` or `console` directly. To email someone, send a System email (see "Email"); never call `services.mailer`.
- Enqueue with `services.jobs.send(job, payload, { startAfter })`.
- A job that works on one Site's data opens it with `services.data.background(siteId)` (see "The tenant data layer"); `media.process` and `media.variants` are the examples.
- **Scheduled jobs**: `defineSchedule({ job, cron, payload })`, listed in `coreSchedules` (so far the daily `email.purge-log`, `rate-limits.purge`, `modules.purge-log`, `site-files.purge`, `media.purge`, `webhooks.purge-log`, `consent.purge-records`, `modules.daily`, `modules.partitions` (Modules' partitioned tables, 02:17), `visitors.purge-salts` (00:04), `imports.purge` (04:23; besides `imports.run`, `imports.survey` and `imports.preview`, which the web process queues), `backups.scheduled` (01:11, see "Backups") and `updates.check` (05:29, see "The update check"), the hourly `modules.hourly` (Modules' scheduled jobs), `domains.verify-due` and `search.rebuild-due` every minute, the hourly `domains.check-all`, and the daily `cdn.refresh-ranges`). `cron` is read in UTC (`17 3 * * *` is every day at 03:17). The migrate step makes every job's queue (and the worker makes any still missing), then the worker registers every listed schedule when it starts (and removes ones no longer listed); when it was down at a due time, the job runs once when it is back. The test harness's `drainJobs()` sends each scheduled job whose time came by the test clock since the last drain.

## Email

Everything about email is in `packages/core/src/email/`, behind `services.email`. Nothing else calls the `Mailer`: every email goes through the System email sender, which records it in the Email log and queues it.

**Sending a System email** (an email to a person with an account, or to an address about to get one, such as an invite):

```ts
await services.email.send({
	type: "review-submitted",                       // from the catalogue
	to: [{ personId }, { email: "new@client.example", language: "it" }],
	data: { authorName, itemTitle, siteName, reviewPath: `/sites/${siteId}/review/${id}` },
	related: { kind: "entry", id },                  // what the Email log links to
});
// → [{ recipient, status: "queued" | "logged" | "turned-off", emailId }]
```

- **The catalogue** (`email/catalogue.ts`) lists every System email type with its category and a Zod schema of its data. `security` emails (invites, resets, sign-in changes) always go; each `work` and `alert` type can be turned off by each person in Your account › Emails, and is then skipped for them (`turned-off`). Add a type to the catalogue, its wording to `email/wording/en.ts` and `it.ts` (a missing one fails the typecheck), and its name (and, for work and alert types, a description) under `emailTypes` in the admin's message files. Never rename a type that shipped.
- **Links** in data are admin paths (`/invites/abc`), never full URLs: the sender puts the admin's origin in front, so System emails only ever link into the admin.
- **Language and layout**: each recipient gets the email in their Admin language (a bare address: the `language` given, else the Install's default), in one built-in layout (`email/layout.ts`: logo or sender name on a bar in the accent colour, heading, paragraphs, one button, footer), under **Install branding** (Install settings › Email (SMTP): sender name, logo address, accent colour, footer text). Every value is escaped. Work and alert emails link to the person's switches.
- **Which SMTP**: each queued email names its SMTP (`SmtpChoice` in `email/outbox.ts`): `{ kind: "install" }`, or `{ kind: "site", siteId }` for a Site's own server. System emails use the Install SMTP. The Install SMTP password is the Install secret `smtp.password`, a Site's the Site secret `smtp.password`, each filled in only for the send; the `email.send` job runs through `services.email.deliver`, which holds the secrets store and the data layer.
- **A Site's mail** (Notifications, Auto-replies, and the emails of Site-level Modules, SDK 1.6) goes out as the Site: `services.email.siteMail.route(scope)` reads the Site's email settings (through its scope) and `siteMail.queue` renders (`renderSiteMail`: a plain, escaped layout without Install branding) and queues one email, recorded in the Email log with its Site (`email_log.site_id`). Through the Site's own server when it has one (its From address, the sender name set, else the Site's name), else the Install SMTP under the Site's name. Its Reply-To is the email's own (a Notification: the visitor) or the Site's reply-to, never the Install SMTP's. A Site that goes back to the Install SMTP while an email waits sends it through the Install SMTP; a Site's server that fails never does.
- **Site SMTP** (`email/site-smtp.ts`, behind `services.email.siteSmtp`; table `site_smtp`; procedures `siteSmtp.get`, `siteSmtp.update`, `siteSmtp.sendTest`, all `structure.edit`; the admin's Site › Email (SMTP)): a Site setting, saved at once and logged `siteSmtp.change` (never the password), a Sensitive action (`siteSmtp.change`). It holds the sender name (null: the Site's name), the reply-to, and the server (host, port, security, username, From address; null: the Install SMTP). Leaving the server out forgets its password; new settings clear its health record. Send test sends at once through the settings in the form (the stored password unless one is typed), or through the Install SMTP under the Site's name when no server is given; it never counts towards Failing. Its health record is `site:<site id>` in `smtp_health`; the third failure in a row (Failing) sends the `site-smtp-failing` alert to the Site's Owners and Staff (`siteOwners`, `siteStaff`), through the Install SMTP, once.
- **No SMTP yet**: until the Superadmin sets the Install SMTP, the email is written to the log (`warn`, with `to`, `subject` and `text`) instead of being sent, and the Email log says "Written to the log".
- **Delivery and retries**: the `email.send` job sends it. A failed attempt is tried again after 1 minute, then a wait that doubles up to 4 hours, until 24 hours after it was queued; then it is Failed. An address the server refuses for good (a 5xx answer to `RCPT TO`) is Failed at once. Completed `email.send` jobs, which hold the body, are deleted after an hour. Delivery is at least once: if the server takes an email but recording that fails, the job is retried and the email can go out twice. Every attempt carries the same `Message-ID` (`<Email log line id@sender's domain>`), so mail clients can drop the copy; losing an email (a password reset, an invite) would be worse than a rare duplicate.
- **Failing**: 3 failed sends in a row make the SMTP Failing: logged as an error, shown in Install settings › Email (SMTP) and as a notice on every admin page for the Superadmin (an email about a failing Install SMTP would go through it). A send that goes through, or saving new settings, clears it. Test emails don't count.
- **Test email**: `services.email.sendTest` sends at once through settings that may not be saved yet, and answers the server's reply. Without a typed password it uses the stored one, but only with the server it was saved for (the same host, port, security and user name, `sameSmtpServer`): naming another server without its password is refused (`SMTP_PASSWORD_NEEDED`, 400), so nobody who may send a test can have a stored password sent where they choose. The same holds for `siteSmtp.sendTest`.
- **The Email log** keeps time, type, recipient, subject, SMTP (`install`, `site` or `none`), the Site for a Site's mail, status (Queued, Retrying, Sent, Failed, Written to the log), attempts, the server's last answer and the related item, never the body. The `email.purge-log` job deletes lines older than 90 days every day.
- **The Mailer** (`email/mailer.ts`) is the edge: `send(email, smtp)` over SMTP with Nodemailer (`email/smtp-mailer.ts`), rejecting with `SmtpDeliveryError` (the server's answer, and whether it refused the recipient for good).
- **Who manages it**: the Install SMTP, Install branding and the Email log procedures (`packages/api/src/procedures/email.ts`) are for the Superadmin, through one gate at the top of that file (`emailAdmin`). Changing the SMTP is a Sensitive action (`installSmtp.change`, see "Sensitive actions") and is recorded in the Audit log, never with the password.

## Secrets and the Master key

Every secret (SMTP passwords, AI keys, CDN tokens, Webhook and CAPTCHA secrets) goes through `services.secrets` (`packages/core/src/secrets/`). Secrets are write-only: code gets a value only inside a callback, and people see only "set" and its last 4 characters.

```ts
const password = { owner: { kind: "install" }, name: "smtp.password" } as const;

await services.secrets.set(password, value);                      // stores or replaces it
await services.secrets.useWith(password, (value) => smtp.login(user, value));
await services.secrets.describe(password);                        // { set: true, lastFour: "…", updatedAt } or { set: false }
await services.secrets.remove(password);
```

- **Owners**: a secret belongs to the Install (`{ kind: "install" }`: Install SMTP, shared AI keys), a Workspace (its AI key) or a Site (its SMTP, CDN token, Webhook and CAPTCHA secrets). `services.secrets` holds the Install's own; a Workspace's or Site's are reached only through the data layer, `workspace.secrets.set("ai.key", value)`, so code can't name another Workspace's secret (see "The tenant data layer"). They have foreign keys to their Workspace and Site, and go when it is deleted. A name is unique within its owner: 1 to 200 letters, digits and `.:_-`, such as `smtp.password` or `webhook:<id>:signing`.
- **No read-back**: there is no function that returns a value. `useWith` refuses a callback that returns the value (or a string holding it), so fill the secret into the call inside the callback and return the call's result. Never log a value, put it in a job payload or send it to the browser. `describe` shows no characters of a secret shorter than 16 characters, so never more than a quarter of one shows.
- **Errors**: `useWith` throws `SecretNotSetError` for a secret nobody set, and `useWith` and `describe` throw `SecretUnreadableError` for one that was changed outside WebCraft. Messages name the secret, never its value.
- **Encryption**: AES-256-GCM, with a random 12-byte nonce per value, under a key derived with HKDF-SHA256 from the Master key and the secret's key version. The authenticated data binds each ciphertext to its owner and name, so a ciphertext copied into another row can't be decrypted there. Only Web Crypto is used, so the same code runs on Bun and Node.
- **Key versions**: the table `master_key_versions` holds the fingerprint of every Master key the Install has had (never a key). The Install's first secret records its key as version 1; each rotation adds the next. Every secret stores the version it is encrypted under.
- **Fingerprint**: `masterKey.fingerprint()` (a `MasterKey` comes from `loadMasterKey(env)` or `parseMasterKey(value, source)`) is a one-way value, 64 hexadecimal characters, safe to store and show. Backup manifests carry it, and restore compares it with the running key (ADR 0032).
- **Start-up**: web and worker read the key with `loadMasterKey`, then open secrets with `openSecrets`, which refuses a key that isn't the Install's current one. An Install with no secrets yet accepts any key.

### Rotating the Master key

`webcraft admin rotate-key` decrypts every secret with the old key and encrypts it again under the new key as the next key version, in one transaction. If any secret can't be decrypted, nothing changes and the command lists it. The new key goes where web and worker will read it from afterwards, `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`; the key being replaced is passed once, in `WEBCRAFT_OLD_SECRET_KEY` or a file.

1. Make the new key: `openssl rand -base64 32`. Store a copy outside the server.
2. Stop web and worker.
3. Put the new key in `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY` in the Install's environment.
4. Run the command in that environment, with the old key:

   ```sh
   WEBCRAFT_OLD_SECRET_KEY='<old key>' webcraft admin rotate-key
   # or: webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-file /run/secrets/old-master-key
   ```

   It prints how many secrets it re-encrypted, the new key version and the new key's fingerprint.
5. Start web and worker. Then remove the old key from wherever you passed it; it opens nothing any more.

Running the command again after it finished changes nothing. It refuses a new key the Install used before, and an old key that isn't the current one. Web or worker left running with the old key can no longer read or write secrets, and both refuse to start again until they have the new key, naming the key version they need.

### When the Master key is lost

Secrets can't be recovered without their Master key, and web and worker refuse to start with any other key while secrets exist. To start again with a new key, deleting every secret:

1. Make a new key with `openssl rand -base64 32` and put it in `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`.
2. `webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-lost` lists the secrets that would be deleted and the lost key's version and fingerprint, and changes nothing. If you still have the old key, or mistyped the new one, stop here and rotate normally.
3. `webcraft admin rotate-key --old-key-lost --confirm-fingerprint <first 12 characters of that fingerprint>` deletes them and makes the new key the next key version. Typing the fingerprint proves you read the listing for this Install; a bare "yes" would not. It refuses when `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY` is still the current key.
4. Start web and worker, and set the listed secrets again.

## Outgoing calls and the guarded fetcher

`services.http` (`OutgoingHttp`) is WebCraft's one way to other servers: `(Request, { connectTo? }) => Promise<Response>`, faked in tests. Calls to hosts someone else chose (a Module's outside hosts, Domain verification and the Domain check, Library downloads, Webhooks, Import from URL, the update check's release feed; later CAPTCHA verification, AI providers) go through the **guarded fetcher** on top of it (`createGuardedFetch({ http, dns })`, `packages/core/src/guarded-fetch.ts`):

- only `http` and `https` URLs without credentials, on the hosts allowed (`allowedHosts`, or `"any"` for any host on the public internet: Import from URL, where a person gives the address);
- the host is resolved first through the `Dns` port (`services.dns`; `systemDns` in the processes, `install.dns` in tests) and refused when any address is loopback, private, link-local, shared (CGNAT), multicast, reserved, documentation or benchmarking space (`isPublicAddress`, IPv4 and IPv6, IPv4 inside IPv6 and NAT64 included); the same after every redirect;
- the call is **pinned to the address checked**: outgoing HTTP gets the first address the host resolved to as `connectTo` and connects there, never resolving the name again, so a DNS server that answers the check with a public address and the connection with an internal one (DNS rebinding) can't reach inside the network; each redirect is checked and pinned again;
- at most 3 redirects (`maxRedirects: 0` hands a redirect back without following it); 303, and 301/302 after a POST, become a GET;
- a timeout (10 s by default, 30 s at most) and a size limit (5 MB, 20 MB at most): the body is read in full, and a larger one is refused. Only the fetcher the Media library builds raises the size ceiling (`ceilings`), to the largest file an Install may take (1 GB), for Import from URL;
- the answer's `url` is the address it finally came from, as `fetch` gives it.

`installAddresses` lets one caller, the Install's checks of its own domains, also call the Install's own addresses although they aren't public (see "Domains"); nothing else passes it. Refusals throw `GuardedFetchError` with `problem` (`scheme`, `host`, `address`, `redirects`, `timeout`, `size`, `unreachable`).

Each process has its own outgoing HTTP, wired in `openInstall` (`openInstallForCommand` uses the web one):

- **Web (Bun)**: `createBunHttp()` (`apps/server/src/outgoing-http.ts`; it uses Bun's own `fetch` TLS options, so it lives with the web process, not in `packages/core`, which keeps to standard APIs, ADR 0006). A pinned call is Bun's `fetch` to the address as the URL's host, with the URL's host name in the `Host` header and as `tls.serverName`, which Bun sends for SNI and checks the certificate against. WebCraft has no outgoing proxy in v1: Bun's `fetch` would honour `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` while Node's never does, so `createBunHttp()` sets those variables empty for the process (Bun reads them again when they are set, but not when they are deleted), and both processes call servers directly (`tests/outgoing-http/no-proxy.test.ts`).
- **Server commands (`webcraft admin …`)** run on Bun: the ones that make calls (`check-update` through the guarded fetcher, `backup` to a bucket) hand `createBunHttp()` to `openInstallForCommand({ http })` or `openBackupForCommand`; the others' outgoing HTTP refuses a pinned call.
- **Worker (Node)**: `createNodeHttp()` (`packages/core/src/http-node.ts`, standard Node APIs only). A pinned call goes over `node:http`/`node:https` with `host` set to the address and the host name as `servername`, which Node sends for SNI and checks the certificate against (a custom `checkServerIdentity` would stop Node reusing connections); the body streams as it arrives, decoded (gzip, deflate, br) as `fetch` decodes it.

On both, the certificate must be valid for the host name, never for the address (an address in the URL is checked as an address); a kept connection is reused only for the same address and host name; a pinned call never follows a redirect itself (the guarded fetcher does, checking each hop), and its timeout and size limit are the guarded fetcher's (the call stops when its signal fires; the body is read as it streams). A call without `connectTo` (S3 storage, the leaked-password check, the sample job) is the runtime's own `fetch`, as before. Each process hands its adapter to `openInstall` (`http`); each adapter refuses to start on the other runtime. Their `ca` option (`createBunHttp({ ca })`, `createNodeHttp({ ca })`) makes pinned calls trust a certificate authority a test made instead of the usual ones.

The guarded fetcher connects to the first address only; when it doesn't answer, the call is `unreachable` even if another address of the host would have. In development the Domain check connects to the first address a `.localhost` name resolves to: `::1` on macOS, where `vite dev` (listening on `localhost`) is too. A system that answers `127.0.0.1` first for `.localhost` names but binds `localhost` to `::1` would see the development domain as unreachable.

## Webhooks

A Site's Webhooks (spec story 139; `packages/core/src/webhooks/`, behind `services.webhooks`; tables `webhooks`, `webhook_deliveries`, `webhook_delivery_attempts`; procedures `webhooks.*`; the admin's Site › Webhooks) are Site settings: everyone who sees the Site reads them (`webhooks.list`, `webhooks.deliveries`, `site.view`), Staff change them (`structure.edit`), each change applies at once and is logged, and none is part of Publish all. How receivers check a delivery is in `docs/webhooks.md`.

- **A Webhook** is a name (its URL's host unless given), an `https` URL, the events it receives (`WEBHOOK_EVENTS`, `webhooks/events.ts`) and on or off; at most 20 per Site (`TOO_MANY_WEBHOOKS`). `webhookUrl` (`webhooks/rules.ts`) refuses at save time what could never be called (`WEBHOOK_INVALID` with `field: "url"` and `reason`): not `https` (`scheme`), credentials in it (`credentials`), `localhost`, a name without a dot, `.local`, `.internal`, `.home.arpa` or an IP address inside a network (`host`); it drops the fragment. Whether the host resolves to a public address is the guarded fetcher's to check on every call, since DNS changes.
- **The signing secret** is a Site secret, `webhook:<id>:signing` (see "Secrets and the Master key"): Staff give one (16 to 200 visible characters; `WEBHOOK_INVALID` with `field: "secret"`), or WebCraft makes one (32 random bytes, base64url) and answers it once, from `webhooks.add` and `webhooks.changeSecret`, never again; the list shows only "set" and its last 4 characters, and only to `structure.edit`. People without `structure.edit` see each URL's origin only, since a URL can carry a token in its path or query; the Audit log records hosts, never paths.
- **Sensitive action** (`webhook.change`): adding a Webhook (`webhooks.add`) and changing its name, URL and events (`webhooks.update`) or its secret (`webhooks.changeSecret`), since it sends the Site's data to an outside address, signed with a secret; API tokens and AI clients can't open such a channel. Switching on and off (`webhooks.setEnabled`), removing (`webhooks.remove`, two steps: the first answers `preview: { name, url, deliveries }`), Send test and Redeliver only use what was confirmed. Logged as `webhook.add`, `webhook.update` (with `changed`), `webhook.changeSecret`, `webhook.switchOn`, `webhook.switchOff`, `webhook.remove`.
- **Events**: `services.webhooks.queueEvent({ siteId }, event, data, at)` queues an event once the change it is about is saved, never inside its transaction: one delivery per Webhook of the Site that is on and receives it, each recorded with its body, and one `webhooks.deliver` job. The `publish` event comes from the Audit log: `afterAudit` turns every `<item>.publish` entry (`entry.publish`, `template.publish`, `form.publish`, `loop.publish`, …; the item's id in `<item>Id`) into it (`publishedBy`), so every way something is published, and every kind of item publishing gains, fires it, and a publish never waits for a receiver or fails because of one. `formSubmitted` is queued once a Submission is kept (never spam; also when one comes out of the Spam folder), with the Form and the Submission's values (the spec sends a Submission's values, story 99), related to the Submission (`webhook_deliveries.related_kind` and `related_id`), so the Submission's own delivery log lists it.
- **The body** is small JSON, the same on every attempt: `{ id, type, occurredAt, site: { id, name, url }, data }`, where `id` is the delivery id and `site.url` where visitors find the Site (`siteAddresses().main`). `publish`'s `data` is `{ item: { kind, id, postTypeId?, path? }, pathChanges, by: { id, name } | null, via }`: what was published, never a secret nor Content values (no titles). A test send's `data` is `{ by, via }`.
- **Sending** (`webhooks/deliveries.ts`): the worker signs each attempt (`webhooks/signing.ts`: `WebCraft-Signature: t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "<t>.<body>">`, with `WebCraft-Delivery` and `WebCraft-Event`) and posts it through the guarded fetcher: only to the Webhook's host, pinned to the address it checked, no redirect followed (the call carries a signature), 10 seconds, a 1 MB answer. The signing secret is read only inside `useWith`, from the store the data layer uses, for the Site the job names (a background scope has no secrets).
- **Retries**: no answer, a timeout, a host that doesn't resolve to a public address, 408, 429 and 5xx are tried again after 1, 4, 16, 64 and 256 minutes (fourfold from a minute), or as long as `Retry-After` asks when that is longer, up to 6 hours; 6 attempts in all, then the delivery is failed. A redirect, another 4xx or an answer over 1 MB fails it at once. Every attempt carries the same delivery id. Statuses: `queued`, `retrying`, `delivered`, `failed`.
- **The delivery log** (`webhooks.deliveries`, newest first, `limit` and `before` to page) keeps each delivery's body and every attempt: when, how long, the receiver's status, the first 500 characters of its answer, or what went wrong (`problem`: `status`, `redirect`, `timeout`, `unreachable`, `address`, `size`, `secret`; never an address it resolved to). `webhooks.purge-log` deletes deliveries older than 30 days, daily. **Send test** (`webhooks.test`) sends a signed `test` event at once from the web process, on or off, and answers what the receiver said; it is never retried and never counts as a failure. **Redeliver** (`webhooks.redeliver`) queues a failed delivery again with the same id and body for a new round of 6 attempts (numbered on), while the Webhook is on (`WEBHOOK_REDELIVERY_REFUSED`: `not-failed`, `test`, `switched-off`).
- **Switched off**: a Webhook switched off receives nothing, and its deliveries still waiting stop (failed, `lastProblem: "switched-off"`; redeliver them once it is on). After 10 deliveries in a row failed (`SWITCH_OFF_AFTER`; test sends don't count, a delivered one starts the count again), WebCraft switches it off (`offReason: "failing"`) and sends the Site's Owners (`siteOwners`, `tenancy/site-owners.ts`, as the Domain check does) the `webhook-switched-off` alert email once. Switching it on starts the count over.
- **Tests** (`tests/webhooks/`) answer receivers on the fake outgoing HTTP and check signatures as a receiver would (`receiverAccepts` in `tests/webhooks/support.ts`); `confirmed(browser, account, call)` there confirms who Staff are again when a test moved the clock past their identity check.

## Modules

A Module is a self-contained feature that reaches WebCraft only through the Module SDK (ADRs 0012, 0024). v1 loads first-party Modules only, in the same process as Core; the boundary is built so that a sandbox later changes the runtime, not the API.

### The SDK and the boundary

`@webcraft/module-sdk` (`packages/module-sdk`) is the public, versioned contract (`SDK_VERSION`, the same as its package version; additions raise the minor version, a change that could break a Module the major one). It holds the manifest schema, `defineModule`, `procedure`, `siteRoute`, `job`, `component`, the table helpers, the event catalogue, the context types Module code gets, `ModuleError` (with `isModuleError`, which knows one by its name, code and status, as a Module may run against another copy of the SDK) and `ModuleRefusedError`, address ranges (`isAddressRange`, `addressInRanges`), and re-exports what Modules need of Drizzle (column builders, comparison operators and the aggregates `count`, `countDistinct`, `sum`, `min`, `max`, never raw `sql`) and Zod, so every Module uses WebCraft's versions.

- **A Module imports nothing from WebCraft but the SDK**, and nothing that reaches around it: no `@webcraft/*` package, no Drizzle, Zod, `pg` or `pg-boss` of its own, no `node:` or `bun` modules, no `fetch`, `process`, `Bun`, `require` or `globalThis` globals. Biome refuses them in `modules/**` and `tests/modules/sample/**`; `tests/modules/boundary.test.ts` scans every Module's imports, globals and `package.json` too. A real Module package depends only on `@webcraft/module-sdk` (and its own outside packages), and Bun's isolated installs then can't resolve anything else of WebCraft's.
- **First-party Modules** are workspace packages, `modules/<id>`, each default-exporting a `defineModule(…)`, listed in `FIRST_PARTY_MODULES` (`packages/core/src/modules/first-party.ts`). Web, worker and the `migrate` step all load that list (`openInstall({ modules })` defaults to it).
- **The sample Module** (`tests/modules/sample/index.ts`, with the Workspace-level `board.ts`) uses every extension point up to SDK 1.3 and is the Module the host's tests drive; copy its shape. SDK 1.4's (what Site routes learn of the visitor, route limits, Site scripts before consent, monthly partitions, `aggregate`, `form.submitted`) are used by Site analytics (`modules/site-analytics`), whose tests drive them, with the definition rules in `tests/modules/sdk-rules.test.ts`. The extension points one Module of a build keeps (Form Submissions, Redirect rules and the 404 log) are shown by the Forms and Redirects Modules instead (SDK 1.5's are the Redirects Module's, SDK 1.6's the Forms Module's; the sample checks that `context.submissions` is refused to it). SDK 1.7's import sources are the WordPress import's (`modules/wordpress-import`), and its rules for Imports the Redirects Module's. SDK 1.8's (Widgets, `widgetGoals`, `context.data.values`, the sender's daily id on `form.submitted`) are Site analytics' again, with Widgets' definition rules in `tests/modules/sdk-rules.test.ts`.

### The manifest

```ts
export default defineModule({
	manifest: {
		id: "sample",                                    // lower-case words joined by dashes
		name: { en: "Sample notes", it: "Note di esempio" },
		description: { en: "…", it: "…" },
		version: "1.0.0",
		sdk: "^1.0.0",                                   // the SDK versions it works with
		level: "site",                                   // switched per Site, or "workspace"
		onByDefault: true,                               // on wherever Staff haven't switched it off
		adminLanguages: ["en", "it"],                    // every label in each; the first is the fallback
		permissions: [{ key: "notes.edit", label: { en: "Write notes", it: "…" }, roles: ["staff", "owner", "editor"] }],
		uses: ["email"],                                 // WebCraft services it uses
		outsideHosts: ["hooks.sample.example"],          // the only hosts it may call
		routePrefix: "sample",                           // its Site routes: /_wc/m/sample/…
```
