```ts
		siteScript: null,                                // or the Consent category of the script it adds
	},
	// tables, migrations, procedures, siteRoutes, adminPages, events, jobs, components
});
```

The manifest is checked with Zod (`moduleManifestSchema`), and the whole definition against the SDK's rules, when the Install loads it (`loadModules` in `packages/core/src/modules/registry.ts`): the SDK range must include `SDK_VERSION`; ids and route prefixes are unique; labels come in exactly the declared languages; a procedure or admin page names a Permission the Module adds or one of WebCraft's checked at its level; tables are in the Module's own schema and follow the tenant rule; migrations sort oldest first; Site routes need a route prefix and a Site-level Module; events are in the catalogue; unknown extension points are refused. A Module that breaks a rule is listed on the Modules screen as `cannot-run`, with the reason, and never runs; the rest of the Install runs on.

### Approval (the consent screen)

Nothing a Module asks for is granted until the Superadmin approves it on Install settings › Modules (`modules.list`, `modules.approve`; Permission `module.approve`, held by the Superadmin only). Approving is a Sensitive action (`module.approve`) and is recorded in the Audit log with what it granted.

- **What a Module asks for** is a list of consent items (`consentItems`): `permission:<key>:<role>` for each role it gives one of its Permissions, `use:email`, `host:<host>`, `script:<category>`. The consent screen shows them in WebCraft's own words (the admin's message files, English and Italian), never the Module's, so a Module can't word what it asks for itself; only its Permissions' labels are its own.
- **An approval** (`module_approvals`) stores the version approved and the items granted. A later version that asks for nothing more runs on it; one that asks for more is `update-waiting`: off everywhere (its switches and data kept) until the Superadmin approves it, the new items marked. Only one version of a first-party Module's code exists in a build, so "held at the approved version" means off until approved.
- `modules.approve` names the version the screen showed: another version answers `MODULE_CHANGED`. `modules.withdraw` takes a Module back to waiting, off everywhere, data kept.
- **The setup wizard** ends with the Modules step: the Modules waiting for approval, ticked (all Modules WebCraft loads are first-party), each with what it asks for.

### Switches and removing data

Staff switch Site-level Modules on each Site and Workspace-level Modules on each Workspace (`modules.siteList`, `modules.setOnSite`, `modules.workspaceList`, `modules.setOnWorkspace`; Permission `module.manage`, held by Staff and the Superadmin). The switch is a row of `module_switches` (a Workspace table); without one, the Module's `onByDefault` applies, to later Sites too. A Module **runs** at a place when it is approved for this version and switched on there; only approved Modules can be switched on. Switching off keeps the Module's data.

**Removing a Module's data** (`modules.removeSiteData`, `modules.removeWorkspaceData`) takes two calls, through `askConfirmation`/`useConfirmation`: the first answers how many rows of each of its tables and how many of its secrets would go, and a confirmation id; the second deletes them (tables in reverse order of the definition's list, so list parents first), removes its secrets there and switches it off. It is logged as `module.removeData`.

### Extension points

- **Tables and migrations.** A Module's tables live in its own Postgres schema, `m_<id>` (dashes become underscores), declared with `moduleSchema(id).table(name, { ...siteColumns(), … })` (or `workspaceColumns()`). A Site table has `workspace_id` and `site_id` with `FOREIGN KEY (workspace_id, site_id) REFERENCES sites (workspace_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE`; a Workspace table `workspace_id` with `REFERENCES workspaces (id) ON DELETE CASCADE`. Site-level Modules have Site tables only. Migrations are SQL in the definition (`{ name, sql }`, names sorting oldest first), run once each by the `migrate` step after WebCraft's own, with the Module's schema first on the search path. The step refuses, rolling everything back: a migration that changes anything outside the Module's schema (compared before and after through Postgres's catalog), changes or reads rows outside it (the transaction's own table statistics and locks, before and after; reading `sites` and `workspaces` and locking them, which foreign keys need, is allowed, changing them isn't), a table or view the Module doesn't declare, a table without its tenant key, a declared table or column the migrations never made (checked on every run, so the TypeScript and the SQL can't drift), and a migration that changed or disappeared after it was applied (by checksum). Never change a shipped migration: add one. The schema check counts pending Module migrations, so web and worker refuse to start until `migrate` ran them.
- **API procedures**, by name and in groups, become `m.<module id>.<name>` in the Management API (all of them public, at `/api/v1/m/<module id>/<name>`; `kind: "read"` (SDK 1.6) marks one that changes nothing, which read-only API tokens may call and which doesn't count as a write) (`adminRouter(services)` in `packages/api/src/module-router.ts`, built from the Install's Modules, served at `/api/rpc` and to the admin's own pages). Each takes `siteId` (Site-level) or `workspaceId` besides its own input, runs only where the Module runs (`MODULE_NOT_FOUND` otherwise), through `can()` with the Permission it names (`FORBIDDEN` with `data.permission`). A `ModuleError` it throws is its answer (`MODULE_ERROR` with its status and `data.code`); anything else is a failure: it goes to the Module log and the API answers `MODULE_FAILED` (500).
- **Permissions** a Module adds are named under its id (`sample.notes.edit`), checked at its level, and held by the Superadmin and the built-in roles it lists (ADR 0028).
- **Site routes** answer `/_wc/m/<route prefix>/<path>` on Sites where the Module runs: declared paths (`/notes`, `/notes/:id`) and methods only (405 with `Allow` for another method on a declared path, HEAD for GET routes), never cached as pages (`Cache-Control: no-store` unless the route sets its own), `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` on Staging addresses like every Site answer. A route never sees WebCraft's own cookies (`webcraft.*`, `isWebCraftCookie` in `packages/core/src/cookies.ts`) and can't set one: name every cookie WebCraft sets on a Site's domain (Edit mode's) `webcraft.<what>`. A route that throws answers the plain 500 and goes to the Module log.
- **Admin pages** (`adminPages: [{ path, label, permission }]`) join the sidebar of the Sites (or Workspaces) where the Module runs, for people holding their Permission (`sites.get` and `workspaces.get` answer them as `modulePages`), at `/sites/<id>/m/<module id>/<path>`. Their screens are registered in the admin's `MODULE_PAGES` (`apps/server/src/ui/modules/pages.tsx`) by the first-party Module that ships one; without one the page says so.
- **Events.** Modules react afterwards, as background jobs (ADR 0024): an Audit log entry the catalogue maps (`entry.publish` → `entry.published` with the Entry's `path` and the `pathChanges` the publish made, `entry.delete` → `entry.deleted`, `template.publish`, `globals.publish`, and, SDK 1.5, `postType.update` and `defaultLanguage.change` → `paths.changed` with `reason` (`urlPattern` or `defaultLanguage`) and their `pathChanges`, when any address moved) queues a `modules.event` job for each Module with a handler, once the change commits. The job runs only if the Module still runs there. Saving and publishing never wait for a Module and never fail because of one.
- **Jobs** of the Module's own (`jobs: { remind: job({ payload, run }) }`), queued with `context.jobs.send(name, payload, { delaySeconds })`, run as `modules.job` in the place they were queued from; payloads are JSON, checked when sent and when run. A job queued inside `context.transaction` is sent once it commits, never after a rollback.
- **Components** (SDK 1.1): `components: [component({ name, label, props, markup })]`, declarative: Props declared as Fields are (labels and help in each admin language; no Relationships, since each Site has its own Post Types) and output in Template markup reading only `props.x` (no Globals, Classes, inline styles or Components, which differ on every Site). Site-level Modules only. The Install checks them when it loads the Module (names unique across Modules); they get the id `<module id>:<Name>`, never change on a Site, and are placed like the Site's own Components, only on Sites where the Module runs (`services.modules.components`, the `ComponentSources` the content store reads). Approving, withdrawing or switching a Module that adds Components clears the page caches it changes; its Placements show nothing where it doesn't run. The consent screen lists them under what it adds. Never rename one that shipped.
- **Scheduled jobs** (SDK 1.2): `schedules: [{ job: "purgeSpam", every: "day" }]` (or `"hour"`) queues the Module's job, with an empty payload, once in every place where the Module runs, from the core jobs `modules.daily` and `modules.hourly` (`services.modules.runSchedules`). The job must take `{}`.
- **Form Submissions** (SDK 1.2): `formSubmissions: submissionReceiver({ receive(submission, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one that keeps the Sites' Form Submissions (at most one Module of a build; a second is `cannot-run`). WebCraft checks a post itself, then hands the `FormSubmission` (Form, language, page, spam reason or null, each field's key, label, type and value) to it inline, before the visitor gets an answer, so nothing is lost; a receiver that throws fails the post and goes to the Module log. Forms render only where such a Module runs (`services.modules.submissionsKeptBy(site)`), and approving, withdrawing or switching it clears the page caches it changes. The consent screen says the Module keeps Form Submissions and what it runs on a schedule. Module form actions (run inline once a Submission is kept) are a seam in the Forms Module's receiver.
- **Site scripts** (SDK 1.3): `siteScript: { code }` adds a script to Site pages where a Site-level Module runs, at most `MODULE_SITE_SCRIPT_MAX_LENGTH` characters; the manifest's `siteScript` names its Consent category (a definition with code and no category is `cannot-run`). The Site serves it as `/_wc/js/<hash>.js` (`services.modules.siteScriptsAt`), pages load it with `defer` after the Site's own scripts, `data-wc-module="<id>"`, held back by the Consent banner until its category is accepted, and never in Edit mode (Modules' code, like outside scripts, isn't Staff's). Switching or approving such a Module clears the page caches it changes.
- **What a Site route learns of the visitor** (SDK 1.4): `context.visitor`, built by the Module host (`modules/visitors.ts`) before WebCraft's cookies are taken off the request, so a Module never sees the address or those cookies: `via` (`staging` or `domain`), `signedIn()` (an edit cookie that holds, or an unexpired admin session from the same client address: judged from what WebCraft keeps, never from what the browser says), `country()` (the countries port), `consent()` (`asks`: `banner`, `none` or `outside`, and `has(category)`, read from the `webcraft.consent` cookie by the Site's mode: Opt-in accepts nothing until answered, Opt-out everything until declined, None everything, and with an outside service WebCraft can't tell), `inRanges(ranges)`, and `dailyId(category, { acceptedInPage })`: an HMAC-SHA256 of the day, the day's salt, the Site, the address and the user agent, under a key derived from the Master key (`visitorIdKey`), only once the visitor accepted the category (with an outside consent service, as the page said). The day's salt (`visitor_salts`) is random, made by the first process that needs it, and deleted the next day (when a new day's salt is made, and by `visitors.purge-salts`), so nobody can tell afterwards whose an id was.
- **Site route limits** (SDK 1.4): `siteRoute({ …, limit: { requests, perSeconds }, maxBodyBytes })`: the host answers 429 with `Retry-After` past the limit, per client address and Site (counted through `rateLimits.count`), and 413 for a bigger body (by its `Content-Length`, then by what comes), before the route runs.
- **Site scripts before consent** (SDK 1.4): `siteScript: { code, beforeConsent: true }` isn't held back by the Consent banner; the page still asks for its category, and the script holds back itself what needs it. The Superadmin approves it as its own consent item (`script:<category>:before-consent`), which the consent screen words as such. A Necessary script can't say it (`cannot-run`).
- **Monthly partitions** (SDK 1.4): `partitions: [{ table, column, label, keepMonths }]` keeps a Module table in monthly partitions by a `timestamp with time zone` column. Its migrations make it `PARTITION BY RANGE (<column>)` with the column in its primary key (the `migrate` step refuses it otherwise, and skips its partitions when it checks the Module's tables); WebCraft makes the partitions (`<table>_<yyyy>_<mm>`, in the Module's schema): the current month and the next two every day (`modules.partitions`), and the month a new row needs at once (`partitions.ts`); and drops a month once all of it is older than the Install keeps it, writing both to the Module log. How many months is an Install setting per table (`module_partition_settings`; the Module's `keepMonths` until changed): `modules.list` answers `partitions`, `modules.setKeepMonths` changes it (`module.approve`, logged `module.changeKeep`), on Install settings › Modules.
- **Counting in Postgres** (SDK 1.4): `context.data.aggregate(table, { where, groupBy, values, orderBy, limit })`, values built from the SDK's aggregates, groups from the table's own columns, limited to the place like every query.
- **SDK 1.6** (the Forms Module's): the `FormSubmission` handed to the receiver carries its `sender` (`FormSender`), which the Module keeps; `context.submissions` (only for the Module keeping Submissions, at a Site; `ModuleRefusedError` with `submissions` otherwise) has `kept(submission)`, which sets off what follows a kept Submission (for one taken out of spam) and `deliveries(id)`, its delivery log; procedures get `context.confirmations` (`ask`, `use`: two-step confirmation, see "Two-step confirmation"; a wrong id answers `CONFIRMATION_INVALID`); selects take an `offset`; and a Site-level Module's `context.email.send` is the Site's mail (through the Site SMTP when it has one, else the Install SMTP under the Site's name; see "Email").
- **`form.submitted`** (SDK 1.4): once a Submission is kept (never spam), Modules reacting to it get the Form's id and key, the page and the language, as a background job, nothing the visitor typed; and `sender` (`FormSender`), what WebCraft knew of the visitor then, worked out from the post (`formSenderOf`, `modules/visitors.ts`), since the request is gone by the time the event runs: `via` (Staging address or domain), `signedIn`, `doNotTrack` (Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control) and `consent` (`asks`, and the categories `accepted`; only `necessary` where an outside consent service asks). Never the address.
- **Redirect rules** (SDK 1.5): `redirectRules: redirectRules({ tables, find(request, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one that keeps the Sites' Redirect rules (at most one Module of a build; a second is `cannot-run`, as is one listing a table that isn't its own). ADR 0024 lets Modules act inline only for form actions and their own routes; a Redirect rule must answer before Entry routing, so this is a third inline place, kept as narrow as the Submission receiver: the Site pipeline asks `find` with a **Site path** (the path as the Staging address writes it, every language but the Default language under its prefix: `fr.example.com/x` asks `/fr/x`) and its language, and gets `{ to, status: 301 | 302, whereNoPage? }` or null. WebCraft turns `to` (a Site path, or an `https://` address; anything else is left out and logged) into the address it has on the visitor's hostname, follows a Site path through the rules again (at most `MAX_REDIRECT_HOPS`, 5, answered in one hop; a loop is never followed: the Site's page answers and the Module log says so once), and skips a `whereNoPage` rule where a page answers. A `find` that throws or takes longer than `FIND_TIMEOUT_MS` (500 ms) is "no rule", in the Module log. Answers are kept per web process with the Site's page cache generation, so each path is asked once until the Site's pages change: any `insert`, `update` or `delete` of the Module on one of `tables` clears the Site's page cache in the same transaction (`moduleData`), and approving, withdrawing or switching the Module clears the page caches it changes. The consent screen says the Module keeps Redirect rules.
- **The 404 log** (SDK 1.5): `notFoundLog: notFoundLog({ record(misses, context) })` makes a Site-level Module the one the Site pipeline hands its misses (at most one of a build). The web process gathers them in memory (`services.modules.noteMiss`, one entry per Site path, language and referring host, with hits and first and last times; at most 5,000 held) and hands them over every 5 seconds (a timer in `web-services.ts`), at once past 500 (`MISS_BATCH_SIZE`), as `modules.notFound` jobs, one per Site where the Module runs (`flushMisses`); `record` runs in the worker like any Module job. A miss on its way when a web process stops is lost: the log is a guide. The consent screen says the Module keeps the 404 log.
- **Import sources** (SDK 1.7): `importSources: [importSource({ key, label, description, permission, file: { maxBytes, extensions }, read(file, context) })]` (Site-level Modules only) adds a source to the Import pipeline, `<module id>.<key>`, offered on the Source step to people holding `import.run` and the source's `permission`, where the Module runs. The Module only reads its file: `read` gets a `SourceFile` whose `text()` streams the file as UTF-8 from the start (as often as it is called: a long run reads it again in each job) and yields `SourceItem`s in the order they are to be written: a `type` before its first item, `entry` items (a Source id, title, slug, parent, old path and values by key, each `text`, `html`, `date`, `file` or `items`, and what it knows of the files its HTML names) and `media` items (a file to fetch with its texts). A `ModuleError` it throws refuses the file. WebCraft does the rest (see "Imports"). The consent screen says what the Module imports from.
- **Redirect rules for Imports** (SDK 1.7): the Module keeping Redirect rules may add `add(rules, context)` and `remove(rules, context)` (both or neither) to its `redirectRules`, for the rules an Import run makes and Undo removes; a rule's target may also be `media:<id>` (see "Redirects and the 404 log").
- **Widgets** (SDK 1.8): `widgets: [widget({ kind, label, description, shape, filters, settings, query })]` adds kinds to the catalog Dashboards are built from (Site-level Modules only: a Widget counts one Site at a time; WebCraft adds Sites up for Workspace Dashboards). `shape` is one the admin draws with its own pieces: `number` (named counts, a rate as `count` and `of`), `series` (lines of counts by hour or day), `table` (columns of a kind: `text`, `path`, `source`, `country`, `device`, `count`, `rate`; rows keyed across Sites) or `funnel` (steps' counts). `filters` lists those it takes (`page`, `source`, `country`, `device`, `goal`; `goal` needs `widgetGoals(context)`, the Site's Goals' names), `settings` its choices (fixed options, or options worked out per Site; values naming things Staff made by name, so templates carry them). `query(request, context)` gets the range, interval, filters, settings and row limit and answers counts only, never ratios, plus `countedFrom` when its counts start later than the range; it runs as the Module acting by itself on a Site where it runs, and a `ModuleError` it throws is shown in the Widget. The registry checks kinds (named once, lower-case words), labels in every language, shapes, filters and settings (defaults it offers, none for options worked out per Site).
- **SDK 1.8 also**: `context.data.values(table, column, where)`, a subquery of one column's values in the place, for `inArray` (Postgres reads the ids, they never travel to the Module); and `form.submitted`'s `visitor`, the sender's daily id when they accepted Analytics on the Site's banner and analytics may count them (null otherwise, and for a Submission taken out of spam later), never kept with the Submission.
- **Seams for later tickets**: `structurePresets` (#110) and `blockTypes` are declared in the definition type and refused until their SDK version.

### What Module code gets

Procedures, Site routes, events and jobs get a `ModuleContext` (`packages/core/src/modules/runtime.ts`), limited to one place and to what the manifest declares; anything else throws `ModuleRefusedError` with a `reason` (`permission`, `table`, `host`, `use`, `job`, `secret`, `email`):

- `data`: its own tables in the place, through the data layer (`select`, `first`, `count`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`, `aggregate`, and `values` for a subquery, SDK 1.8); the Workspace and Site are filled in, and any other table is refused. `transaction(work)` runs table changes and Audit log entries together.
- `audit(action, details)`: recorded under the Module's id. In a procedure the person acted (`via: admin`); elsewhere the Module did (`via: module`, no person).
- `email.send(message)` (needs `uses: ["email"]`): the `module-message` System email (a work email people can turn off), in the Module's words for each recipient's Admin language (else its first), in the built-in layout, recorded in the Email log.
- `http(url, options)`: the guarded fetcher, to its declared hosts only. When a call fails the Module gets WebCraft's own error (`GuardedFetchError` with its `problem`, or `ModuleRefusedError`), never the error behind it, which could name the address.
- `secrets`: `set`, `describe`, `remove` its own secrets in the place (stored as `module:<id>:<name>`), and `placeholder(name)`, which WebCraft replaces with the value inside an outgoing call (URL, header or body) to a declared host, percent-encoded in the URL (its path and query) so no character of it can change the address; Module code never sees a value, and a call carrying a secret never follows a redirect.
- `jobs.send`, `log.info/warn/error` (the Module log), `now()`, `module`, `place`, `submissions` (SDK 1.6, the Module keeping Submissions only); a procedure also gets `person`, `can(permission)` and `confirmations` (SDK 1.6).

When a Module acts by itself it is a `ModuleActor` for `can()`, opening its place through the data layer like anyone.

### The Module log

`module_log` keeps what Modules wrote and every failure while they ran (a procedure, Site route, event or job that threw, an event that couldn't be queued), with the Workspace, Site and details (never a stack or a secret), 90 days (`modules.purge-log`, daily). The Superadmin reads it on Install settings › Modules (`modules.log`). A failed event or job is tried twice more; a refusal is logged and never retried, since the Module must change.

### Testing Modules

`createTestInstall({ modules: [sample, sampleBoard] })` loads Modules (this build's first-party Modules unless given); every test database has had `TEST_MODULES`' migrations (`tests/harness/modules.ts`: the first-party Modules and `SAMPLE_MODULES`). `tests/modules/first-party.test.ts` fails when a first-party Module can't run or its migrations break the step's rules. The isolation guard (`tests/isolation/procedures.test.ts`) lists every test Module's procedures too: add a line for each procedure a first-party Module adds. `moduleApi<typeof sample>(browser, "sample")` (`tests/harness/modules.ts`) calls a Module's procedures, typed from its definition (`ModuleApi` in the SDK). `install.restart({ modules })` runs another version; `install.migrate()` runs the `migrate` step. Tests are in `tests/modules/`.

## The Management API and API tokens

The Management API (ADR 0029, spec › Management API) is every procedure of `packages/api/src/router.ts` and the Install's Modules' (`adminRouter(services)`). The admin calls them all at `/api/rpc`; the public ones are also served to API tokens at `/api/v1` (`createPublicApi`, `packages/api/src/public.ts`), which the MCP server (#106) and the CLI (#107) are generated from (ADR 0030).

- **Paths** (ADR 0034): a public procedure answers `POST /api/v1/<router path with slashes>` (`/api/v1/entries/publish`, `/api/v1/media/folders/list`), through oRPC's OpenAPI handler: the input as a JSON body (multipart form data when it holds a file: `media.upload`, `media.replace`, `imports.uploadCsv`), dates as ISO strings (read back through the input's schema), the answer as plain JSON, a refusal as `{ code, status, message, data }` with its status. Module procedures are all public, at `/api/v1/m/<module id>/<name>`, and say whether they read or write (`kind`, SDK 1.6; a write unless `read`).
- **Which are public**: `PROCEDURES` in `packages/api/src/catalogue.ts` lists every procedure, `internal()` or a public `read(summary)` / `write(summary)`; the typecheck fails while one is missing. Internal: the setup wizard, signing in, the shell, a person's own account and API tokens (`me.*`, `apiTokens.*`), Install settings, `workspaces.create`, Staff, invite links, the Audit log so far, approving Modules and their log, Edit mode's link, the visual builder's own procedures (`builder.*`: scripts use the Template markup procedures) and `media.suggestAltText`. Everything Install-level is internal, since a token acts within one Workspace.
- **The OpenAPI document**: `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` (open to anyone; OpenAPI 3.1, `servers: [{ url: "/api/v1" }]`, bearer auth). Each operation's id is the dotted path (`entries.publish`), tagged by its group (`m.<id>` for a Module's), with its summary and input schema, and WebCraft's marks: `x-webcraft-access` (`read`, `write`), `x-webcraft-sensitive` (the Sensitive action, never callable with a token; read from the procedure's `sensitive(…)`), `x-webcraft-confirmation` (it takes a `confirmation`: two steps). The document of a build with its first-party Modules is checked in at `docs/api/openapi.json`; `bun run api:document` writes it again, so every change to the public API shows in review. How scripts call it: `docs/api/README.md`.
- **API tokens** (`packages/core/src/api-tokens.ts`, `services.apiTokens`; tables `api_tokens`, `api_token_sites`; procedures `apiTokens.list`, `create`, `revoke`; the admin's Your account › API tokens): a person makes one for one Workspace they reach, every Site of it (later ones too) or chosen ones they see, optionally read-only, lasting 30, 90 (the default) or 365 days or never. Creating is a Sensitive action (`apiToken.create`), logged in the Workspace. The token, `webcraft_` and 43 random base64url characters, is answered once; only an HMAC-SHA256 of it is kept (a key derived from the Master key, `apiTokenKey`), with its last 4 characters as a hint, so a request finds it with one indexed lookup and a new Master key ends every token, as it signs everyone out. Revoking (`apiToken.revoke`) stops it at once; rows stay, so a script is told its token was revoked.
- **A request** carries `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (cookies are never read at `/api/v1`). `services.apiTokens.use` checks it (401 `TOKEN_INVALID`, `data.problem`: `missing`, `unknown`, `expired`, `revoked`, or `locked` with `Retry-After` while its person's account is locked) and answers `access`: a `TokenActor`, the person's access read fresh, limited to the token's Workspace and Sites, `via: { kind: "token", name }`. Procedures then run as for anyone (`signedIn` takes `context.token`); `context.person` is the token's person and `context.session` null.
- **Losing access**: removing a Member, changing their Sites and changing Staff's Workspaces revoke, in the same transaction, the person's tokens whose Workspace (or every listed Site) they no longer reach (`revokeTokensOutOfReach`, logged `apiToken.revoke` with `reason: "access"`); a later Membership never brings one back. `use` checks the same on every request and revokes a token there too. A role change or fewer Sites take effect on the next request through `can()`. Deleting a person or a Workspace deletes their tokens.
- **Limits**: per token, 300 requests a minute and, of them, 30 writes (`API_TOKEN_LIMITS`, counted with `rateLimits.tally` under `api-token:<id>:requests` and `:writes`, so the counters hold a keyed hash, never the token); past either, 429 `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` with `Retry-After` and `data.limit`. Every answer carries `RateLimit-Limit`, `RateLimit-Remaining` and `RateLimit-Reset` (seconds) of the limit closest to running out. A read-only token calling a write gets 403 `TOKEN_READ_ONLY` before it runs; an internal or unknown path 404 `NOT_FOUND`.
- **Never for a token**: Sensitive actions (`SENSITIVE_ACTION_REFUSED`, ADR 0030) and Install-level procedures (internal). Publishing and deleting Structure take two steps (see "Two-step confirmation").
- **Seams**: OAuth grants (#106) are one more `Actor` kind for `isApiClient` and the same `/api/v1`-style checks; Toolsets on tokens come with them.

## Signing in and the setup wizard

- **`@webcraft/auth`** is the only way into sign-in: `services.auth.signIn`, `session(headers)`, `signOut`, `createPerson` (inside the caller's transaction). Better Auth 1.7.18 (pinned in the root `package.json` catalog) sits behind it in `packages/auth/src/auth.ts`; no other package imports it. Its own HTTP endpoints are not served: WebCraft's procedures call it. When you upgrade it, read its security advisories and keep the version exact.
- **People** are the `people` table; their password hash is a row in `credentials`. Install-level roles are text keys in `install_roles` (`superadmin` and `staff`, ADR 0028; see "Tenancy and permissions"). Emails are stored in lower case.
- **Passwords** need at least 12 characters (`assertNewPasswordAllowed`). Extra checks plug in as `passwordChecks` in `createAuth`; `createInstallServices` plugs in the leaked-password check.
- **The leaked-password check** (`leakedPasswordCheck` in `packages/auth/src/leaked-passwords.ts`) checks every new password (new accounts, password changes) with a partial-hash lookup through `services.http`: only the first 5 characters of the password's SHA-1 hash leave the server (`GET <range service>/range/<prefix>` with `Add-Padding: true`), and the password is refused (`PASSWORD_REFUSED`, `leaked`) when the answer lists the rest of its hash as seen at least once. It fails open: when the service can't be reached, times out (5 seconds) or answers with an error, the password is accepted and a warning is logged. The Superadmin switches it off in Install settings › Security (`install.security.update`, a Sensitive action), for an Install that is always offline.
- **Sessions** end after 7 days unused and 30 days after sign-in, by WebCraft's clock (`services.clock`), so tests move them with `install.clock.advance`. `auth.activeSession(id)` reads one by its id, without renewing it, for checks away from the admin's hostname (Edit mode). A session in use is renewed at most every 10 minutes, and records the client address it was used from. The cookie is `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Secure` (except on a `.localhost` development host), signed with a key derived from the Master key: a new Master key signs everyone out.
- **Your sessions** (Your account › Sessions): `me.sessions.list` shows each session's browser and system (from its `User-Agent`), its network (the client address with the end hidden, `203.0.113.*`; no outside location lookup, which would send people's addresses to another company) and when it was last used. `me.sessions.revoke` signs one out at once (this browser's own one too, clearing its cookie), `me.sessions.revokeOthers` every other one. `me.changePassword` (a Sensitive action) signs out every other session in the same transaction; changing 2FA does the same with `auth.revokeOtherSessions`.
- **Lockout** (`packages/auth/src/lockout.ts`): 10 wrong passwords in a row, signing in or confirming who you are, lock the account for 15 minutes. While it is locked nothing is checked, not even the right password, and trying doesn't make the lock longer; a right password, or the lock, starts the count again. Addresses nobody signs in with never lock. The answer is `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` (423) with `retryAfterSeconds` and a `Retry-After` header. When an account locks, `onAccountLocked` sends the `account-locked` security email and writes `account.lock` to the Audit log with the address of the last attempt.
- **Rate limits** on the auth endpoints (signing in, confirming who you are, setup tokens, invite links): at most 10 requests a minute from one client address, counted in Postgres (`services.rateLimits`, `RATE_LIMITS` in `packages/core/src/rate-limits.ts`) so every web process shares the count; then `TOO_MANY_REQUESTS` (429) with `Retry-After` until the minute is over, and one warning in the log. Requests whose address the web process can't tell share one count. Features with limits of their own count under keys they make with `rateLimits.count(key, rule)`, which answers whether the request is within the limit (Forms' per-address limits, Consent records, Modules' Site routes' `limit`), or `rateLimits.tally(key, rule)`, which also answers what is left and when the window ends (API tokens' limits). A key names a client address, which is never kept: `rate_limits.key` holds an HMAC-SHA256 of it under a key derived from the Master key (`rateLimitKey`). The `rate-limits.purge` job deletes ended counters every day. Better Auth's own rate limiter stays off: its endpoints aren't served.
- **The setup token**: while the Install has no Superadmin, every start of the web process prints a new one-time token and a `setupUrl` in the log (`warn`, fields `setupToken` and `setupUrl`). Only its SHA-256 hash is stored; the last 10 printed stay valid. Claiming the Install with one creates the Superadmin and deletes every token. Creating the Superadmin is serialised with a Postgres advisory lock, across processes.
- **`webcraft admin create-superadmin <email> [--name <name>] [--language <en|it>]`** does the same headless: the password is asked twice on a terminal, or read from the first line of standard input (`printf '%s\n' "$PASSWORD" | webcraft admin create-superadmin ops@agency.example`); it is refused on the command line. It needs the Install's environment (`DATABASE_URL`, `WEBCRAFT_ADMIN_HOST`, `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`) and finishes setup, so the wizard never opens.
- **Setup stages** (`services.setup.stage()`): `claim` (no Superadmin: the wizard asks for the token), `continue` (the Superadmin exists and goes through the wizard's remaining steps, signed in), `done` (the wizard answers 404 for good).
- **The Master key banner** shows to the Superadmin until they confirm the running Master key is stored outside the server. The confirmation stores who, when and the key's fingerprint, and is logged; with another key the banner returns.

## The admin

The admin is a TanStack Start app in `apps/server/src/routes` (file routes) and `apps/server/src/ui` (everything else). It reads and changes data only through the Management API: `getApi()` (`ui/api.ts`) calls the procedures in-process while a page renders on the server, and `/api/rpc` in the browser.

- **Shell context**: the root route loads `shell.context` on every navigation (who is signed in, Admin language, theme, setup stage, banners); routes read it with `Route.useRouteContext()`. After a change that affects it, call `router.invalidate()` or navigate.
- **Text**: every word people see comes from `apps/server/src/messages/en.json` and `it.json`, through `useT()` (`t("shell.nav.overview")`, with ICU placeholders such as `{name}`). Add each key to both files; a missing key fails the typecheck, and `tests/i18n/admin-messages.test.ts` checks that every language has every key with the same placeholders. Page titles come from `pageTitle(language, key)` in the route's `head`.
- **Right to left**: `<html lang dir>` follows the Admin language (`ADMIN_LANGUAGES` in `@webcraft/core/admin-languages`). Use logical classes (`ms-`, `me-`, `ps-`, `pe-`, `start-`, `end-`, `text-start`, `border-s`) and flip direction icons with `rtl:rotate-180`.
- **Light and dark**: `<html class="light|dark">` when a person chose, no class to follow the device (`prefers-color-scheme`); the `dark:` variant and the colour variables in `packages/ui/src/styles/globals.css` handle all three.
- **The top bar**: on a Site's pages, "Staging" (opens the Staging address in a new tab through `staging.open`, `ui/shell/StagingButton.tsx`), "Edit on site" (`entry.edit`) and Publish all with how many Drafts the person may publish (`entry.publish` or `structure.edit`; the count is `place.drafts`, loaded with the Site; `ui/publishing/PublishAll.tsx`, opened at once by `?publishAll=1`). The Staging address's "Sign in" goes to `/staging/<site id>?path=…`, which opens it at that page once the person is signed in.
- **Levels and the sidebar**: `ui/shell/levels.ts` holds each level's sidebar groups and items (`label`, `icon`, `to`, `requires`: the Permission that shows it). A group can carry `requires` too: the Site level's Structure group requires `structure.edit`, so Members see Content items only (and its `note` says "Staff only"). The Site level's groups are Content (Overview, Dashboards where the person sees one (`place.dashboards`, as at the Workspace level), Pages, each of the Site's Post Types, Media, Globals, Imports for `import.run`: the Post Types come from `place.postTypes`), Structure (Post Types & Fields, Libraries) and Site settings (Domains & Staging, Languages, SEO, Consent banner, Email (SMTP), Webhooks, Outside scripts, Modules); each Form's item follows Media in Content (see "Forms"); Module pages follow. The Site's `place` also carries `modulePermissions`: the Permissions Modules running there add that the person holds (`sites.get` answers them), for screens a Module's Permission opens, such as a Form's Submissions. The levels are the Install, a Workspace and a Site: pages under `routes/_admin/workspaces.$workspaceId.tsx` are at the Workspace level and pages under `routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.tsx` at the Site level. Those two routes load the Workspace or Site and the person's Permissions there, and set `place` in the route context; the sidebar, the breadcrumbs (the Workspace's and Site's names first) and the context switcher read it. A page reads `Route.useRouteContext().place.permissions` to decide what to offer, never a role name. A page sets its breadcrumb with `staticData: { crumb: "<message key>" }`, or, when its name is data (a Post Type's name, an Entry's title), with `crumb` in what its loader answers; `pageTitleOf` makes such a page's `<title>`.
- **Pages** inside the shell are routes under `routes/_admin/` (signed in only). Start a page with `PageHeader`; use the shadcn components from `@webcraft/ui/components/*`.
- **The setup wizard's steps** are the list in `ui/setup/steps.tsx`: each has a title, a hint, a stage and a panel. After `two-factor` comes `email` (the Install SMTP, which can be skipped), then `modules` (approving the first-party Modules, ticked). The last step finishes setup.
- **Structure screens for the builder**: Site › Templates (`sites.$siteId.templates.tsx`) and Site › Components (`sites.$siteId.components.tsx`) open the visual builder, which is full-screen, outside the shell (`routes/builder.$siteId.$.tsx`, see "The visual builder").
- **Content screens** (`ui/content/`): Post Types & Fields (`routes/_admin/sites.$siteId.post-types*.tsx`, Structure: `structure.edit`; a Post Type other than Pages and the Globals have a Data tab), Loops (`sites.$siteId.loops*.tsx`, Structure: `LoopEditor.tsx` with the query builder, the Loop items' Fields and values, and the Data tab), each Post Type's Entries and the Entry editor (`sites.$siteId.entries.$postTypeId*.tsx`) and the Globals editor (`sites.$siteId.globals.tsx`).
  - **Tabs** (`ui/Tabs.tsx`): the ARIA tabs pattern (one tab stop, the arrow keys, Home and End), panels kept mounted once shown; screens keep the open tab in the address (`?tab=data`), so a check can open it.
  - **The Data tab** (`DataTab.tsx`): a real item's values from the Draft, as JSON in a read-only text area (reached, scrolled and selected from the keyboard), and the bindings that read each, each with a Copy button that says what it copied in a live region (the Clipboard API, else the copy command).
  - **Field rules in the browser**: the Field types, their traits (translatable by default, can be required, can have a default) and limits come from `@webcraft/core/field-rules`, which imports nothing, so the admin reads the same rules the content store checks. Never import `@webcraft/core` itself into browser code (types only).
  - **One control per Field type** in `FieldValues.tsx` (`FieldValue`, used by the Entry editor, the Globals editor and a Field's default), and one set of settings per type in `FieldSettingsPanel.tsx`. A new Field type adds its control and its settings there; `offeredTypes` (`FieldBuilder.tsx`) decides which types a new Field can be where it is (a Body field only on a Post Type that has none, at the top). Media Fields choose files through `MediaPicker` (`ui/media/`, built on the Media library's preview pieces in `ui/media/preview.ts`); rich text is edited in `RichTextEditor`, which cleans pasted HTML with the same cleaner the server uses (`@webcraft/renderer/rich-text`).
  - **The Field builder** (`FieldBuilder.tsx`, `StructureEditor.tsx`) keeps Fields as drafts (`field-drafts.ts`) until the screen saves the whole list (`postTypes.update`, `globals.setFields`); a problem the server answers (`fields[2].fields[0].key`) is located on its Field and setting, and shown there (in the server's words) and in a list above the Fields.
  - **Drafts save by themselves** through `useAutosave` (`autosave.ts`): a moment after typing pauses, the changes (merged by value) go to `entries.saveDraft` or `globals.saveDraft`, one save at a time; a refused change is kept and sent again with the next one, leaving the page saves what is waiting, and the browser asks before a tab with unsaved changes closes. Co-editing (#71) replaces this with the shared document. A save that loses a race shows `CHANGED_MEANWHILE`'s words with Try again and Reload.
  - **Problems in words**: `problemText` (`problems.ts`) words every rule code (`VALUE_RULES`) from the Field's settings, and values refused as the wrong kind by what they are; `errorMessage` (`ui/errors.ts`) words the content error codes (`ENTRY_*`, `GLOBALS_*`, `PATH_TAKEN`, `PAGE_*`, `POST_TYPE_*`, `CONFIRMATION_INVALID` by its `problem`); `settingProblemText` words a refused setting from its `SETTING_RULES` code (`content.settingProblems.*`, with Field and Block types by their names), falling back to the server's English for a code it doesn't know.
  - **The block editor** (`ui/content/body/`): a Body field's Blocks, each edited in place, in our own small editor over the Block JSON (no editor library: the same contenteditable approach as `RichTextEditor`). `InlineEditor` is one piece of formatted text (the browser types into it; `inline-dom.ts` reads it back as runs and places the caret by character); `BodyEditor` holds the Blocks and the keys between them (Enter splits, Backspace at the start joins or turns a Block back into a paragraph, the arrow keys cross Blocks, Ctrl/⌘ with B, I, E, K and Shift+X format, Alt+Shift with the arrows moves a Block, Ctrl/⌘ with `]` and `[` nest list items; Tab always leaves); `BlockMenu` is the `/` menu and Add Block (a combobox over a list box, filtered by what the Field allows, `choices.ts`). Markdown shortcuts turn a paragraph into a Block as it is typed (`## `, `### `, `#### `, `- `, `* `, `1. `, `> `, then ```` ``` ```` and `---` on their own). Checks are worked out in the browser with the renderer's `bodyChecks` and shown, with the server's problems, under their Block.
  - **Every drag has a keyboard way**: lists that drag by a handle (`useDragOrder` in `reorder.tsx`) also offer Move up and Move down in each item's menu, and say where the item went (`Announcer`).
  - **Languages**: `sites.get` answers the Site's `languages` (`{ default, all }`, the Default language first), carried in the Site's `place`; the language tabs of the editors and the chips of the Entries list show them all.
- **Times** are shown in UTC with `formatUtc`, and days with `formatUtcDate` (`ui/format.ts`), in one fixed form: the server renders pages first, and its date formatting differs from the browser's in small ways, which breaks hydration.
- **shadcn components** come from preset `b1Fk3bmIS` (`style: base-vega`, zinc, RTL on, see `packages/ui/components.json`). Add one with `bunx shadcn add <name>` in `packages/ui`, then run `bun run lint`: generated code sometimes fails the accessibility rules; fix the component (several are marked `WebCraft:`) rather than switching a rule off, and give every visible or screen-reader string a prop so it can be translated.

## Testing

Tests live in `tests/`, grouped by area, and run with `bun run test` from the repo root. They test at the spec's seams and check what a person, visitor or client would observe: HTTP responses, emails that went out, outgoing calls, job outcomes and later responses. They never import internal functions or read tables directly; refactoring without changing behaviour must not break a test.

**Seam 1** is the web process's handler, booted in-process against a real Postgres. `createTestInstall()` from `tests/harness/index.ts` gives each test file its own Install:

| Part | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `install.secrets` | Every secret of the Install (`set`, `useWith`, `describe`, `remove`), for any owner, encrypted under its Master key: for tests of the secret store. WebCraft's own code reaches a Workspace's or Site's secrets through the data layer. |
| `install.env` | The environment a real process needs to run as this Install: `DATABASE_URL`, `WEBCRAFT_ADMIN_HOST` and `WEBCRAFT_SECRET_KEY`. |
| `install.request(url, init)` | Sends a standard `Request` through the handler, in process; a `Request` given keeps the client address `fromAddress` gave it. Always use a full URL: the hostname decides where it goes. `install.adminUrl("/healthz")` builds admin-host URLs (`https://admin.test/...`); a Site's Staging address is `https://<slug>.sites.test` (`sites.get` answers it as `stagingUrl`), which shows Drafts to the people reviewing it only: for what visitors see, put the Site live first (`goLive`, below). |
| `install.browser()` | A person's browser on the admin: it keeps cookies and sends the admin's origin. `browser.api` is the typed Management API client (`await browser.api.session.current()`); errors reject with their API `code`. `install.browser({ origin })` sends requests from another site's pages. Each browser comes from its own client address (`browser.address`, which a test can change); give `{ address }` to choose one (null: one the web process can't tell), `{ userAgent }` for the browser it says it is, and `{ headers }` for headers on every request (a proxy's `X-Forwarded-For`, a CDN's visitor header, or a client's forgery of them). |
| `install.createSuperadmin(account?)` | Creates the Superadmin as `webcraft admin create-superadmin` does and answers the account (email, password `TEST_PASSWORD`). |
| `install.superadmin()` | A browser signed in as the Superadmin (made first when there is none). |
| `install.createWorkspace(name?)`, `install.createSite(workspaceId, { name?, slug? })` | A Workspace or Site, created by the Superadmin through the API. |
| `install.createStaff({ workspaces })` | A Staff member, the way people become one: invited, then accepting the link in the email. Answers `{ account, personId, browser }`. |
| `install.createMember({ workspaceId, role, sites? })` | A Member the way people become one: the Superadmin invites them and they accept the link with a new account, signed in. `install.addMembership` gives an existing person a Membership directly, for tests of what a Membership may never be. |
| `install.signIn(account, browser?)` | A new browser, signed in; `browser` takes the same options as `install.browser()`. |
| `install.setupToken()` | The setup token the web process printed when it last started (the harness starts like the web process, printing one while there's no Superadmin). |
| `install.jobs.send(job, payload)` | Enqueues a job as the web process would. |
| `install.drainJobs()` | First hands the 404 log's misses the Site pipeline gathered to the job queue (`services.modules.flushMisses`), then runs every job that is due by the test clock, including jobs those jobs enqueue and scheduled jobs whose time came, and returns `{ completed, failed }`. `drainJobs({ only: ["media.process"] })` runs only those jobs, leaving the rest queued (to see what visitors get between two steps). |
| `install.lastEmailTo(address)` | Runs the due jobs, then answers the newest email to the address: sent through the Install SMTP or, while it isn't set, written to the log (`{ to, subject, text }`). `inviteLinkIn(email)` finds an invite link in one. |
| `install.sendSystemEmail(email)` | Sends a System email as WebCraft's features do (`services.email.send`); drain the jobs to send it. |
| `install.mail` | The fake Mailer: `sent` holds every email the server took (with the `smtp` settings it went through, password included), `attempts` every try; `fail(response)` makes every send fail with that answer until `accept()`; `rejectRecipient(address)` refuses one address for good; `clear()` forgets them. |
| `install.storageDir` | The Install's local-disk storage folder (`WEBCRAFT_STORAGE_DIR`): a new temporary folder per test Install, removed by `close()`. Its layout (`<workspace id>/<site id>/media/…`) is what Superadmins back up and mount, so tests may look at it. |
| `createFakeS3(install.http, settings)` | A fake S3-compatible bucket on the fake outgoing HTTP (`tests/harness/fake-s3.ts`): it keeps objects, answers ranges, takes multipart uploads (`openUploads` counts those neither finished nor aborted), and checks every request's Signature Version 4 with its own implementation, refusing a wrongly signed one like a real bucket. |
| `install.http` | The fake outgoing HTTP: `respond(method, url or RegExp, { status, body, headers } or a function)`; bodies may be text or bytes, and each recorded request has `body` (text), `bytes` and `address`: the address it connected to, the one the caller pinned it to (`connectTo`) or else the first `install.dns` answers at that moment, as a runtime's `fetch` resolves the name again; `requests` holds every call. A request nobody prepared an answer for fails like an unreachable server. The leaked-password range service (`LEAKED_PASSWORD_RANGE`) answers that no password is leaked unless a test answers it otherwise; `reset()` forgets that answer too, so the lookup is then unreachable. |
| `install.clock` | Time stands still until `advance(ms)` or `set(date)`. The job queue reads the same time inside Postgres, so a job sent with `startAfter` in an hour runs only after `advance` passes it, and retries wait for their delay. `advance` jumps like a process that slept, so moving days ahead is quick. Sessions expire by this clock too. Starts at `2026-03-02T09:00:00Z` unless you pass `{ now }`. |
| `install.logs` | Every log entry the Install wrote. |
| `install.tablesHolding(text)` | The tables anywhere in the Install's database (WebCraft's, Modules', the job queue's) holding `text` in a row: for what must never be kept, such as a visitor's address. It reads no table's layout. |
| `install.dns` | The fake DNS the guarded fetcher asks: `answer(name, addresses)`; any other name doesn't resolve. `answerInTurn(name, [first, second, …])` gives one answer per lookup, then keeps giving the last (DNS rebinding). `lookups` holds every name asked. The admin host resolves to the Install's addresses (`TEST_INSTALL_ADDRESSES`, documentation addresses `203.0.113.10` and `2001:db8::10`, also its `WEBCRAFT_PUBLIC_ADDRESSES`). |
| `install.ai` | The fake AI providers (`InstallPorts.ai`): `answer(text or a function of the request)`, `fail(problem?)`; `requests` holds every call, key included. Unavailable until a test answers. |
| `install.tls` | The fake TLS the Domain check asks: `answer(name, { expiresAt, trusted? })`, or `null` for none; any other name answers with no certificate. `probes` holds every name asked. |
| `install.connectDomain(name, { certificate? })` | Points a domain at the Install, as its DNS records would: it resolves to the Install's first address, every `http(s)://<name>/…` request the Install makes reaches its own handler, and it answers HTTPS with a certificate valid for 90 more days (or the one given; null for none). Move the clock a minute and drain the jobs to have it verified (`tests/domains/`). |
| `install.restart({ masterKey?, modules? })` | Starts the Install again, as web and worker do (with another Master key, or other Modules, such as a new version of one, when given), running their start-up checks and printing a new setup token while there's no Superadmin. |
| `install.migrate({ modules?, backup? })` | Runs the `migrate` step on the Install's database as `webcraft admin migrate` does, with the Install's Modules (or the ones given), and answers what it did (`appliedMigrations`, `createdQueues`, `backup`). Without `backup: true` it skips the Backup, as `--no-backup` does. |
| `install.backup({ to? })` | Takes a Backup as `webcraft admin backup` does: where Install settings › Backups says, or into the file or folder `to`. |
| `install.restore(archive, { replace? })` | Restores a Backup into the Install's database as `webcraft admin restore` does (with the Install's Master key, storage and the test suite's Modules), then starts the Install on it with a job queue, as web and worker would after it. `replace: { confirm }` is `--force` with the database's name. |
| `install.checkUpdate()` | Reads the release feed now, as `webcraft admin check-update` does. |
| `install.backupDir` | The Install's Backups folder (`WEBCRAFT_BACKUP_DIR`): a new temporary folder per test Install, removed by `close()`. |
| `install.close()` | Stops the Install and drops its database. |

`createTestInstall({ database: "empty" })` gives an Install whose database nobody migrated, and `{ database: "unreachable" }` one whose database doesn't answer. Every test Install starts with the Master key `TEST_MASTER_KEY` unless you pass `{ masterKey }`; tests never use the key in `.env`. Its sites domain is `sites.test` (`TEST_SITES_DOMAIN`) unless you pass `{ sitesDomain }` (`null` for none), its admin host `admin.test` unless you pass `{ adminHost }`, its edge `caddy` unless you pass `{ edge }`, its public addresses `TEST_INSTALL_ADDRESSES` unless you pass `{ publicAddresses }`, and `WEBCRAFT_TRUSTED_PROXIES` unset (the edge's default: test browsers' own addresses, `10.x.y.z`, are private, so they count as trusted proxies, which matters only when a test sends `X-Forwarded-For`) unless you pass `{ trustedProxies }`. Import from URL waits 30 seconds unless you pass `{ mediaImportTimeoutMs }` (to test a slow server without waiting). The update check reads `DEFAULT_UPDATE_FEED` unless you pass `{ updateFeed }` (`null` for `off`); nothing answers it unless a test does (`install.dns.answer("api.github.com", […])` and `install.http.respond("GET", DEFAULT_UPDATE_FEED, …)`). It loads no Modules unless you pass `{ modules }` (this build has no first-party Modules yet); every test database has had the migrations of the test suite's Modules (`TEST_MODULES` in `tests/harness/modules.ts`).

`tests/harness/images.ts` makes small images inside the tests (never files copied from anywhere): `photo({ width, height, format, orientation, camera })` (red left half, blue right half, so turning it shows; `camera: true` adds a camera make and GPS data), `heicPhoto` (through `sips` on macOS or `heif-enc` where libheif's tools are installed, as CI does; `canMakeHeic` says whether this machine can), `pdf()`, `upload(bytes, name, type)` for a browser-like `File`, and `imageInfo` / `pixel` to look at what the Install serves.

`tests/harness/staging.ts` is the Staging address as the people reviewing a Site use it, and a Site's visitors: `goLive(install, siteId)` puts a Site on its Primary domain `<slug>.live.test`, verified at once (as "Check now" does), and answers its origin; tests of what visitors see (published pages, the page cache, files, Form posts, Consent records, Modules' routes) request it there, since the Staging address shows Drafts to reviewers only. `openStaging(install, adminBrowser, siteId, path)` opens the Staging address from the admin (`staging.open`) in a new browser holding the Staging cookie (`page(path)` loads its pages); `stagingVisitor(install, slug)` is a browser it doesn't know yet, and `givePassword(visitor, password, path)` gives the Site's staging password on its page. The Staging address's own tests are in `tests/staging/`, Publish all's in `tests/publishing/`.

`tests/harness/code-mode.ts` turns Code mode on in an Edit mode browser (`openCodeMode`, then `edit("code/open", …)` and the other `code/…` requests) and reads Code mode's tags in a page (`codeTagsIn`). `tests/harness/edit-mode.ts` opens Edit mode as a browser does: `openEditMode(install, adminBrowser, siteId, path)` asks for the link with `editMode.open`, follows it in a new browser on the Site's origin and loads the page; `page(path)` loads pages with the edit cookie, `edit(action, body, options)` sends edit requests as the editing bar does (`options` drop the header token or send another origin, to test refusals). A test browser sends `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` for requests to its own origin. `openEditMode(…, path, { safeMode: true })` follows an "Edit in Safe mode" link.

`tests/harness/seo.ts` reads what a page tells search engines as a crawler does (`titleIn`, `metaName`, `metaProperty`, `canonicalIn`, `jsonLdNode`), and `withoutStructuredData` sets a page's JSON-LD block aside for checks that a page holds no `<script>` at all; the SEO tests are in `tests/seo/`. `tests/harness/site-safety.ts` holds the one check every Site test uses on pages: `expectSafePage(response, policy)` checks the fixed security headers, the policy the page is under (`strict`, `relaxed`, `reportOnly` or `editMode`) and that the HTML carries no inline code (`expectNoInlineCode`: no script without `src` or with code (a JSON-LD data block must be JSON with no `<` or `>`), no `<style>`, `style` or `on…` attribute, no `javascript:` address; attack text escaped into values doesn't count), and answers the HTML. `expectSiteHeaders` checks any other answer (files, redirects, refusals). Use them for every new built-in feature's output. `tests/harness/api-tokens.ts` is a script with an API token: `createApiToken(browser, input)` makes one as the browser's person (confirming their password when asked) and `tokenClient(install, secret)` calls `/api/v1` with it (`api`, typed like the admin's client, its answers plain JSON; `request(path, init)` for anything else). The tests of tokens, their limits and the OpenAPI document are in `tests/api/`. `tests/harness/fake-npm.ts` puts a fake package on the fake outgoing HTTP (`fakeNpmPackage(install, name, versions)`, real gzipped archives with the registry's integrity hashes; `publish` adds a version, `tampered` serves one that doesn't match its hash), for Library tests.

The Consent banner's tests are in `tests/consent/`: the setting, what pages hold back in each mode, the words as Content, `{{@consentSettings}}` and Consent records (a visitor's `POST /_wc/consent` is a standard `Request` with `fromAddress`). Tests about something else that render outside scripts or videos set their Site to `none`.

Every test Install reads the real countries file format from a small file of its own (`tests/harness/countries.ts`): `203.0.113.0/24` is in Ireland, `198.51.100.0/24` in Italy, `192.0.2.0/24` in France and `2001:db8::/32` in Germany; the test browsers' `10.x.x.x` addresses are in no country it knows. `tests/harness/analytics.ts` is a visitor's browser sending what the collection script sends (`analyticsVisitor(install, origin, { address, userAgent })`: `view(path)`, `send(event)`, `answer(choices)` on the Consent banner), `liveSite` puts a Site on a verified domain with Site analytics on (Staging addresses are left out), and `analyticsApi(browser)` calls the Module's procedures. Site analytics' tests are in `tests/analytics/`, the served collection script run in a stand-in page among them (`script.test.ts`).

`tests/harness/forms.ts` is a visitor's browser filling in Forms without JavaScript: `formVisitor(install)` loads a page (`open`), reads a Form's hidden inputs (`form(n)`, `formIn(html, n)`) and sends it as a browser posts it (`send`: the Site's `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, the page as `Referer`, its own client address; options leave out or forge what a test needs). The time trap needs time to pass: `await install.clock.advance(5000)` between loading and sending.

`tests/harness/trees.ts` writes element trees briefly: `tree(element("article", [element("h1", [value("title")])]))`, `text("…")`, and attributes as `element("a", [text("More")], { href: [{ type: "text", text: "/blog/" }, value("slug")] })`; comments and Conditions are written as their stored shapes, and `outlet()` is the Site template's `{{@outlet}}`, and `placement(componentId, { heading: "Hi" })` places a Component. `tests/sites/first-site.test.ts` shows a whole Site: Post Type, Entry template, Entry, publish, the rendered page and the page cache. Most Template tests write Template markup instead (`templates.saveEntryTemplateMarkup`), as `tests/sites/template-markup.test.ts` does; `tests/sites/loops.test.ts` and `tests/sites/archives.test.ts` show Loops, pagination and Archive templates on pages. Property-based tests use fast-check (`tests/markup/round-trip.test.ts`): generate inputs, drive them through the API, and check what comes back.

A seam-1 test:

```ts
import { afterAll, beforeAll, expect, test } from "bun:test";
import { samplePing } from "@webcraft/core";

import { createTestInstall, type TestInstall } from "../harness/index.ts";

let install: TestInstall;
beforeAll(async () => {
	install = await createTestInstall();
});
afterAll(async () => {
	await install.close();
});

test("the sample job emails the status of a URL", async () => {
	install.http.respond("GET", "https://status.example.org/ping", { status: 204 });

	await install.jobs.send(samplePing, { url: "https://status.example.org/ping", notify: "ops@agency.example" });
	const report = await install.drainJobs();

	expect(report.failed).toEqual([]);
	expect(install.mail.sent.map((email) => email.subject)).toEqual(["https://status.example.org/ping answered 204"]);
});
```

`tests/worker/sample-job.test.ts` shows the job drain, the fake outgoing HTTP and the clock; `tests/email/` shows the fake mailer and `install.sendSystemEmail`; `tests/web/health.test.ts` shows the database modes. Tests in one file share their Install, so give each test its own data (its own Workspace, its own email address) or create an Install per test when they must not see each other. `tests/worker/node-worker.test.ts` starts the real worker with `node`, the way to check something runs on Node; give it `install.env`. `tests/outgoing-http/pinned-address.test.ts` calls real servers on 127.0.0.1 and ::1 with both processes' outgoing HTTP, Bun's in the test and Node's in a `node` process (`node-calls.ts`, one JSON call per line); its HTTPS servers use certificates from `createTestCertificateAuthority()` (`tests/harness/certificates.ts`), which issues certificates for made-up names and addresses, trusted only by a client given its `cert` as `ca`. It also sends a System email with the real Mailer to `startSmtpServer()` (`tests/harness/smtp-server.ts`), a real SMTP server in the test process, which the browser tests use too (the production web process sends test emails with the real Mailer). Start a real `bun` process (the CLI, the web entry) in an empty directory when a test leaves a setting out on purpose: Bun fills missing variables from the `.env` in the directory it starts in (see `tests/secrets/start-refusal.test.ts`).

**Workspace isolation**: every procedure that takes a `workspaceId` or `siteId` gets one line in `tests/isolation/procedures.test.ts`, proving it works on the caller's own Workspace and refuses another's (404 or 403). The last test there fails when a procedure is missing:

```ts
isolation.refuses("sites.list", (api, { workspaceId }) => api.sites.list({ workspaceId }), { as: ["staff", "editor"] });
```

`as` names who calls it (Staff assigned to that one Workspace by default, or a Member of it). Each public procedure's line also runs through `/api/v1` with a token the Superadmin made for that one Workspace (refused there for a Sensitive procedure), so a token never reaches another Workspace even when its person does. The helper is `workspaceIsolation` in `tests/harness/isolation.ts`.

**Seam 2** is a real browser, for accessibility (and later the editors). `tests/browser/harness.ts` starts a test Install served by the production web process (`bun run build` first; started through `tests/browser/web.ts`, which hands it the test suite's Modules) on `http://admin.localhost:<port>`, with Sites' Staging addresses on `http://<site slug>.sites.localhost:<port>` (`browserInstall.siteUrl(slug, path)`, for Edit mode and the Staging address's own checks) and their Primary domains on `http://<site slug>.live.localhost:<port>` (`browserInstall.goLive(siteId)`, then `liveUrl(slug, path)`, for what visitors see), and Chromium through Playwright; `tests/browser/checks.ts` holds the checks; files are named `*.browser.ts` so `bun test` leaves them out, and `bun run test:browser` runs them. Every check runs with the device set to light and to dark (the admin follows the device).

- `expectAccessibleScreen(page, where)`: axe-core finds no violation of WCAG 2.0, 2.1 or 2.2 at A or AA, nor of its best practices; the first Tab reaches "Skip to main content" and following it moves focus into the `<main>` it points at; Tab visits every link, button and field in page order (a date or time box once, though the browser stops on each of its parts), each with a visible focus indicator; no positive `tabindex`; Tab always moves on (no traps); the page logged no errors.
- `expectOverlayUsable(page, trigger, "menu" | "dialog", where)`: opens a menu or dialog from the keyboard, checks it with axe, and Escape closes it with focus back on the trigger.

`browserInstall.signIn(page, account)` signs in through the sign-in page the first time for an account and hands later pages the same session: the web process limits auth requests per client address, and every page comes from this machine. `browserInstall.signInWithOldIdentityCheck(page, account, minutesAgo)` signs a page in with a session whose identity check is old, so the next Sensitive action opens "Confirm it's you" (the web process runs on the real clock, so the test Install makes that session with its own clock set back). Real processes started by tests (the CLI, the web process here) do the real leaked-password lookup, or accept the password when offline, so their test passwords must not be on any leaked list.

`startBrowserInstall()` also gives the test Install behind the web process (`browserInstall.install`), to prepare data through the Management API, send System emails and drain jobs before the page opens (see `tests/browser/email.browser.ts`).

**Adding a screen** is one line in `tests/browser/screens.ts`: its name, its path and who looks at it (`superadmin`, `staff`, `owner` or `signed-out`). A screen inside a Workspace or Site gives its path as a function of the shared fixture (`({ siteId }) => `/sites/${siteId}``); prepare more data in `admin-screens.browser.ts` through `install.install`, the same Install in the test process. For a state a path can't reach (a wizard step, an error shown, an open menu), write a test like `tests/browser/setup-wizard.browser.ts`: drive the page there with Playwright, then call `expectAccessibleScreen` or `expectOverlayUsable`. Don't retest behaviour seam 1 covers.

**The visual builder** is checked in `tests/browser/builder.browser.ts`: its top bar, Layers panel, style panel, insert menu and Template markup view in light and dark, every Layers panel move from the keyboard (checked by what is saved), the insert menu, a Media image, the canvas, the style panel, "Make it a Field" and Publish. The canvas shows Staff's own Template (Checks look at it) and runs no script, so the checks leave it out (`expectAccessibleScreen(page, where, { exclude })`; axe can't look inside a frame without scripts). Playwright's mouse runs a script in every frame to watch for native drags, so a drag on the canvas is tested as the pointer events a drag makes, sent to the canvas page.

**Code mode** is checked in `tests/browser/code-mode.browser.ts`: the panels in light and dark, choosing a suggestion and moving between the panels from the keyboard, the preview while typing (markup and CSS, under the strict policy), Format, selecting an element, Run and the console, and the conflict prompt. **Edit mode** is checked in `tests/browser/edit-mode.browser.ts`: the page with the editing bar, its menus, editing text and rich text from the keyboard, and focus never under the bar. The checks see inside open shadow roots (the bar draws itself in one), count text edited in place as a tab stop, and ignore the inline-style reports axe itself causes on a page under a strict CSP.

**Dashboards** are checked in `tests/browser/dashboards.browser.ts`: a Dashboard with every shape of Widget and a chart shown as a table, the Widget picker, a Widget's menu and editor, moving and resizing a Widget from the keyboard (checked by what is saved, and said in the live region), a Workspace Dashboard, Site › Goals with its Goal editor and Site › Funnels with its Funnel editor (a step moved from the keyboard), in light and dark. The web process counts by the real clock, so the test sends its visits at the real time. Their lists are lines of `screens.ts`. Seam-1 tests are in `tests/dashboards/` and `tests/analytics/` (`goals.test.ts`, `funnels.test.ts`).

**Site › Redirects** is checked in `tests/browser/redirects.browser.ts`: the rules (one added when a path changed), the 404 log and its ignored paths, the rule dialog and a rule's menu from the keyboard, Add redirect from the log starting at the target, and a refused target said beside its field, in light and dark.

**Forms** are checked in `tests/browser/forms.browser.ts`: a Form on a Site's page, the same page sent back with errors (the error summary takes focus, its links move focus to their fields) and the message once sent, in light and dark; the Forms list and each tab of the Form builder; a Form's Submissions tab (a Submission open, the Spam folder) and Emails tab as an Owner sees them, and Site › Email (SMTP), in light and dark; building a Form, reading Submissions (opening one, marking it unread, the delete dialog) and routing a Notification from the keyboard. Their seam-1 tests are in `tests/forms/` (`emails.test.ts`, `notifications.test.ts`, `submissions.test.ts`) and `tests/email/site-smtp.test.ts`.

**Imports** are checked in `tests/browser/imports.browser.ts`: the Import list, the steps of a new Import (Source, Map and Preview from the keyboard), a run in progress, an Import report with its tabs and the Undo dialog, and the CSV side sheet an Owner opens from a Post Type's Entries. **The WordPress import** is checked in `tests/browser/wordpress-import.browser.ts`: the Source step with the export file uploaded in parts and surveyed, Map variant B with the live sample Entry and Preview from the keyboard, and a run's report with its leftovers and Redirect rules (the web process queues on the real clock, so the test catches the test Install's clock up before it drains). Their seam-1 tests are in `tests/imports/` (`support.ts` uploads a CSV file, starts a run and drains the worker; `wordpress.ts` uploads an export file in parts and runs a WordPress Import, with the made-up fixture `fixtures/old-mill-export.xml`; `wordpress-upload.test.ts`, `wordpress-run.test.ts` and `wordpress-again.test.ts`).

**The Media library** is checked in `tests/browser/media.browser.ts`: its dialogs (New folder, a folder's menu, Import from URL, Delete with where the file is used, Empty Trash, the Workspace's quota) from the keyboard, and setting a Focal point without a mouse; the Trash and a file's details are admin screens.

**SEO** is checked in `tests/browser/seo.browser.ts`: the Entry editor's SEO & sharing card with the SEO check's warnings, writing SEO fields from the keyboard, a Post Type's SEO tab (tokens, the Schema type, a refusal's summary taking focus) and Site › SEO, in light and dark.

**The Consent banner** is checked in `tests/browser/consent.browser.ts`: each layout in light and dark (axe and the keyboard, the dialog's focus kept inside, Cookie settings opening it again), answering it, a held-back video's placeholder, reduced motion, a page without JavaScript (`install.newPage(theme, { javaScript: false })`) and an outside consent service's answers.

**Site script safety** is checked in `tests/browser/site-scripts.browser.ts`: Site › Libraries and Site › Outside scripts with their menus and dialogs (a snippet's problems too), the "Edit on site" menu, and in Edit mode the notice a failing Site script shows, Safe mode opened from it and turned off again, and outside scripts' placeholders.

**Publish all** is checked in `tests/browser/publish-all.browser.ts`: the list for Staff and for an Owner (Structure shown, never theirs to tick), in light and dark, opened and closed from the keyboard, and an item unticked and the rest published without a mouse. **The Staging address**'s page for people it doesn't know (with the password form, and after a wrong password) is checked in `tests/browser/staging.browser.ts`, in light and dark, and giving the password from the keyboard.

**Site pages** are checked in `tests/browser/site-pages.browser.ts`: an Entry's page and the 404 page, inside a Site template with a header, navigation and footer, prepared through `install.install` and opened with `install.siteUrl`. Built-in Site output that later tickets add (Components, Forms, Popups, the Consent banner) gets its line there.

```ts
export const ADMIN_SCREENS: readonly Screen[] = [
	// ...
	{ name: "Site › Overview", path: ({ siteId }) => `/sites/${siteId}`, as: "staff" },
];
```

## Conventions

- **Words**: use `GLOSSARY.md` terms exactly, in code, tests, UI text and commits. British spelling in prose ("licence", "normalise").
- **TypeScript**: strict, with explicit `.ts`/`.tsx` extensions on relative imports and only erasable syntax (no `enum`, `namespace` or parameter properties). That lets Node run shared code and the worker without a build step.
- **Runtimes** (ADR 0006): `packages/*` and `apps/worker` use standard Web and Node APIs only; Biome rejects the `Bun` global and `bun:*` imports there, and their tsconfigs don't load Bun's types. `apps/server`, `apps/cli`, `tests` and `scripts` may use Bun.
- **Edges go through ports**: time through `Clock`, email through `Mailer` (only the email sender calls it; features send System emails with `services.email.send`), calls to other servers through `services.http`. Tests replace them with fakes; nothing else is faked.
- **Secrets** go through `services.secrets` only: never in another table, a log line, a job payload or a response (see "Secrets and the Master key").
- **Workspace data** goes through the data layer (`services.data`), which runs `can()` first (ADR 0007); no query on tenant data without a Workspace, and no check of a role name: check a Permission.
- **Errors**: Site visitors and unknown hostnames see plain, unbranded answers. Unexpected errors are logged and answered with a plain 500.
- **Logs**: JSON lines through `Logger` (`services.log`), with fields rather than values pasted into the message.
- **Licences**: every workspace's `package.json` says `"license": "AGPL-3.0-or-later"`; new dependencies must pass `bun run check:licences`.
- **Commits**: Gitmoji + Conventional Commits with the ticket number, such as `✨ feat(auth): add setup wizard (#50)`.
- **Before you finish**: `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck` and `bun run test` all pass.

## Reference-material denylist

Names of third-party products that informed WebCraft must never appear in anything tracked (ADR 0013, `docs/reference-material.md`). `scripts/check-denylist.sh` enforces it: it reads one case-insensitive extended regular expression per line from `WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST` or from the file named by `WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE`, and checks tracked files, file names, the messages of the commits in `--range` and a `--branch` name. It prints only where a match is (file and line, commit, branch), never the list or the matching text.

```sh
WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE=~/.config/webcraft/reference-denylist.txt bun run check:denylist -- --range main..HEAD
```

To check every commit locally, turn on the opt-in hooks (staged files before a commit, then the commit message):

```sh
git config core.hooksPath .githooks
export WEBCRAFT_DENYLIST_FILE=~/.config/webcraft/reference-denylist.txt   # in your shell profile
```

Without a list the check is skipped locally with a warning and fails in CI.

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pull requests and on pushes to `main`:

- **Lint, typecheck, build and licences**.
- **Tests** against a Postgres 18 service, with the current Node LTS installed for the worker test.
- **Accessibility (browser)**: builds the admin, installs Playwright's Chromium and runs `bun run test:browser`.
- **Deploy files (Compose smoke test)**: `bun run test:deploy`.

`.github/workflows/images.yml` publishes the web and worker images to the GitHub Container Registry for amd64 and arm64 (each built on a runner of its own architecture, since Bun can't run the admin's build under QEMU emulation; pushed by digest, then joined into one multi-platform tag) on pushes to `main` (`main`, `sha-<commit>`) and on release tags `v1.2.3` (`1.2.3`, `1.2`, `1`, `latest`), with OCI labels and the AGPL licence. Check workflow changes with `actionlint`.
- **Reference-material denylist** over tracked files, the commit messages being added and the branch name, with the list from the `REFERENCE_DENYLIST` repository secret. It fails when the secret is missing, as it is for pull requests from forks.
